# re8 console — design history

**Companion to `re8-console-spec.md` — accompanies specification version 0.23.1 (2026-07-30).** This document holds the *rationale and resolution history* of the re8 design: architectural decisions, rejected alternatives, and the adversarial-review registers that drove the specification to its current state.

**Why it is separate (SOL-H2/D121).** The decision log and gap registers grew to outweigh the normative text, and because they necessarily restate *superseded* values they made contradictions hard to see — several review findings were conflicts between a "resolved" register entry and the section it claimed to resolve. The split enforces one rule:

> **`re8-console-spec.md` states only *current* values. Superseded values, alternatives and the reasoning behind them live here.**

Nothing in this document is normative. Where the two disagree, **the specification wins** — and the disagreement is a defect to be reported.

**Contents**

| Section | Content |
|---|---|
| 1 | Architectural decision log (D1–D125) |
| 2 | Gaps & inconsistencies register — internal review (G1–G53) and second adversarial review (SOL-A1…SOL-H5) |
| 3 | Source provenance |

---

## 1. Architectural decision log

**Feature set frozen 2026-07** — the three post-gap features (D33 text overlay, D34 PS/2 keyboard, D35 PS/2 mouse + hardware cursor) close the feature set; no further features are planned before the emulator-planning stage.

| # | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | W65C02S @3.3V, 7.159MHz (master÷3 — D20) | 65C816 (16-bit); eZ80; ColdFire; NOS CPUs | one of two in-production classic-bus CPUs (the 816 being the other); 3.3V kills level shifters; static core enables the debug story |
| D2 | Single custom ASIC (oito) for VDP+APU+mapper | separate sound MCU (ATtiny); VS1053B-class PCM chip; discrete 74-series (GameTank-style) | one chip simplifies BOM; audio adds no pins (PWM+I²S) and only small on-die RAM; differentiation lives in silicon |
| D3 | Hybrid tile renderer + mini-blitter | pure tile engine (rigid); pure framebuffer/blitter (76.8–153.6KB buffers, huge bus) | tile efficiency + blitter flexibility (destructible terrain, procedural gfx, sprite injection) |
| D4 | 100% digital ASIC; analog on motherboard | on-chip composite modulator; on-chip DACs | first-time-right silicon; analog timing unverifiable cheaply at low volume |
| D5 | AD725 for composite/S-Video | passive Y/C mixing from internal YUV | AD725 in production, proven, outputs both formats |
| D6 | CH7035B scaler → DVI-D @1080p60 | ASIC-native TMDS; RP2350/small-FPGA scaler (480p/720p cap); TFP410/ADV7513 raw serializer; DisplayPort; analog-only | integrated framebuffer+scaler+TX for a few dollars; nearest-neighbor for pixel art; beats TV 240p rejection & 30–80ms TV lag |
| D7 | HDMI connector, DVI-D signal, no license (refined by D30) | licensed HDMI ($5K/yr + $1.05/unit + $10–20K testing) | royalty-free TMDS; MiSTer precedent |
| D8 | CH7035B self-boot from I²C ROM (Strategy A) — Strategy B dropped (D31) | CPU-driven init (Strategy B) | video as appliance: alive before CPU boots, survives CPU crash, works in factory test |
| D9 | Genesis-compatible DB9 controllers | custom controller standard | ergonomic, still manufactured, huge installed base |
| D10 | 74LVC244A translators per port; controller data read into oito | 74HC244 (wrong — not 5V-tolerant at 3.3V); wiring pads into oito unprotected | LVC244A inputs are 5V-tolerant at a 3.3V rail; oito reads/latches pad state so the joypad register + change-IRQ live in silicon (§6.3, §9) |
| D11 | Parallel NOR flash cartridges + **console-side fixed-plus-switchable 16KB mapper** ("MMC3-style" corrected by SOL-F8/D94) | SPI/QSPI flash (no XIP on 6502); SD card; RAM loading | **execute-in-place without a preload step** (the named NOR has 70 ns access — SOL-H5); dumb cheap carts; banking in oito |
| D12 | prog8 as SDK language | cc65; LLVM-MOS C; TS-subset-only; MicroPython/JS (interpreter ≫ 16KB) | static memory model fits 16KB; near-asm codegen; retargetable without forking |
| D13 | ~~Keep TS subset as adoption front-end (status open)~~ **superseded by D29** | — | — |
| D29 | **Drop the TypeScript SDK** — prog8 is the single normative SDK language (decision 2026-07) | keep TS subset as a second front-end; TS→prog8 transpiler; LLVM-MOS C path | one language keeps debugger/symbol-model/examples coherent; a second front-end is unjustified maintenance for a solo/boutique console; commercial-reach argument doesn't outweigh the toolchain cost |
| D30 | **Digital A/V emits DVI-compatible video only** (no TERC4/HDCP/CEC) + separate optical S/PDIF digital audio + analog line-out (decision 2026-07; **licensing language moderated by D85/SOL-E14**) | licensed HDMI with cable audio; MiSTer-style HDMI audio | emits no HDMI-only signalling, removing the old TERC4-vs-"no license" technical contradiction (G31); DVI-D and S/PDIF are commonly described as royalty-free. **The legal conclusion is not asserted here — it requires counsel (D85).** Scale-up path = become a low-volume adopter |
| D31 | **CH7035B fixed 1080p60, Strategy-A self-boot; **fixed output, no EDID dependency (SOL-E5)**, no CEC; no per-mode reconfig** (decision 2026-07) | Strategy B (CPU-driven runtime mode switching); shipping 480p/720p | fixed output needs no BIOS-ROM dependency; §6.7 shared 256/320 timing keeps input sync constant so the scaler never reprograms; custom-timing/nearest-neighbor acceptance is a board bring-up item, not emulator-blocking (G10) |
| D32 | **On-cart FRAM saves** (battery-free), oito-decoded via a new SAVE_CĒ line (36-pin connector); SD/digital titles save to `.sav` files (decision 2026-07) | battery-backed SRAM; console-side shared NVRAM; EEPROM-only | FRAM is nonvolatile + battery-free + high-endurance (no coin-cell save loss); keeps saves with the game (portable); oito-owned decode keeps carts logic-free; console NVRAM would need allocation logic and tie saves to one unit. Emulator persists a CRC32-keyed `.sav` image |
| D33 | **Text overlay layer** (§6.8): top-priority 1bpp char layer, 40-col 8×8 / 80-col 4×8, 2-bank font RAM + `font_sel`, transparent-bg + opaque-textbox attributes, whole-plane fine scroll; free glyphs via sprites (feature 2026-07) | 640px 8×8 80-col mode (needs a 2nd dot clock); 2000-entry character-OAM for arbitrary per-glyph placement | reuses tile-plane + BG-scroll machinery; no pins / no external parts / no video-timing change; grid ≈ one BG plane of bandwidth; a char-OAM would duplicate the sprite engine at ~16× OAM for a rare need — covered by grid + fine scroll + sprite-glyphs |
| D34 | **PS/2 keyboard via the controller ports** (§9.1): bidirectional PS/2 host in oito on a port's data pins, passive DB9↔PS/2 adaptor, built-in key→joypad injection, `re8.keyboard` SDK + region layout tables (feature 2026-07; host made bidirectional by D35) | dedicated PS/2 connector; active-MCU adaptor; receive-only host | reuses the port + DB9 5V/GND → no new pins/connector; key→joypad map makes every joystick game keyboard-playable for free; bidirectional host (shared with the mouse) also gives keyboard LEDs + clean device auto-detect. Cost: 2 pull-ups + open-drain drive + 5V budget |
| D35 | **PS/2 mouse + fully hardware-rendered cursor + hardware hit-test** (§9.2): oito accumulates deltas → scaled/clamped position → top-most cursor from VRAM tiles + hotspot (1 tile 8×8 / 4 tiles 16×16 — corrected by SOL-B9/D111); game gets move/click/wheel events only; **pick registers report the sprite/BG-cell/tile under the pointer**; mouse-first single-source pointer arbitration; upgrades §9.1 host to **bidirectional** (mouse enable cmd); IntelliMouse wheel, cursor bounds, D-pad/keyboard-driven system pointer, device auto-detect, `re8.mouse` SDK (feature 2026-07) | cursor as a normal sprite (counts against budget); software cursor rendering; CPU-side pixel→cell + bounding-box hit-testing; receive-only PS/2 (can't enable a mouse) | cursor as an independent top layer costs one tile (8×8) or four (16×16 — SOL-B9) and no sprite slot; hardware position/scale/clamp means the game only sees events; **hit-test taps the existing compositor (one comparator + latches) and turns a per-click CPU loop into a register read**; bidirectional is mandatory for the mouse and free-riders keyboard LEDs. Joystick-safety: open-drain drivers released unless a port is positively identified as PS/2 (never contends a controller) |
| D125 | **Post-review verification fixes**: sweep divider made integer (÷200 = 239.70 Hz, not ÷199.75); RAM figure corrected 14.7 → **14.4 KB** (14,704 B); `IRQ_STATUS` declared **edge-latched** (only `STATUS`.0–1 and `INPUT_STATUS`.2 are level); SOL-E4's single-container proposal withdrawn in favour of retaining both dot clocks with `DE` (verification pass 2026-07-26) | shipping the four errors found | ÷199.75 is not realisable in hardware; 14,704 B is 14.36 KB (the 14.7 figure divided by 1000); a level-derived VBLANK bit would re-set the instant it was acknowledged and storm; and the single 426-dot container would have pillarboxed **analog** output too, since the R-2R ladder shares the RGB bus — a product regression the "HDMI-only cost" wording concealed |
| D124 | **Final stale-phrase sweep**: "zero-latency execute-in-place" → "execute-in-place without a preload step"; "digital fan-out costs almost zero current" deleted; reset-state exception list corrected to the one genuinely non-zero default (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-H5) | keeping phrases already contradicted elsewhere in the document | three of the review's six had been fixed in passing (I²S-to-CH7035B by D83, STATUS.3 by D110, the accuracy-goal conflict by D119). Of the rest: the named NOR has 70 ns access so "zero-latency" was false, and the real advantage is needing no copy-to-RAM; the fan-out claim contradicted §7's own analog-buffer requirement (SOL-E9 specifies three buffer paths precisely because one source cannot drive five outputs); and listing `CURSOR_CTRL` source = 0 as a reset "exception" was meaningless since 0 is the general reset value |
| D123 | **Standing rule: every performance figure MUST carry a workload or the label "estimate"; cross-machine comparisons removed from the spec; PCM cost restated with its workload (≈2.7–6%)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-H4) | quoting bare numbers ("3–6%", "1,187 tiles/frame", "negligible", "a few cycles") and cross-ISA comparisons | most of these were already fixed by SOL-E15/B17/H3; what remained was the durable part — a rule that stops the pattern returning. Comparisons to Genesis/Amiga/Lynx are **deleted rather than qualified**: a technical specification has no business comparing itself to hardware it has never been benchmarked against (they survive only in the G28 page punch-list). The CPU MIPS figure is kept but labelled as arithmetic from cycle counts |
| D122 | **RFC 2119 keyword convention adopted (MUST/MUST NOT/SHOULD/MAY for requirements only); flagged intensifiers replaced with measurable limits; systematic conversion deferred to the SOL-H2 split** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-H3) | mixing requirements and commentary in one voice; converting the whole 376 KB document immediately | a reader could not tell what was binding, and the review named the tells exactly — all of which appeared here repeatedly. The bounded, high-value part (convention note + replacing "comfortable", "essentially for free", "zero cost", "harmless", "every display", "a few cycles" with numbers) is done now; a full keyword pass is deferred to the split, when every normative statement is being relocated and can be classified as it moves — avoiding churn on text about to change file |
| D121 | **Document split accepted and specified: normative `re8-console-spec.md` + `re8-design-history.md` (§16/§17/superseded narratives), executed after the SOL review concludes, sequenced with the §J closure package** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-H2) | keeping decisions, gaps and normative text interleaved; performing the split immediately | §16+§17 now outweigh the normative content and restate superseded values, which demonstrably hides contradictions — several SOL findings were conflicts between a "resolved" gap entry and the section it resolved. But restructuring is mechanical, substantial work, and doing it in the closing moments of an 80-item campaign is precisely how new inconsistencies appear; so it is specified with a standing rule (normative text states only *current* values) and scheduled as its own pass |
| D120 | **Status restated as "architecture draft" with an explicit outstanding-work table and the principle that a disposition log records decisions made, not decisions correct** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-H1) | "all 53 register entries dispositioned / sufficient to plan and build / campaign complete" | the review's headline judgement, accepted at the outset: the claim was withdrawn before SOL-A1 was started. The campaign then proved the point — a large share of SOL findings were defects in text previously marked resolved, several introduced by earlier remediation. The final wording is honest in both directions: it does not pretend the spec is unfinished, nor that the qualification and validation dependencies do not exist |
| D119 | **Two named emulator accuracy tiers defined; Tier 1 (compatibility) is the conformance target, Tier 2 (cycle-exact) optional** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B18) | "non-cycle-perfect" in the front matter alongside "cycle-accurate" in §14.5 | the two statements have contradicted each other since the document's first line and survived every revision. Tiers were already being used informally — SOL-G9 invented "Tier 1/Tier 2" for input timing with nothing to reference — so defining them closes the contradiction *and* gives the accumulated timing decisions (per-pixel palette, line-224 snapshot, pick-pixel sampling, ~2.5 ms input quantisation) one place to live. Golden ROMs validate Tier 1 |
| D118 | **Blitter throughput restated as guaranteed minimums under a defined benchmark (≥1,000 interleaved / ≥350 blanking-only); ~1,187/~406 retired** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B17) | quoting ~1,187/~406 as though specified | those numbers came from an unstated calculation — no copy mode, tile size, arbitration assumption or concurrent load — and SOL-A8 has since changed the blitter itself, so they describe a machine that no longer exists. A stated workload plus a floor is checkable; everything outside it is labelled an estimate. Same move as SOL-E15: replace a confident number with a specification plus its measurement condition |
| D117 | **Hit-test sampling timing pinned**: sample taken as the raster reaches the pick point; pick coordinates latched at line 0 so mid-frame writes apply next frame; `HIT_STATUS`.5 set on sample and cleared each frame; results readable through VBLANK (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B16) | "once per frame" without a named event | the sampling instant determines which scroll and OAM state the result reflects, so two implementations would otherwise report different tiles under the same pointer. Sampling at the pixel comes free from tapping the compositor; latching coordinates at frame start prevents a mid-frame `PICK_X` write from requesting a point the beam already passed. Deliberately the same idiom as SOL-B7's shadow-OAM timing |
| D116 | **Keyboard FIFO/map rules pinned**: 16-byte FIFO dropping on overflow with a W1C sticky flag; `KBD_MAP_IDX` bit 7 = `E0` extended prefix giving 256 slots; `KBD_MAP_VAL` bit 4 targets `_EXT`; lower port wins with two keyboards; hot-unplug flushes and clears injected bits (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B15) | a one-byte map index; unstated depth, overflow-clear and unplug behaviour | the index was a genuine design flaw, not just a missing number: without an extended flag, `E0 4B` (right arrow) collides with `4B` — and arrow keys are exactly what the D34 injection exists to map. Dropping rather than overwriting preserves partially-consumed sequences; clearing injected bits on unplug prevents a stuck button |
| D115 | **IRQ semantics specified as a state machine**: `IRQ̄ = NOT(any(STATUS & ENABLE))` combinational, masked events still record, unmasking a pending event asserts immediately, same-cycle set beats W1C, all bits level-derived, `INPUT_STATUS`.2 re-latches while the FIFO is non-empty, raster-compare fires at line start with no retroactive firing (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B14) | "oito ORs enabled+pending sources onto IRQ̄" | that phrasing left seven software-observable behaviours undefined. Recording masked events is what makes a source pollable while its interrupt is off (the basis of D44); set-beats-clear avoids silently losing an event to a coincident acknowledgement, at the cost of at most one spurious re-entry; level-derived FIFO status forces handlers to *drain* rather than merely acknowledge, which is the correct contract |
| D114 | **`FONT_ADDR_HI` bit 7 selects the font *write* bank, independent of the display bank; bank-0 writes permitted only while the boot overlay is active** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B12) | a port hard-wired to the cart bank (G45) | firmware is required to load the region charset into **bank 0**, which the port could not address at all — the feature was impossible as written. Separating write-target from display-target is independently useful (rewrite the cart font while the default is shown). Gating bank-0 writes on the boot overlay is what actually enforces G45's promise that the default font survives for system text, and it reuses an existing one-way state rather than adding a mechanism |
| D113 | **Text char-map given a fixed 80-cell (160-byte) row stride in all modes; 4,480 B region; no wrapping; unsigned scroll shifting up/left; off-screen cells not fetched** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B11) | "always cols × 28" (D40) with a per-mode stride | `cols` varies 32/40/64/80, so the stride was undefined and a live mode switch would reinterpret the same bytes as a different grid, scrambling the display. A fixed stride means a mode change alters only how many cells are *shown*, never where a cell lives — (col 5, row 3) keeps its address in all four configurations — for at most 4,480 B of 128 KB, partly unused in narrow modes |
| D112 | **Cursor arithmetic fully specified**: 4.4 fixed-point scale (0 ⇒ 1.0) with retained fractions, single-threshold ×2 acceleration, truncate-toward-zero and saturate, Y inversion and overflow-bit discard, D-pad ramp 1→4 px, MOUSE_STATUS self-clearing, X_LO-latched coordinate atomicity, ±127 wheel saturation (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B10) | leaving scale format, acceleration, rounding, clearing and atomicity to implementers | cursor feel is immediately noticeable to players, so divergence here is user-visible rather than academic. Retained fractional remainders are what make slow, fine positioning work; treating scale 0 as 1.0 avoids a frozen-pointer failure; latching on X_LO prevents a mid-read packet from tearing the coordinate pair |
| D111 | **`CURSOR_TILE` is four consecutive row-major tiles in 16×16 mode (4-aligned, low 2 index bits ignored); "one tile" corrected everywhere; no cursor flip** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B9) | claiming the cursor costs "one VRAM tile" while offering a 16×16 size | plain arithmetic: a 16×16 4bpp image is **128 bytes** and a tile is **32** — the two claims could not both hold, and a cursor rendering 32 bytes of a 128-byte image would be a very visible bug. Row-major quad ordering reuses the multi-tile sprite convention of §6.6 so the system has one layout rule; ignoring the low index bits keeps the fetch trivial; flip is explicitly not provided since a pointer never needs it |
| D110 | **`STATUS.3` sprite-overflow latched through VBLANK — cleared at line 0 or by write-1** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B8) | clearing at VBLANK start | the flag reported "this frame" yet was erased at the moment the frame became inspectable, so it could only be read by polling during active display — defeating its purpose. This is not cosmetic: per-frame overflow detection is how a game implements **sprite multiplexing** (rotating OAM priority so excess sprites flicker rather than vanish), a standard technique the original semantics would have made unusable. W1C also matches the IRQ_STATUS/INPUT_STATUS idiom and lets a game measure sub-frame regions |
| D109 | **Shadow-OAM snapshot pinned to the start of line 224; DMA effects apply from the cycle the burst completes; mid-frame DMA tearing documented** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B7) | "once per frame during VBLANK" and "DMA takes effect on the current frame" (D41) | neither identified *when*, and they contradict each other for a DMA issued during active display — where "current frame" holds only for lines not yet drawn. Naming line 224 as the snapshot event makes both the next-frame rule for VRAM-port writes and the immediate-effect rule for DMA exact, and turns mid-frame tearing into documented behaviour rather than a surprise. Both are schedulable events in the existing scanline loop |
| D108 | **`OAM_DMA_OFS` ($4065) added; `OAM_DMA_LEN` semantics pinned (0 = all 128, 129–255 clamp, OFS+LEN wraps within OAM)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B6) | a length register alone (D39) | D39 claimed a game moving six sprites pays ~32 cycles instead of 683, but with only a length the DMA can refresh a **prefix** and nothing else — a game whose moved sprites sit at indices 40, 41 and 90 gained nothing. One offset driving both source and destination keeps the shadow buffer and OAM in lockstep without a second register, and makes D39's cost claim true rather than aspirational. Clamping (not wrapping) matches the saturating choice in D99 |
| D107 | **`OAM_DMA_SRC` re-encoded as a 1KB block number (bits 0–3, address = block << 10)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B5) | the D39 wording "supplies A15:A10" alongside the formula `(value & $3C) << 8` — which describe different things; documenting the sparse legal set $00,$04,…,$3C | the text and arithmetic contradicted each other, and the shifted form forced callers to pre-shift *and* remember which bits were live, inviting bugs for no benefit. A block number is one shift from an address (`addr >> 10`), and all 16 values are valid since 16KB is exactly sixteen 1KB blocks, so there is no illegal encoding to define. Nothing has been built against the old form |
| D106 | **Y scroll corrected from 9-bit to 8-bit; `$4013`/`$4019` become reserved** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B4) | keeping a 9-bit Y scroll; repurposing the bit as a plane-height selector or 512px-tall map | the name-table is **256 px tall**, which 8 bits addresses exactly — bit 8 had nothing to point at. (X genuinely needs 9 bits for the 512 px width.) A 512-px-tall plane was considered and rejected: it would double BG name-table VRAM from 4 KB to 8 KB per plane and raise fetch bandwidth for a capability nothing has requested. Leaving the bit reserved keeps that door open at zero cost |
| D105 | **All VRAM address arithmetic wraps modulo 128 KB — one uniform rule** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B3) | clamping; an "invalid tile" behaviour or error flag; leaving overflow undefined | a 12-bit index alone spans the entire 128 KB, so any non-zero pattern base can carry a high index past 17 bits — this is routine, not exotic. Wrapping is literally what the hardware does (the address bus is 17 bits; the high bits do not exist), so it is free and deterministic, whereas clamping needs comparators that buy nothing. Consolidates what SOL-A8, SOL-B1 and SOL-A12 had each stated separately |
| D104 | **Palette port semantics pinned**: 6-bit masked index, increment only on HI *write* (wrapping 63→0), reads never increment, LO/HI independently visible, and **writes take effect at the next composited pixel** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B2) | leaving increment/wrap/read behaviour and effect timing undefined | the effect-timing question is player-visible, not merely an implementation detail: per-pixel palette lookup is what makes **mid-scanline palette swaps** work, the classic way to exceed 64 on-screen colours. It also imposes a real constraint on emulators — palette state must be sampled per pixel, since caching it per line silently breaks those effects |
| D103 | **VRAM port semantics pinned**: all eight stride codes (1/2/4/8/32/64/128/256), single post-increment on every read *and* write, **no read buffer**, 17-bit wrap, address latched at access start, independent per-access arbitration (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-B1) | "stride select (1/2/32/64/…)" and unstated read/increment behaviour | this is the CPU's only path to VRAM, so an ellipsis where a specification belongs guarantees divergent implementations. Strides are chosen to match real structures (cell, tile row, OAM entry, tile, name-table row). The no-read-buffer rule is called out because NES-style ports *do* buffer, so an implementer with that background would otherwise get it wrong; latching the address at access start makes mid-stall pointer writes harmless |
| D102 | **Emulator input becomes a timestamped `(cycle, device, state)` event queue with a modelled ≈2.5 ms acquisition cadence and two accuracy tiers** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G9) | "present pad state directly in the registers each frame" | after D66 corrected acquisition to ≈400 Hz, a frame-granular model cannot reproduce sub-frame transitions, mid-frame change-IRQ timing, or a press-and-release inside one frame — and games will rely on the change IRQ, so its timing must match hardware. Modelling the cadence rather than the TH protocol keeps oito's sequencing internal while making the observable timing right, consistent with how raster and blitter events are already scheduled |
| D101 | **Debug-info pipeline marked pending validation; breakpoint/symbol identity is (bank, address); proof fixtures in CI per host; map-file-over-DAP fallback** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G8) | describing the synthesized-ELF trick as if verified; address-only breakpoint identity | per-bank compilation makes `$8100` exist in all 64 banks, so an address-keyed breakpoint fires everywhere and a DWARF line table cannot express which bank is meant. The probe can shadow `BANK_SELECT` by sniffing $4080 writes it already sees, so bank-qualification costs no signal. Carrying the plan is acceptable *because* the fallback — serving line info from our own map file over DAP — is entirely under our control |
| D100 | **`@irqsafe` subset defined and build-time enforced; handlers set flags, main loop acts; nested IRQs forbidden; dispatcher clobber list published** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G7) | relying on "event-driven" callbacks without a reentrancy rule | this is the root cause behind SOL-A5, G4 and G6: prog8 parameters live in fixed cells, so a callback entering a routine the main program is already inside overwrites its parameters mid-call. Since callbacks are the SDK's headline feature, the safety property had to be stated and checkable — `@irqsafe` is verifiable by walking the handler's call tree, the same analysis dead-code elimination already performs |
| D99 | **IRQ wrapper saves/restores `BANK_SELECT`; bank-stack updates made SEI/CLI-atomic and saturating; `far_call` forbidden in IRQs and with the save window open** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G6) | leaving bank state unprotected across interrupts; letting the 8-deep stack wrap silently | three parties now touch `BANK_SELECT` and only oito's PCM steal restored it. An IRQ that switches banks silently corrupts a main-line read — the classic weeks-to-find bug — so the wrapper makes handlers safe by construction. Saturating with a flag beats wrapping, which would corrupt bank state invisibly. The save/restore is deliberately a safety net, not permission: far calls in IRQs still hit prog8's non-reentrant static cells |
| D98 | **System-RAM budget published and §15.1 corrected 15.5 KB → ≈14.4 KB; SDK regions declared so the linker fails on overlap; shadow-OAM kilobyte reclaimed when `re8.sprites` is unlinked** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G5) | a compile-time overflow promise with no enumerated budget | the check is only meaningful if every consumer is declared, and several were added after the promise was written — above all the **1 KB shadow OAM** made mandatory by D41, which is exactly the 0.8 KB the old "≈15.5 KB usable" figure was missing. Splitting compiler-owned from SDK-owned allocations lets the map fail on overlap and the allocation meter report the true remainder |
| D97 | **"No recursion" restated as "ordinary subroutines are non-reentrant; no automatic recursion, manual recursion possible"** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G4) | listing "No recursion" as a hard limit | it is not true as written — upstream documents manual recursion with programmer-managed state — and an incorrect entry in the *hard limits* list misleads in a specific direction: a developer porting a recursive algorithm would conclude it is impossible rather than manual. The practical bound is the **256-byte hardware stack**, not the language. Stating non-reentrancy precisely also makes the IRQ caveats of SOL-A5 and SOL-G7 coherent, since they stem from the same property |
| D96 | **Typed-symbol source resolved by selection, not assumption**: capture the pinned compiler's real `-dumpvars` output, then use it if typed / else emit an SDK-generated type table / else patch upstream; versioned schema; symbol identity is **(bank, address)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G3) | asserting the dependency was closed while §14.4 said it was unconfirmed | the review correctly identified an acknowledged open dependency wearing a "resolved" label. D27's model is sound; only its data source is unknown, and the fallback (SDK-emitted type table) is mechanical and under our control — so the honest close is a decision procedure plus fixtures, not a claim. Per-bank compilation makes address-only symbol identity ambiguous, which SOL-G8 shares |
| D95 | **`re8.properties` becomes a versioned SDK artefact referenced by the spec, paired with the pinned `prog8c` hash and validated by a CI build; the spec states only what the target must achieve** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-G2) | transcribing exact key spellings into the spec as normative | the document itself admitted the spellings were unverified, so the "normative command line" was illustrative text wearing specification's clothes. Only a CI compile against the pinned toolchain actually validates key names; pairing the file with the compiler hash catches the exact upstream-rename drift D28 exists to prevent |
| D94 | **"MMC3-style" renamed to "console-side fixed-plus-switchable 16KB mapper"** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F8) | keeping the MMC3 label | re8 implements none of what defines an MMC3 — no PRG modes, CHR banking, scanline IRQ or serial write protocol. The label implies capabilities we do not have and invites "where is the scanline IRQ?", which the VDP's `RASTER_CMP` answers differently. The honest lineage is UxROM-like; the genuinely distinctive property is that banking lives in the console (D11), which is why cartridges are one chip. Also added to the G28 page-refresh punch-list |
| D93 | **Cartridge write path gated by `CART_WE_ENABLE` (SAVE_CTRL bit 4, reset 0) plus a physical `WĒ` strap on retail boards; "read-only" wording corrected to "factory-programmed NOR"** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F7) | leaving the write path permanently live; calling an in-system-programmable NOR "read-only" | the design deliberately passes writes so smart carts work, which leaves a retail game sitting on writable flash — a wild pointer could walk a JEDEC unlock sequence. A reset-cleared register gate matches the two write-enables already in the design, and a footprint strap costs nothing on a per-SKU board. Because the gate is a register rather than a console mode, D22's one-connector property is untouched |
| D92 | **Save-file contract defined**: virgin FRAM contents *unspecified* (games must self-validate) while emulators zero a new `.sav`; fixed 16,384-byte size with pad/truncate; key `<title>_<crc32>` with CRC **recomputed from the image**; atomic temp-file+rename writes (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F6) | assuming a blank/erased pattern; keying on the header's CRC field; unspecified file size and write behaviour | FRAM has no erased state, so promising one would be false on real hardware — the honest rule is that a game validates its own save, with emulator zero-fill kept explicitly separate as a determinism choice. Recomputing the CRC prevents a wrong or malicious header value from mis-keying saves, and atomic writes matter because games save exactly when users quit |
| D91 | **Header checksum and ROM CRC32 defined exactly** — 16-bit little-endian byte sum over $00–$19/$1C–$3F excluding itself; standard reflected CRC-32 (0x04C11DB7, init/xor 0xFFFFFFFF) over the whole padded image with its own field zeroed; header-version accept-if-≤ rule (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F5) | leaving "header checksum" and "ROM CRC32" unspecified | both are **format ABI** — a packer and boot ROM that disagree reject valid cartridges. A plain sum keeps the boot stub trivial (it guards blankness/corruption, not tampering) while the standard zlib-variant CRC-32 means every existing tool computes it correctly, and covering the padded image makes the value a true identity for `.sav` keying (D32). Rejecting *newer* header versions rather than guessing keeps forward compatibility safe |
| D90 | **EEPROM removed; FRAM is the sole save device** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F4) | specifying an exact EEPROM part plus a separate SDK/mapper protocol with busy polling | two save protocols would tax every game, the SDK and the emulator — and because software can observe EEPROM's busy state, its timing would enter the *compatibility contract*, not just the BOM. EEPROM's ~10⁵ endurance also undercuts D32's battery-free-durability rationale (a 30 s autosave exhausts it in ~a month) for a BOM saving that is small beside shell, PCB, flash and assembly. Same instinct as dropping per-cell inverse (SOL-A10) and the render-mode bit (SOL-A8): remove options that add contract surface without earning it |
| D89 | **Save device named `FM18W08` with a pulsed-`SAVE_CĒ` access model and published timing margin** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F3) | treating the save chip as an always-selected SRAM (implied by D37) | a real parallel FRAM needs a **falling CE edge per operation** plus precharge, and its **130 ns cycle** exceeds its 70 ns access. Against a 139.7 ns CPU cycle this fits at one access per cycle with **9.7 ns margin** — small enough that the constraint is recorded, since a faster CPU (the rejected 125 ns option) would have required wait states. 32KB part with A14 tied low exposes the 16KB window |
| D88 | **System RAM and VRAM named as orderable parts** — `IS61C256AL` 32K×8 with A14 tied low (16KB decoded) and `IS61C1024AL` 128K×8, 10 ns, with a 55 ns substitute permitted (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F2) | "16KB/128KB async SRAM, 10 ns, 3.3V" as a BOM entry | a requirement is not a part: production status, package, pinout, current and timing were all uncheckable. 32KB parts are more available than 16KB, so over-provisioning and decoding only half is the pragmatic choice — and it keeps the 16KB limit a *design* limit, leaving §15.1 and `ram_end=3FFF` truthful. Timing margin is large enough that 55 ns parts qualify, widening supply |
| D87 | **Cartridge flash named at both capacities: `SST39VF040` (512KB, TSOP-32) and `SST39VF080` (1MB, TSOP-40)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-F1) | citing only a 512KB part while specifying a 1MB maximum; "Macronix" as a BOM entry | the single named part could not demonstrate the headline 1MB configuration, and 20 address lines require more than 32 pins. Keeping both in the SST39 family — already chosen for the boot flash — means one JEDEC program/erase implementation covers cartridge, boot flash and updater. 70 ns access fits both the CPU cycle and the PCM-steal budget; the TSOP-40 footprint is called out because the cartridge shell is fixed |
| D86 | **Refresh compatibility restated as a specification**: digital output exactly 1080p60, cadence quantified (~1 frame per ~1,100), analog rate quoted as 60.055 Hz ±0.01 % with a named validation matrix (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E15) | "every 60 Hz display tolerates it"; "CRTs lock fine"; "negligible cadence adjustment" | universal compatibility claims about untested hardware are unfalsifiable. The digital path actually deserves a *stronger* statement — the scaler re-times to exact 1080p60, so the sink never sees 60.055 Hz — while the analog path gets an honest tolerance plus a test matrix shared with the SOL-E7 AD725 validation |
| D85 | **All categorical HDMI/DVI/S-PDIF licensing claims withdrawn and replaced by engineering facts plus an explicit "requires counsel" caveat** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E14) | "license-clean", "needs no HDMI Adopter agreement **at any volume**", "royalty-free open standard", and characterising third-party boards as non-compliant | these are **legal determinations**, not engineering conclusions, and HDMI LA materials treat connectors and sources as licensed product categories rather than only trademarks/features — so the question is genuinely specialist. The verifiable facts (no TERC4/HDCP/CEC/InfoFrames, no HDMI marks) are retained; only the legal inference is withdrawn |
| D84 | **Analog audio output stage specified**: 2nd-order delta-sigma PWM at master÷8, 3rd-order active 20 kHz reconstruction filter, NJM4556-class line driver at 1 Vᵣₘₛ/≤100 Ω with separate series feeds to jack and SCART, DC blocking, mute-until-ready, short tolerance (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E13) | "2× PWM → passive RC → jack + SCART" | the same defect as SOL-E9 on the audio side — a passive RC gives the wrong output impedance, no DC blocking, no defined level, and lets two destinations load a shared node. Stating the PWM order and clock is also what makes the filter designable and completes the emulator's output model; mute-until-ready prevents power-cycle thumps |
| D83 | **I²S/S/PDIF format fully specified**: LRCLK = master÷448 = 47,940.3 Hz, BCLK = master÷7 = 64 fs, PLL-generated MCLK = 128 fs on its own pin, standard I²S 16-in-32 MSB-first, channel status declaring 48 kHz at "level II / variable" accuracy (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E12) | saying "I²S" without defining a single parameter; declaring "sample rate not indicated" | every clock divides from the master, so the chain is synchronous by construction and the transmitter needs a 128 fs reference (hence one added pin). S/PDIF has no code for 47,940 Hz; declaring 48 kHz with variable accuracy is honest and universally locked-to, whereas "not indicated" makes some equipment refuse to sync |
| D82 | **Digital-audio transmitter changed WM8805 → TI `DIT4192`; optical module specified as a current 3.3V TOSLINK part with defined drive** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E11) | WM8805 (Cirrus marks it end-of-life); an unspecified "TOSLINK module" | the project's founding constraint is that every part be orderable new (§1 philosophy #2) — citing an EOL part for the digital-audio path broke the one rule the design cannot bend. DIT4192 is active production with the same function; the optical module now names a real class of part with its drive requirements, which "TOSLINK module" did not |
| D81 | **"15 kHz VGA" renamed to "15 kHz analog RGBHV (DE-15)" and its signal specified** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E10) | keeping the "VGA" label with a compatibility caveat | VGA is 31.5 kHz; calling a 15.734 kHz output "VGA" sets exactly the wrong expectation for someone plugging in a PC monitor, which the spec itself says will fail ~95% of the time. Naming the connector rather than implying a standard is honest, and the signal (0.7 Vₚₚ/75Ω, active-low H/V, optional CSYNC) is now stated |
| D80 | **Analog video buffering brought into scope and specified**: one shared R-2R ladder node, 2× THS7374 (2× gain, 75Ω line drive) for SCART and VGA, a separate unity-gain buffer for the AD725's 0.714V AC-coupled input, and SCART pin-16/pin-8 drivers (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E9) | excluding analog buffers as "PCB-stage"; driving all five outputs from one unbuffered ladder | the scope rule declares *all active components* in scope, and buffers are active parts — the exclusion was self-contradictory and left the BOM unbuildable. One buffered node per destination is what makes "five simultaneous outputs" true rather than aspirational; the AD725 needs a different level and coupling from a 75Ω line driver, so it gets its own stage |
| D79 | **CSYNC generated in oito as `NOT(HSYNC XOR VSYNC)` with active-low convention; AD725 fed composite sync on one pin, its internal combination unused** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E8) | "XNOR/AND combination of H/V" (two mutually exclusive gates, and AND is wrong for active-low); letting the AD725 combine H/V internally | oito owns the raster and knows exactly where each sync edge belongs, so deriving CSYNC there is deterministic and identical for every consumer — and SCART pin 20 and VGA need CSYNC anyway, so one generator serves three outputs. Pairs with SOL-E7: with equalisation/serration omitted per the 240p convention, the XNOR yields exactly the waveform the AD725 validation must confirm |
| D78 | **AD725 reclassified as provisionally selected pending 240p bench validation; sync waveform made normative (negative polarity, no equalisation/serration); fallback = interlaced 480i on the composite path only** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E7) | assuming 240p works because CRTs are tolerant; leaving the sync waveform implied | the datasheet specifies **interlaced** RGB at TV rates, and NTSC's 4-field colour sequence assumes 525-line interlace — so subcarrier phase, burst insertion and vertical lock on a 262-line progressive frame are unproven. Capture devices and upscalers are far less tolerant than CRTs and are how most users will view this output. Risk is contained to composite/S-Video; RGB/VGA/HDMI are unaffected |
| D77 | **CH9904 restated as an 8KB I²C boot ROM at address `0x57`; substitution rule and versioned config artefact defined** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E6) | describing it as a "<1KB configuration device" | the "<1KB" figure described my estimate of the *register writes*, not the part — CH9904 is 8192×8. Pinning address `0x57` matters because a generic EEPROM substitute that does not match it silently fails; the spare capacity usefully holds the register sequences the SOL-E3 qualification will produce. Larger EEPROMs also take longer to become readable, reinforcing the RESETB-after-EEPROM-ready ordering of SOL-E2 |
| D76 | **Digital output made unconditional 1080p60 with no EDID/HPD dependency; connector +5V/DDC/HPD/ESD requirements added** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E5) | claiming the CH7035B autonomously reads EDID and confirms 1080p60 (D31) | the public brief describes HPD as an interrupt **to a processor** and EDID as read **through programmed registers** — both presuppose a host, and Strategy A gives the scaler none. Rather than substantiate a capability we cannot verify, the design stops needing it: 1080p60 is universally supported, so a fixed-function console has nothing to negotiate. Failure mode stated honestly (no digital picture on a non-1080p60 sink; use the analog outputs) |
| D75 | **oito outputs `DE`; both dot clocks retained; scaler must track two geometries from DE (SOL-E3 go/no-go)** — *the fixed 320-px pillarboxed container of the first draft was withdrawn on verification* (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E4) | relying on shared line/frame timing alone (D25's argument); proving the scaler auto-measures both modes | constant HSYNC/VSYNC frequency does not tell a scaler where the active pixels are — the modes differ in dot clock, total dots and active width, and no DE was output. Explicit DE makes the active window unambiguous in both modes. The single-container variant was withdrawn because the analog ladder shares the same RGB bus, so it would have pillarboxed CRT output too — a product regression. Accepted cost: +1 pin, and dual-geometry tracking becomes a qualification criterion on an already-provisional part |
| D74 | **CH7035B reclassified as *provisionally selected pending evaluation-board qualification*, with go/no-go criteria and a costed fallback (oito line-doubles to 480p)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E3) | treating custom-240p acceptance as a mere "bring-up detail" while the part is foundational; an undefined "worst case" fallback | public data starts at 640×480 and promises nothing about 256/320×224, nearest-neighbour scaling or the integer letterbox, so the dependency must be gated before schematic freeze. Per the review, a fallback that changes image quality must be a *selected design*: internal line-doubling to standard 480p costs a second ~25.175 MHz PLL output but preserves integer-scaled pixel art |
| D73 | **CH7035B `RESETB` added to the supervisor (≥20 ms post-power, ≥100 µs pulse, EEPROM stable first) and a dedicated 27.000 MHz reference crystal specified** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E2) | leaving the scaler unreset and unclocked; deriving its reference from the 21.477 MHz master | "self-boot" (D31) described how the part configures itself but not what it needs to *start* — it had neither a reset source nor a timebase. 27 MHz is conventional for this converter class and divides cleanly to 148.5 MHz for 1080p60; keeping it independent of the master is deliberate since the scaler frame-buffers and re-times anyway. The EEPROM-before-RESETB ordering prevents the scaler booting a blank config |
| D72 | **CH7035B rails corrected to the manufacturer's domains: 1.8V `DVDD` + filtered 1.8V `AVDD_PLL`, 3.3V `VDDH`/`AVDD`/`VDDMQ`/`VDDMS`/`VDDIO`; the ~1.2V LDO is deleted** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-E1) | the invented "~1.2V core" rail from D23 | Chrontel's reference design has **no mandatory 1.2V core** — 1.2–3.3V is selectable for `VDDIO` only, and with oito driving 3.3V RGB (D43) VDDIO is 3.3V. Meanwhile the genuinely required **1.8V** domains were missing. The tree loses an LDO and gains a 1.8V consumer: simpler *and* correct. Sharing oito's 1.8V rail is allowed only after noise/current/sequencing analysis, since the scaler's SDRAM interface is a switching load |
| D71 | **Controller-port power policy: 250 mA/port budget, 350 mA total, 500 mA polyfuse as the *trip* target; short-circuit, inrush and out-of-scope devices defined** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D7) | a flat "~500 mA per port" (set in G51 without analysis) | 2 × 500 mA at 5V is a **5 W** auxiliary load — larger than the console's entire ~3–5 W envelope — and would have driven the buck, the 9 V supply and the thermal design to power accessories nobody ships. Real draws are <10 mA (pads), 20–50 mA (mouse), 100–150 mA (keyboard). A polyfuse *holds* at rating and trips above it, so budget and trip point are different numbers |
| D70 | **PS/2 line circuit fully specified**: 4.7kΩ pull-up to 5V on the console PCB, 1kΩ series into the LVC receiver, **BSS138 gate-driven pull-down (drain on the 5V line, source GND, gate from oito)**, ≤10pF TVS, defined power-off state (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D6) | naming "BSS138-class or a bidirectional shifter" as if it were a circuit; the classic BSS138 bidirectional level-shifter topology | the safety question is entirely topology-dependent: in the gate-driven arrangement oito's pin never sees 5V (only the FET drain does), whereas the familiar bidirectional shifter ties both rails through the body diode and would give 5V a path toward the ASIC. Rise time ≈1µs against a ≥30µs half-period leaves ample margin |
| D69 | **PS/2 identification specified as a full state machine** — inhibit/request-to-send framing with odd parity, Reset ($FA then $AA) *distinct from* Identify ($F2 → $AB $83 / $00 / $03), wheel knock, enable, scancode-set-2, plus RESEND/parity/timeout/BAT-fail/hot-unplug handling that always releases the lines (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D5) | "issue Reset and read the device ID" | Reset and Identify are different operations returning different bytes, and none of the ACK/RESEND/inhibit/parity/timeout states existed — leaving the protocol unimplementable and error cases undefined. Making every failure path release the transmitters keeps a mis-declared port from ever contending with a joystick |
| D68 | **Explicit `PORT_MODE` ($407C) replaces circular PS/2 auto-detection; transmitters released only in PS/2 mode; receive-only `PORT_HINT` from an unsolicited BAT frame** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D4) | auto-probing by sending PS/2 Reset (requires driving the very lines the safety rule protects); keyed/active adaptor; analog line-state detection; a DB9 identification strap | the old invariant forbade the action needed to satisfy it, and assumed a plug-detect signal the DB9 lacks. Explicit mode makes the rule non-circular; a PS/2 device's spontaneous BAT `$AA` gives a *passive* hint that recovers most auto-detect convenience with zero risk to a joystick, because nothing is ever driven |
| D67 | **Six-button acquisition published as an 8-sample table with explicit signature, active-low inversion, and device-identification rules for 3-button / 6-button / Master System / unplugged / malformed** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D3) | prose ("repeated, and on the third TH=hi") | prose is not implementable in RTL and left the signature, sample order, inversion convention and failure cases ambiguous; the 6-button signature is sample 8 reading D0–D3 = 1111, which a 3-button pad structurally cannot produce (it drives D2/D3 low on every TH-low) |
| D66 | **Controller acquisition rate corrected 4 kHz → ≈400 Hz (≈2.5 ms/port), latency restated ≈2.5 ms** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D2) | ~250 µs/4 kHz polling (G11's original figure) | Sega's six-button sequence requires **>2 ms TH-high between acquisitions** for the pad's counter to reset — a 250 µs cadence never allows it, so original and clone pads would misreport. The corrected rate is still far inside one 16.6 ms frame, so the sub-frame conclusion survives with an honest number; one cadence is used for both pad types to keep a single verifiable timing model |
| D65 | **SELECT driven by a 5V `SN74AHCT125`, not the 3.3V LVC244A** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-D1) | driving SELECT from a 3.3V LVC output; open-drain + pull-up on SELECT | the controller's 74HC157 is a 5V **HC** part needing ~0.7 × V_CC ≈ 3.5V for a valid high, which a 3.3V LVC output cannot guarantee over rail/load/temperature — the multiplex and 6-button handshake would be unreliable. AHCT accepts 3.3V logic (TTL V_IH 2.0V) and outputs ~4.9V; one quad package covers both ports. LVC channels remain correct for the 5V→3.3V receive direction |
| D64 | **§6.3 reclassified as a signal budget, not a pinout; power/ground derived (≥1 VDD/VSS pair per ~8 SSOs + core + isolated PLL) = ~18–22; full 144-pin table made an explicit pre-tapeout deliverable** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C8) | presenting "~18 power pins / ~13 spare / comfortable" as a result; fabricating a pin table without a PDK | the budget legitimately answers "does this fit LQFP-144?" (yes, 8–12 spare) but cannot be a pad-ring spec — I/O cell library, ESD strategy, SSO current and bond-wire inductance are unknown until the §6.4/G30 fab route is engaged. Better to state the derivation and owe the table than to imply precision |
| D63 | **Breakpoint RDY asserted on the *following* cycle, with a published timing budget and mandated logic-analyser validation** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C7) | claiming same-cycle assertion "within nanoseconds" | the measured path (t_ADS + ribbon/transceiver + PIO detect + open-drain edge ≈ 79 ns) does not fit the ~70 ns available before PHI2 falls in the same cycle; deferring one cycle gives the full 139.7 ns with margin. Accepted consequence: the CPU halts having fetched the breakpoint opcode, so the probe reports the latched fetch address — ordinary bus-breakpoint behaviour, invisible to the developer |
| D62 | **Probe given a defined electrical design**: 74LVC245 transceivers (default receive), ~33 Ω series damping, OE pulled disabled so an unpowered probe is high-Z, header-only power with a Schottky blocking USB back-feed, ≤300 mA budget (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C6) | direct MCU-to-bus connection with no transceivers, termination or power-state definition | an unpowered probe's protection diodes would clamp 36 bus lines and could crash a running console; USB back-powering would energise oito and the SRAMs through their I/O pins with the console off — the classic way to destroy a debug target; unterminated ribbon rings at a 7.16 MHz bus rate |
| D61 | **Probe MCU changed RP2040 → RP2350B (48 GPIO, 3× PIO @150MHz)** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C5) | RP2040 + external latches/multiplexers; a CPLD/FPGA probe | the header carries 36 non-power signals and RP2040 has only 30 GPIO — the advertised feature set was physically impossible; external latches would add capture latency exactly where breakpoint timing is tightest, and an FPGA is a large cost/toolchain jump for an accessory. 48 GPIO wires all 36 concurrently with no multiplexing |
| D60 | **Single-step encoded on the existing `DBḠ` wire — level = debug mode, rising edge = advance one cycle, implicit ack via the observed PHI2 pulse, entry/exit sampled at cycle boundaries** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C4) | a separate request/ack pin pair; letting the probe drive PHI2 | there was only one wire and no protocol on it, so "one cycle per request" was unimplementable; the probe driving PHI2 would contend with oito's output; edge-encoding needs no new pins, which matters because the 40-pin header is fixed and the probe is already I/O-constrained (SOL-C5) |
| D59 | **Instruction jamming keeps `BE` HIGH and relies on oito chip-select suppression; `BE` reserved for true bus mastering; bench validation mandated before RTL freeze** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C3) | tri-stating the CPU via BE while injecting opcodes | BE low makes Address, Data **and** R/W̄ high-Z, and because the data buffer is bidirectional it plausibly disables the CPU's data *input* — the CPU could never consume the injected byte; holding BE high also preserves A0–A15/SYNC so the probe can still qualify injection on opcode fetch. CS-suppression already provided the contention-freedom BE was reached for |
| D58 | **Normative control-line termination**: RDY open-drain wired-AND with pull-up (CPU `WAI` + oito + probe), BE/IRQ̄/RES̄ pulled up, NMĪ and SOB tied high; oito RDY/BE pads open-drain (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C2) | assuming an internal RDY pull-up (the modern W65C02S has none and RDY is bidirectional); leaving "NMI unused" as an electrical statement | three parties share RDY and the CPU drives it low during `WAI`, so only a wired-AND scheme works — push-pull driving would fight the CPU; floating unused inputs are a classic intermittent-crash source |
| D57 | **oito's `R/W̄` pad made bidirectional and driven read-high for the whole PCM steal; full bus-master timing sequence pinned, releasing BE before RDY** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-C1) | leaving RWB to the CPU during steals (BE tri-states it, leaving the cartridge strobe floating while ROM_CĒ is asserted — a potential flash write); generating cartridge strobes independently | WDC specifies BE tri-states Address, Data *and* RWB, so a bus master must drive all three; costs no extra pin since R/W̄ is already in the host-bus group; BE-before-RDY ordering stops the CPU clocking a cycle with its outputs still tri-stated |
| D56 | **Canonical boot image is the compatibility contract; fast-boot defined by a generated, byte-identical handoff-state table (§11.1.1) with a versioned handoff-state number** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A13) | "the documented post-init state" (no such document existed); removing fast-boot entirely | undefined handoff state is a compatibility fork hiding inside an optimisation — two emulators could start the same cartridge differently; deriving the table from the firmware keeps the two in sync by construction, and versioning it makes firmware changes that alter handoff visibly compatibility-relevant |
| D55 | **Cursor bounds selector widened to 6 fields (real 9-bit X, inclusive, invalid rect disables clamping); `HIT_TILE_HI` added and `HIT_STATUS` given a 2-bit winner field** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A11 and SOL-A12) | one-byte bound value claiming a nonexistent hi bit; truncating hit tile IDs to 8 bits; separate hi registers per plane | both were fields too narrow for the values they promised; widening the *selector* and packing both high nibbles into one register avoids new address space; a winner field answers "which layer did I click?", which a single sprite-winner flag could not |
| D54 | **Text attribute byte re-encoded**: fg 4 bits, bg 2 bits (entries 0–3), opaque flag, blink; per-cell inverse dropped; blink = global 1 Hz, 50% duty, phase-reset on enable (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A10 and SOL-B13) | keeping the impossible "bg entry + inverse + blink in 3 bits"; expanding cells to 3 bytes | the original allocation simply did not fit; a 3-byte cell would grow an 80×28 map to 6,720 B and break the 2-byte stride; inverse is redundant because fg and bg live in the same byte and can be swapped by the author; 4 background entries cover panel + highlight, and the entries are chosen per sub-palette |
| D53 | **§8.5 APU reference model made normative** — enumerated globals (16 active flags need 2 bytes), ADSR rate table, LFSR seed/direction/taps, 240Hz sweep clock, non-interpolating wavetable, 8.8 PCM pitch with exclusive end address, ADPCM loop-state restore, 20-bit current-position, 20-bit accumulator with hard clamp, golden-hash conformance (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A9) | leaving behaviour as prose in §8.1–8.4; keeping "exponential step tables"/"soft-clamp" (untestable); 16-bit current-position | voice layouts without arithmetic cannot yield identical audio across implementations; a separate normative section keeps §8 readable while giving emulator authors one exact contract; the 16-bit position field contradicted 20-bit sample addressing introduced by D36 |
| D52 | **Blitter given a normative operational definition** (pitch registers, mode enum, mask source, zero = NO-OP, per-nibble transparency, ascending overlap, RMW on odd edges, ignore-while-busy, IRQ on final byte) **and `VIDEO_CTRL` render-mode bit deleted** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A8) | leaving behaviour to implementers; inventing a meaning for the render-mode bit; W/H = 0 meaning 256 | eleven externally-visible behaviours were undefined, so two conformant emulators could render differently; source/destination pitch was missing entirely, without which only contiguous runs can be copied; the render-mode bit was incoherent because the blitter writes VRAM rather than compositing, so deletion is more honest than invention |
| D51 | **Banked calls via SDK `far_call` wrappers, one compilation unit per bank + post-link packer, `pc_start=$8040`** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A7) | depending on prog8 `extsub @bank` (upstream implements it only for targets with real bank support, and emits target-specific code); expecting a 64KB-address-space compiler to build a 1MB banked image | turns a compiler-capability gamble into a build tool we own and can test; explicit wrappers are auditable from IRQ context; the packer also fixes the header/`pc_start` collision at $8000 and owns vector emission |
| D50 | **`%option romable` normative; ROM→RAM init copy; build-time ROM-safety gate; exact toolchain pin; prog8 ROM-ability declared *pending validation*** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A6 and SOL-G1) | assuming default prog8 output is ROM-safe (it may emit self-modifying code and inline variables); a version *floor* ("≥12.0") as a pin | a cartridge executes from flash, so any runtime write into the image is fatal, and upstream marks `romable` experimental — the honest position is a mandatory option plus a build gate that fails on any write target outside $0000–$3FFF, with a published conformance fixture. Recorded fallback if it fails: patch the compiler or revisit D12 |
| D49 | **IRQ trampoline calls the handler via a pushed synthetic return address; wrapper saves A/X/Y and issues `CLD`; NMI vector points at a real `RTI`** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A5) | plain `JMP ($0014)` (a jump, not a call — the handler's `RTS` would pop the interrupted code's return address); self-modifying `JSR` operand (incompatible with ROM execution, SOL-A6); fixed direct call table (loses runtime installability) | push-return + indirect jump is the standard 6502 idiom for an indirect *call* and needs no writable code; `CLD` is required because 6502 interrupts do not clear decimal mode while prog8 assumes binary arithmetic |
| D48 | **All unmapped I/O space reads $00 and ignores writes; no open bus, no mirroring; write-only registers read $00** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A4) | open bus / last-data-bus latch; mirroring the register file across $5000–$7FFF; leaving each range separately worded | open bus is timing-dependent and the classic source of emulator divergence, while a synchronous ASIC can drive $00 for free; mirroring would invite alias dependencies and freeze register-space growth; one rule for every case is testable. Cost: software must shadow write-only registers, which the runtime already does |
| D47 | **`CART_CONFIG_BANKS`/`CART_CONFIG_SAVE`/`CART_LOCK` ($4082–$4084)**: firmware loads cartridge geometry + save metadata from the header and locks it; fixed bank = `bank_count−1`; `BANK_SELECT` masked/mirrored (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A3) | oito parsing the header itself (needs a ROM parser in silicon); padding every ROM to 1MB (wasteful and still leaves save metadata unreachable); leaving the fixed bank undefined | the fixed window must select the *last physical* bank, which is unknowable without a size register — broken for every cartridge under 1MB; `SAVE_CTRL` referenced a header bit oito could never see (a defect introduced by G41/D37); locking at handoff stops a game restating its own geometry; masking gives classic mirroring instead of undefined reads |
| D46 | **Add `BOOT_W̄Ē` + `BOOT_FLASH_WE` gate; boot flash = SST39VF010 128KB with JEDEC program/erase** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A2) | omitting a write strobe (made the "field-updateable" firmware impossible); reusing `RAM_W̄Ē` as the flash write strobe | the two-stage brick-safe boot exists to allow field updates, which need a program strobe; a *shared* strobe would let a mis-decoded stray write program the firmware and defeat the whole design, so one dedicated pin buys a large safety margin; a reset-cleared enable bit mirrors the `SAVE_CTRL` write-protect idiom; naming the same SST39 family as the cartridge means one flash algorithm serves both |
| D45 | **`BOOT_SRC` (BOOT_CTRL bits 0–1) selects internal stub / external flash / disabled for the $E000–$FFFF overlay; stub mirrored 4×; recovery reads a firmware image via the cartridge window** (SOL review 2026-07-26, resolves SOL-A1) | a single overlay-enable bit (left the internal stub unmapped and unreachable); stub at $F800–$FFFF with the rest of the window undefined | the stub must own $FFFC/D at reset yet still validate external flash, which requires *both* mappings and a switch between them; the RAM-trampoline is already the spec's idiom for remapping the running window; 4× mirroring keeps every read defined; recovery via the cart window reuses hardware that always works |
| D44 | **2KB-unit base registers: bits 0–5 significant, bits 6–7 unwired (ignored, read 0); `CURSOR_TILE` = 12-bit tile index** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G50) | leaving the excess bits undefined; declaring them reserved-must-be-zero | 8 bits × 2KB reaches 512KB against 128KB of VRAM, so 2 bits are meaningless; *ignored* is more forgiving than *must-be-zero* (a stray write gets a defined in-range result), costs nothing to implement, and read-back-0 makes truncation visible to tooling; the cursor pointer now matches the 12-bit index width used everywhere else |
| D43 | **CH7035B driven as 24-bit RGB-888 with oito's 12 bits bit-replicated** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G49) | RGB-444 direct (not a supported input mode on the part); zero-filling the low nibble; RGB-666 | the datasheet lists only 565/666/888, so 4:4:4 cannot connect one-to-one; replication maps $F→$FF so full-scale white is 100% (zero-fill gives 94% — a dull white); costs only a fan-out of 2 on 12 PCB traces, no parts and no oito pins; the CH9904 config must select 888 |
| D42 | **Tone generators clocked at master ÷ 12 (1.789773MHz) with 12-bit periods** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G48) | driving tone dividers from the 21.48MHz master (lowest tone 655Hz ≈ E5 — no bass); widening the period to 16 bits at master speed | ÷12 is *exactly* the NES APU clock (same colorburst ancestry), so range becomes **27.3Hz–112kHz** and existing NES period tables port unchanged; 12 bits fit the existing register layout (pulse `+1` bit 3 was free outside noise mode), so no reshuffling; one prescaler in oito, no pins |
| D41 | **On-die 1KB shadow OAM; corrected VRAM bandwidth table** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G46) | per-line OAM scanning straight from VRAM (costs 57K–229K accesses/frame and made the old "≈80K display fetch / 278K free" figure unattainable) | standard VDP practice (NES/Genesis) and the single biggest bandwidth win available; 1KB on-die is small beside the 6KB font RAM; honest budget becomes ~175–200K display / ~157–182K free, which still supports the interleaved blitter. Accepted cost: OAM writes via the VRAM port take effect next frame (why OAM DMA exists) |
| D40 | **Text layer: 2,240-cell budget (cols×28), each font bank 3KB holding both 8×8 and 4×8 sets, `TEXT_CTRL` sub-palette select** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G45) | 2,000-cell budget (didn't cover 80×28); deriving 4×8 glyphs by column-dropping the 8×8 face (mangles letterforms); leaving the text palette implicit | the layer is 28 rows tall so the map must be sized cols×28 — 2KB more VRAM out of 128KB is free; a 4-wide face is a genuinely different bitmap and 1KB/bank is cheap on-die; an explicit sub-palette field matches the cursor's and removes the ambiguity |
| D39 | **OAM DMA source is 1KB-aligned; full burst ≈683 CPU cycles, VBLANK-recommended, non-abortable; `OAM_DMA_LEN` allows partial transfers** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G43) | 256-byte page source (4× too small); shrinking OAM entries to 4 bytes; no length register | 8-byte entries are fully used by the §6.6 sprite format, so OAM stays 1KB and the *source* must match; 1KB of 16KB RAM is a normal shadow-OAM trade (NES spends 256B of 2KB); a length register turns the common "moved a few sprites" case from 683 cycles into tens |
| D38 | **`BOOT_BANK` ($4008) pages the boot flash in 8KB pages through the $E000–$FFFF overlay; page 0 resident** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G42) | a second/larger overlay window; a flat 64–128KB aperture (no address space for it); putting charsets/layouts in the cartridge instead | reuses the console's existing fixed+banked idiom rather than inventing a second paging model; a resident page 0 makes page switching safe; 4 bits covers the stated 128KB max; inert after handoff so a running game is unaffected; gives the emulator an exact address contract |
| D37 | **Save chip maps over $8000–$BFFF via `SAVE_CTRL` ($4081)**, with write-protect and sub-16KB mirroring (review 2026-07-25, resolves G41) | a dedicated save window (no free address space); the reserved $5000–$7FFF (only 12KB — can't hold a 16KB save, and spends I/O space on a rarely-used device) | zero address-space cost and no new pins; the 16KB aperture matches A0–A13 exactly; it is the standard cartridge-RAM idiom (Genesis SRAM-over-ROM, GB MBC); save access is rare/bursty so briefly hiding the ROM bank is harmless, and the SDK hides the swap entirely |
| D36 | **PCM fetched by oito bus-mastering the CPU bus** (RDY+BE, NES-DMC-style bursts); sample addresses narrowed to **20-bit/1MB** (review 2026-07-25, resolves G40) | 24-bit/16MB linear addressing (unreachable); buffered-only PCM (kills long samples); dedicated cart address bus (+14 pins) | the cart is addressed by the CPU's own A0–A13, so independent fetch is impossible; bidirectional address pads make oito a bus master for **+2 pins (BE, RDY)** instead of 14; costs ~3–6% of CPU cycles and trades an exact *instruction* budget for an exact *bus* budget; lets samples cross bank boundaries |
| D14 | Event-driven SDK (`set_handler` + function refs) | polling loops in game code | register-free game code; SDK owns the IRQ dispatcher |
| D15 | Ghost port $4FFF logging | UART; memory buffer logging | 4 cycles/char, zero RAM, inert in production, identical in emulator |
| D16 | Probe = RDY/SYNC/BE bus techniques on static core | software breakpoints in ROM; on-chip debug unit in oito (beyond JTAG) | zero ROM overhead, zero ASIC cost beyond 4 JTAG pins; NMOS-era 6502 couldn't do this |
| D17 | Synthesized DWARF ELF from 64tass listing | hand-written DWARF; abandoning source-level debug; LLVM-MOS-only | standard IDE tooling "tricked" with a code-free symbols ELF |
| D18 | SkyWater 130nm, full-custom (Caravel/chipIgnite abandoned) | dedicated mask set; structured ASIC/eFPGA; FPGA product; Caravel harness | open PDK keeps EDA cost low, but Caravel's ~38 I/O / ~100-part QFN shuttle can't carry a 104-signal, low-thousands LQFP-144 part; custom pad ring required; fab route + economics re-open (§17-G30) |
| D19 | oito package = **LQFP-144**, **~134–138 pins used** (116 signal + ~18–22 power/ground — G47, +BOOT_W̄Ē SOL-A2, +DE SOL-E4, +MCLK SOL-E12; a signal budget, not a pinout — SOL-C8), custom pad ring (decision 2026-07) | LQFP-64 (unbuildable — G7); LQFP-128 (fits only if controllers moved off-chip) | keeps 8-bit VRAM (real 128KB, full blitter bandwidth) and in-oito joypad read (change IRQ); **8–12 spare pins** after the G40/G41/G47/SOL-A2 additions (RDY, BE, SAVE_CĒ, RAM and boot-flash strobes incl. BOOT_W̄Ē) — LQFP-144 still comfortably correct, LQFP-128 would now be tight |
| D20 | Master clock 21.47727MHz (6×NTSC); **CPU = master ÷ 3 = 7.159MHz** (decision 2026-07) | separate CPU oscillator; 8MHz via 24MHz master (breaks NTSC lock, sits at rating limit) | synchronous 3:1 ratio → deterministic, exact 455 CPU cycles/line; ~12% margin under the 8MHz@3.3V rating. Trims marketed "8MHz" to ~7.16MHz |
| D21 | **16-voice APU** (4 PSG + 8 wavetable + 4 PCM) replacing the 4-ch PSG (decision 2026-07) | keep 4-ch PSG (under-powered vs VDP); add FM operators (Genesis flavor, more logic, hard to emulate) | matches audio to the VDP tier; no extra pins (PWM+I²S unchanged), small on-die wave RAM + ROM-streamed PCM (no external audio RAM); wavetable+PCM chosen over FM for flexibility + emulability |
| D22 | **Two-stage boot ROM** (internal ~2KB stub + external updateable flash) + smart-cartridge family (decision 2026-07) | boot straight into cart (no firmware); internal-only fixed ROM; external-only (brick risk) | internal stub = unbrickable recovery, external flash = updateable firmware; one connector serves game/dev/update/SD carts (reads+writes pass through); dev-bootstrap via probe over the expansion header (no console USB); SD cart = digital games + firmware updates |
| D23 | **Multi-rail power** (5V/3.3V/1.8V/PLL — the ~1.2V rail was an error, deleted by SOL-E1/D72) from 9V; supervisor PoR/brownout (decision 2026-07) | single 3.3V rail (never feasible — 5V/1.8V always required) | multi-rail is mandatory (AD725/controllers 5V, oito core 1.8V, CH7035B 1.8V+3.3V domains — corrected by SOL-E1); the CPU/memory/logic domain stays single-3.3V, preserving the no-level-shifter benefit |
| D24 | **60Hz-only, single timing**; NTSC composite; region-agnostic RGB/VGA/HDMI (decision 2026-07) | dual 60/50Hz PAL; PAL60 | one clock/crystal, no PAL slowdown, HDMI covers modern TVs worldwide, RGB SCART region-agnostic; only pure-composite into a 50Hz-only vintage CRT is unsupported |
| D25 | **Canonical raster timing**: shared 1,365-tick/455-cycle line, 262 lines, 60.055Hz fixed; dot clocks 341/426 dots-line as independent PLL outputs (decision 2026-07) | per-mode line lengths; selectable 60.00/59.94 | one line/frame timing means mode-switch never re-syncs the display; independent dot-clock PLL makes hi-res dot count exact without an integer master divider; no clean 60.00/59.94 exists on an NTSC-locked ÷3 line, and the CH7035B re-times HDMI anyway |
| D26 | **Bare-metal IRQ via RAM-vector trampoline**: fixed $FFFE/F → `__irq_entry` → `JMP ($14)`; `sys.set_irq()` writes the RAM cell (decision 2026-07) | patch the ROM vector (impossible at runtime); resident OS vector table | ROM vectors are fixed at build time; a RAM-indirected stub is the standard 6502 pattern (C64 CINV) and needs no OS; SEI-wrap makes the update atomic |
| D27 | **prog8 variable inspection via flat typed symbol table** (from `-dumpvars`), no synthesized `.debug_info` (decision 2026-07) | synthesize full variable/type DWARF | prog8's static allocation has no stack frames, so a fixed name→address→type table is complete; DWARF frame-base machinery would be effort for nothing |
| D28 | **Pin prog8 ≥ 12.0 and bundle a known-good `prog8c`** in the SDK (decision 2026-07) | float on latest upstream; fork the compiler | reproducible builds immune to language drift; bundling is the source of truth and avoids a compiler fork (a stated design goal) |

---

## 2. Gaps & inconsistencies register (resolution history)

*Provenance note:* G1–G29 were derived from the three source conversations. G30–G38 (and expansions of several earlier entries) were added after an external adversarial hardware review (`re8-adversarial-hardware-analysis.md`, 2026-07-19); see §18. **G40–G53 were added by the final technical review (2026-07-25)** — a self-consistency, arithmetic, datasheet and toolchain audit of this document after feature freeze. Unlike G1–G39 (which were largely *inherited* ambiguities), G40–G53 are **defects found in the spec's own resolved text**; several are genuine design conflicts, not wording issues.

### A. Blocking for any emulator (memory/registers/timing)

- **G1 — CPU clock. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §3, D20.]** Master clock pinned at **21.47727 MHz** (6× NTSC colorburst); **CPU PHI2 = master ÷ 3 = 7.159 MHz**, synchronous (fixed 3:1). Exact budget: **455 CPU cycles/line, 119,210/frame** (262 × 1,365 master ticks). Corrects the marketed "8 MHz" to ~7.16 MHz (page-update item, G28). Exact raster line-totals/porches → new G39.
- **G2 — ZP $0000–$001F reservation. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §5.0.]** Not hardware: zero page is ordinary RAM, decoded by nothing in oito. The "internal chip logic" comment was a misnomer for a **software convention** — the re8 runtime/boot stub reserve these 32 bytes for fast variables (IRQ scratch, VBLANK/frame flags, joypad shadows, VRAM-pointer shadow, bank shadow, runtime pointers), and prog8 allocates from $0020 up. Emulator treats it as plain RAM. A bare-metal program not using the runtime may reclaim it.
- **G3 — Register map. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §5.1.]** The three conflicting generations are replaced by a single canonical `$4000–$40FF` map with defined addresses, R/W direction, bit-fields and reset state. Knocks down or advances G4, G6, G13, G14 and gives G5/G27/G37 concrete register anchors.
- **G4 — Audio system. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §8, D21.]** The under-powered 4-ch PSG is replaced by a **16-voice APU** (4 enhanced PSG + 8 wavetable + 4 PCM/ADPCM) with a full register file, ADSR envelopes, sweep, 15-bit LFSR noise (defined taps), IMA-ADPCM, on-die wave RAM, ROM-streamed PCM, a stereo mixer at ~48 kHz, and I²S + PWM output — all reachable via the $40A0 indirect port. Fully emulatable. (Marketing "4-channel PSG" now needs updating to the APU — page-refresh item, G28.)
- **G5 — CPU↔VRAM access. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — §5.1 interface + §6.5 arbitration.]** Interface: auto-incrementing VRAM address/data port ($4040–$4044); blitter runs VRAM→VRAM; ROM→VRAM loads are CPU loops through VRAM_DATA; OAM via CPU-RAM→VRAM DMA ($4061). Arbitration: single 21.47 MHz VRAM bus, fixed priority **display > blitter > CPU port**; the CPU may access VRAM any time but is **RDY-stalled** until a free cycle (immediate in blanking, brief stall in active display); the blitter's blanking-only vs interleaved modes are the ~406 vs ~1,187 tiles/frame figures. **Residual (cycle-accurate pass only):** exact fetch schedule and stall counts — not emulator-blocking. *Corollary noted:* "graphics never steal a CPU cycle" is overstated — VRAM_DATA transfers and OAM-DMA do stall the CPU.
- **G6 — Collision semantics. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — §6.6.]** COLLIDE_A/B latch the **first** colliding pair in raster order since the last COLLIDE_CLEAR (A=lower index, B=higher); COLLIDE_STATUS.1 sets on further distinct pairs; collision is on opaque sprite-pixel overlap independent of visual priority; overflow-dropped sprites don't participate. Fully emulatable.
- **G7 — oito pin budget & VRAM capacity contradiction. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §6.3.]** The unbuildable 64-pin tally (omitted power/ground, VRAM strobes, syncs, clocks, audio, mapper, controller I/O) and the 64KB-vs-128KB VRAM error (2¹⁷ × 4 bits = 64KB) are fixed by moving to **LQFP-144** with an itemized budget (**116 signal / ~134–138 total** after G47, SOL-A2, SOL-E4 and SOL-E12), an **8-bit VRAM data bus** (VA0–16 + VD0–7 = real 128KB, full blitter bandwidth), and controllers read into oito. Package/pad-ring decision D19; fab-route economics moved to G30.
- **G13 — IRQ status/acknowledge semantics. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — see §5.1.]** IRQ_STATUS ($4002) is **write-1-to-clear** per bit; IRQ_ENABLE ($4001) masks sources; oito ORs enabled+pending onto IRQ̄; the CPU reads IRQ_STATUS to prioritize among coincident sources (no fixed hardware priority). Reads are non-destructive.
- **G14 — Bank register placement. [RESOLVED 2026-07.]** BANK_SELECT is canonically at **$4080** (§5.1), a 6-bit register for the $8000–$BFFF window. The `extsub @ $C000 @bank N` example remains a *separate* error — it names an address in the *fixed* window; banked code/data must live in $8000–$BFFF. `extsub` codegen must target the switchable window and write $4080. (The bank *address* conflict is closed; the example's window error is a documentation fix.)
- **G37 — Sprite overflow & priority contract. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — §6.6.]** Per-line evaluation scans OAM 0→127, keeps ≤32 (lowest index wins), drops the rest and sets STATUS.3; limit is 32 sprites with **no separate pixel/tile budget**; lower OAM index draws on top; six-level priority ladder (sprite/Plane-A/Plane-B × 2 priorities + backdrop) fully specified. (Worst-case wide-sprite fetch bandwidth is a cycle-accurate refinement — G39.)

**— Found by the final technical review (2026-07-25) —**

- **G40 — PCM cartridge streaming vs cartridge addressing.
