[ACCEPTED — blocker, D256.]** v0.3 gave an unconditional `8N + 6` while v0.3's own §6.5 subjected the VRAM write to slot arbitration and paused the transfer for the line-224 snapshot. Replaced by a two-phase state machine with a one-byte write buffer and **three stated cases**: `8N + 6` on blanking lines, up to 4× that under active display, plus up to 341 cycles when crossing the snapshot. Three stale **683-cycle** references purged.
- **R4-APU1 — A refill cannot fit the FIFO. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D257.]** Arithmetic I never did: a **16-byte burst** into a **16-byte FIFO** at an **8-byte threshold** requires 24 bytes of storage. Changed to `min(16, free_space, bytes_until_end)`, so the burst is 8 bytes at threshold. The worst-case PCM tax consequently falls from the impossible **12.1 %** to **6.7 %**, with ≈3.4 % sustained for four streaming voices.
- **R4-APU2 — Requests ordered before their own cause. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D258.]** The tick order placed the refill request at step 5 and the mix tick at step 6, while §8.5 said requests arise *from* the mix tick's drain. Reordered so the mix tick executes first and requests are raised after it, which moves the first stolen CPU cycle — software-visible against raster events.
- **R4-APU3 — Duplicated normative rule. [ACCEPTED, resolved in passing.]** Only one statement of add-then-consume now remains, alongside the pseudocode that encodes it.

#### Cartridge

- **R4-CART1 — `OE#` polarity is inverted. [ACCEPTED — blocker, and the most dangerous finding, D259.]** `R/W̄` is **high for read** and `OE#` is **active low**, so wiring one to the other disables the memory during reads and enables it during writes. On a CPU write with `ROM_CĒ` still asserted — the case §11.3 explicitly called "harmless" — the flash would have driven the data bus **against the CPU**. Corrected with equations in physical pin levels and **one 2-input NAND per device on the cartridge** (`74LVC1G00` for ROM-only, `74LVC2G00` for ROM+FRAM). Every reference board changes.
- **R4-CART2 — The flash reset net has no contact. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D260.]** The wiring table connected flash `RESET#` to a cartridge `RES̄` that the 37-contact list does not contain, and §1 still said 36. Resolved by **tying `RESET#` inactive on the cartridge** — the datasheet permits it, and a flash left mid-command is recovered by the updater's own reset command, which it must be able to issue anyway. Connector stated **once** as 37, with the arithmetic shown.

#### HDMI

- **R4-HDMI1 — The CH7035B has no MCLK input. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D261.]** Verified directly from Chrontel's datasheet: the audio interface is **pin 56 `I2S_D/SPDIF`, pin 57 `I2S_WS`, pin 58 `I2S_CK`** and there is no fourth pin. My four-wire connection was invented. Removed, along with oito's `MCLK` output — **126 → 125 signal pins**. The same fetch also yielded facts worth having: the part supports 48 kHz among its documented I²S rates, is orderable as **`CH7035B-BFI`** in 88-QFN with MOQ 168/tray, wants **1.8 kΩ** DDC pull-ups and a **47 kΩ** HPD pull-down, and has defined reserved-pin handling — several of which closed or tightened older items.
- **R4-HDMI2 — Public evidence does not establish the HDMI configuration. [ACCEPTED, D262.]** The datasheet titles the part a **DVI Transmitter**; its block diagram *does* contain a TERC4 encoder and an audio-packet mixer, so HDMI audio is plausible — but nothing public shows a CH9904 self-boot image selecting HDMI signalling, generating InfoFrames or setting N/CTS with no host. Kept provisional, with protocol-analyser capture added as a gate. Also noted: the documented input-resolution list tops out at 1366×768 and **does not include 256×224 or 320×224**.
- **R4-HDMI3 — `R_ILIM` = 75 kΩ does not give 250 mA. [ACCEPTED, D263.]** The limit is inversely proportional to `R_ILIM` (≈26,600/kΩ mA), so 75 kΩ gives ≈355 mA. Changed to **133 kΩ for a nominal 200 mA**, taken from TI's selection table rather than my arithmetic, with re-derivation from the min/max equations added as a gate. My "250 mA is the lowest setting" was also wrong — the part has a special 75 mA mode.
- **R4-HDMI4 — TMDS coupling contradicts itself. [ACCEPTED, D264.]** The protection table still said "AC-coupled" while §7.1 correctly said AN-B008 shows no such capacitors. Table corrected to "topology per AN-B008; no added series AC-coupling".
- **R4-HDMI5 — "Licensed HDMI product" asserted before licensing exists. [ACCEPTED, D265.]** Fair and important. HDMI LA treats a product as licensed only once the manufacturer is a registered Adopter *and* the first article has passed self-test and ATC testing — both listed as outstanding in the same document that called re8 licensed. **All such language is now conditional**: designed *as* an HDMI 1.4b source, trademarks not to be used until the gates close.
- **R4-HDMI6 — No exact approved connector. [ACCEPTED, D266.]** The licence requires a single complete model number from the Approved Connector list, not "19-pin Type-A". Added as a BOM and compliance gate.
- **R4-HDMI7 — "Any modern television" overstates it. [ACCEPTED, D267.]** The mode is fixed and unconditional, and §7.1 defines the no-picture failure, so the phrase is replaced by **"a compatible 1080p60 sink"** with the failure retained.

#### Audio

- **R4-AUD1 / R4-AUD2 — The ASRC is not implementable, and the quality claim is false. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D268. Owner decision.]** Both stand. Linear interpolation is not transparent because the ratio is near unity: it droops **−1.26 dB at 10 kHz, −2.89 at 15 kHz, −5.29 at 20 kHz**, and modulates as the fractional phase sweeps — my "far below the 16-bit floor" was simply wrong. The owner chose a proper filter, so it is now a **64-phase × 32-tap polyphase FIR** with published coefficient width, arithmetic widths, rounding, saturation, passband ripple, stopband and THD+N targets, plus a defined dual-clock FIFO, servo error equation, update cadence, trim bounds, startup and **explicit underflow/overflow behaviour**. The phase increment is derived from **exact integers** (21,477,270 × 256 / (448 × 12,288,000)), not the rounded decimal that would have given a different value.
- **R4-AUD3 — The jack cannot be both line-out and headphone drive. [ACCEPTED, D269. Owner decision.]** Correct on every count: coupling capacitors sized for 20 Hz into 10 kΩ give a **6.25 kHz corner into 32 Ω**, a ≤100 Ω source badly attenuates 32 Ω, the ±70 mA headline is specified into 150 Ω under different conditions, and level is owned by an APU register *game software* controls — not a safe headphone control. Owner chose **line-level only**, stated plainly, with headphones needing an external amplifier.
- **R4-AUD4 — Separate resistors do not make branches independent. [ACCEPTED, D270.]** True. Replaced the absolute claim with a bounded one — short-circuit tolerant, and no branch pulls another below 50 % under any *legal* load — added the missing TRS and RCA rows to the protection table, and specified mute as a **shunt FET per branch** off the power-good node rather than as an adjective.
- **R4-AUD5 — Old and new audio clocks conflict. [ACCEPTED, D271.]** §6.3 still had the PLL producing a 12.2727 MHz audio MCLK, superseded by the `AUDXI` oscillator. Removed. "Exactly 48.000 kHz" softened to **nominal**, since the oscillator is ±50 ppm.

#### Toolchain, editorial

- **R4-SW1 — Importing a prelude does not propagate `%option romable`. [ACCEPTED, D272.]** Correct — it is a module option and propagation to importers is not documented. The build now **injects the directive textually into every generated source** and fails if any unit lacks it.
- **R4-SW2 — Generated declarations cannot place compiler-created cells. [ACCEPTED, D273.]** The decisive point: source injection can only name what source declares, so it cannot relocate compiler temporaries, static parameter cells or existing BSS. **The contingency becomes normative** — switchable banks hold code and read-only data only, all mutable state lives in the fixed-bank unit — rather than waiting on a mechanism that may not exist.
- **R4-SW3 — Prog8 remains a selected experiment. [ACCEPTED as stated, D274.]** Agreed without qualification; the SDK and tool ABI are no longer described as implementation-ready.
- **R4-DBG1 — The corrected IRQ counts are still wrong. [ACCEPTED, D275.]** The epilogue count assumed the `BEQ` was *not* taken on the path where it is. Rather than publish a fourth guess, **the specification stops publishing hand-counted metrics** and generates them from the assembled listing. The "a fifth of the stack" claim was also wrong: 15 of 256 bytes is **6 %**.
- **R4-SPEC1 — Counts have drifted. [ACCEPTED, D276.]** §1 said 36-pin, §11 summed to 37, the BOM said ~152 oito pins, §6.3 said ~150, the version history said 125 against §6.3's 126. All reconciled and stated **once**, with `re8-pins-<ver>.csv` named as the future source.
- **R4-SPEC2 — RCA not propagated. [ACCEPTED, D277.]** Added to the BOM driver row and the protection table.
- **R4-SPEC3 — Stale statements. [ACCEPTED, D278.]** CEC rationale rewritten now that the link is HDMI; the sprite example corrected from "128 sprites of 8 px" to 32, since the count limit binds first.


### K. Sixth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round5.md`, 2026-07-27)

*Provenance:* external hostile re-review of spec **v0.5**. **32 findings — 7 blockers, 16 high, 8 medium, 1 editorial.** No capability decision needed the owner: every finding either corrected arithmetic, removed a contradiction, or made an existing claim honest. Result: spec **v0.6**.

**Every numerical claim in this review was verified independently before being accepted**, including the ones that were easiest to take on trust. All checked out — notably that the v0.5 ASRC servo has a pole at exactly 1.000.

#### Video fetch and CPU bus

- **R5-VID1 — Fine scroll needs an extra column. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D279.]** A wrapping plane with `SCROLL_X mod 8 ≠ 0` shows part of a tile at each edge and needs **`C + 1`** columns. Hi-res worst case is then `41 × 6 × 2 + 160 + 512 = 1,164` against **1,155** slots — nine fetches with no defined outcome, and a right edge that would have been wrong at every non-zero fine offset. Fixed by making the sprite budget **`min(512, capacity − BG − text)`**, which is what the queue order already implied: sprites are last, so sprites are what runs out. Worst case 503 bytes.
- **R5-VID2 — Line 261 must fetch line 0. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D280.]** The one-line-ahead scheme I introduced in v0.5 had no frame wrap. Line 0's data can only come from line 261, which contradicted the blanking table (which gave lines 225–261 entirely to the blitter), the line-0 latch point, and had no reset priming rule. Now: **line 261 carries a display fetch, lines 223–260 carry none, frame-latched registers move to the start of line 261**, and line 0 of the first frame after reset displays the backdrop. Blanking-only availability falls to **49,694** accesses — exactly the reviewer's figure — so 794 tiles becomes **776**.
- **R5-VID3 — The latch table contradicts the two-line latency. [ACCEPTED, D281.]** §6.5 said "two lines later" while §6.7 still said "next scanline"; all `CURSOR_*` were per-line while §9.2 said position was frame-latched; and font timing became ambiguous once the fetcher moved. Replaced with a table carrying **separate *sampled at* and *first displayed on* columns**, cursor position split out, and **font RAM reclassified as per-pixel** — it is read by the compositor, not the fetcher, so the lead never applied to it.
- **R5-VID4 — Exact fetching is both Tier 1 and Tier 2. [ACCEPTED, D282.]** Three stale passages still called the fetch pattern a Tier-2 residual after v0.5 made the queue Tier-1-observable. Corrected everywhere; **there is no Tier-2 residual in the fetch path**, and a renderer sampling "VRAM state at each pixel" is explicitly non-conforming.
- **R5-CPU1 — The request is raised too late for RDY. [ACCEPTED — blocker, and the sharpest hardware finding, D283.]** v0.5 said a `VRAM_DATA` access becomes pending *at the end of* its CPU cycle. That cannot work: the W65C02S samples read data on the PHI2 falling edge of that same cycle, and RDY must go low **before** that edge to hold the processor. A read cannot complete and be given its data afterwards. Fixed by **fixing the PHI2 edges within the 3-tick cycle** (rise at 3k, fall at 3k+2), decoding at **tick 3k+1**, and asserting RDY one full tick before the falling edge — with the margins to be proven on published waveforms rather than asserted.
- **R5-VRAM1 / R5-APU3 — Two `auto-increment` contradictions. [ACCEPTED, D284.]** Both register maps made increment conditional on a control bit while the normative prose said every access increments unconditionally. Since the reset value is 0, the two readings differed immediately after reset. Both made conditional.

#### DMA, PCM, blitter

- **R5-DMA1 — One byte cannot hold two. [ACCEPTED, D285.]** The claim that a one-byte write buffer lets the next source read proceed while the current byte waits is simply false — the entry is occupied. Changed to a **two-entry FIFO**, with source reads stalling when both are full.
- **R5-DMA2 — Favourable-phase examples. [ACCEPTED, D286.]** 1,024 master ticks is 341⅓ CPU cycles and resumption is restricted to a PHI2 boundary, so the snapshot penalty rounds to **342** and depends on entry phase; the 54-cycle example is qualified as the blanking best case.
- **R5-APU1 — 6.7 % is not a maximum. [ACCEPTED, D287.]** Correct and subtle: bounding the refill by *free space* is not enough, because the 8.8 pitch field lets a voice consume up to 255 samples in one mix tick and arrive at an empty FIFO, making the next grant a full 16 bytes and the cost 12.1 %. **The refill is now capped at 8 bytes unconditionally**, which makes 6.7 % a genuine ceiling under any pitch.
- **R5-APU2 — Ordering and CPU phase. [ACCEPTED, D288.]** §8.5 still said step 5 after the global order moved requests to step 7. Worse, "stolen cycles begin immediately after the mix tick" cannot hold: 448 and 3 are coprime, so a mix tick walks through all three CPU phases and the steal must wait for the in-flight cycle. A **per-phase timing table** now defines request, RDY, BE, first stolen byte and release.
- **R5-BLIT1 — An unstated mask cache. [ACCEPTED, D289.]** The algorithm called `maskbit` twice per byte while the prose charged one mask read per eight pixels — which silently assumed a cache. The **cache is now in the pseudocode** with its reload point and row-end discard. `BLIT_W`'s unit renamed from "pixels" to byte-pairs to match the algorithm.

#### Cartridge

- **R5-CART1 — The corrected equations sit above the uncorrected wiring. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D290.]** This is the worst kind of miss: round four's `OE#` polarity fix added correct equations and then left **both reference tables still saying `OE# ← R/W̄`**, recreating the exact bus-contention hazard the fix was for. Every reference connection now uses the NAND output.
- **R5-CART2 — The NAND invalidates the one-chip claims. [ACCEPTED, D291.]** A consequence I introduced and did not propagate: the spec still promised a one-chip cartridge, no glue logic, and FRAM sitting "directly on those lines". A plain cart is now **flash + `74LVC1G00`**, a saving cart **flash + FRAM + `74LVC2G00`**, both added to the BOM with decoupling. The claims are withdrawn.
- **R5-CART3 — `<< 13` versus `<< 14`. [ACCEPTED, D292.]** The wiring table puts cartridge A0 at flash bit 0 and A1–A13 at bits 1–13, so the offset is 14 bits and the bank shifts by **14** — which the same paragraph then confirmed with `BANK_SELECT = addr >> 14` while stating `<< 13`. Corrected.
- **R5-CART4 — Boot and cartridge flash called the same family. [ACCEPTED, D293.]** Left over from before the Macronix switch; §2 already said the opposite. Deleted.

#### HDMI and audio

- **R5-HDMI1 — MCLK stale in three places. [ACCEPTED, D294.]** Removed from the main pin budget last round but still present in the BOM row, §7.1 and §8.1. Purged.
- **R5-HDMI2 — Intra-pair matching off by 39×. [ACCEPTED, D295.]** AN-B008 §2.8.3 recommends **5 mil (0.127 mm)**; the spec said **5 mm**, which is ~197 mil. Corrected, with the 100 mil inter-pair figure added.
- **R5-HDMI3 — One present-tense logo claim survived. [ACCEPTED, D296.]** The licensing section correctly said the trademarks may not be used yet, then said "re8 uses it" two sentences later — so v0.5's claim that all language became conditional was itself false. Fixed.
- **R5-HDMI4 — Gates remain open. [ACCEPTED as stated.]** Agreed; provisional status retained.
- **R5-HDMI5 — DDC had two schematics. [ACCEPTED, D297.]** Routing it and leaving it unconnected are different circuits with different compliance behaviour, and deferring the choice is not a reference design. **Routed** is now the decision; only the pull-up rail and isolation part remain open.
- **R5-AUD1 — 32 taps cannot give 100 dB. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D298.]** Verified with the Kaiser order estimate: for a 4 kHz transition, **32 taps/phase yields ≈46 dB** and 100 dB needs ≈78. The target was asserted, never computed. Changed to **64 phases × 64 taps at ≥80 dB** (≈85 dB by the same estimate), with a **published generator script** so the ROM is a build artefact rather than a magic table.
- **R5-AUD2 — The stopband edge is meaningless and the algorithm incomplete. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D299.]** "Beyond 24 kHz" describes frequencies a 48 kHz output cannot represent; what matters is the **input** Nyquist, **23.97 kHz**. Added phase-selection bits, history advance, coefficient generation, stereo scheduling and normalisation, and stated plainly that **no vector exists yet**, so the ASRC is specified but not yet reproducible.
- **R5-AUD3 — The servo is unstable. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D300.]** Verified: the integral-only law `increment += e × 2¹²` on an integrating plant gives poles at **1.000 and 0.996** — marginally unstable, a limit cycle rather than a one-second settle. Replaced by a **PI law with Kp = 2¹⁸, Ki = 2¹⁴**, poles at **0.912 and 0.822**, with the loop gain `g = K/2³²` and the characteristic polynomial shown so the result can be checked rather than believed.
- **R5-AUD4 — Contradictory startup. [ACCEPTED, D301.]** "Prefilled to 16" then "released once full-depth-reached" named two different states. Now one: **exactly 16 frames**, accumulator zero, phase zero, history cleared, servo enabled from the first update.
- **R5-AUD5 — "Exactly 48 kHz". [ACCEPTED, D302.]** The oscillator is ±50 ppm, so the physical rate is nominal. "Exact" is now reserved for the rational used to compute the phase increment.
- **R5-AUD6 — RCA still missing from the BOM. [ACCEPTED, D303.]** Added to the driver row and its interface field; "the two analog paths" corrected to three.

#### Toolchain, power, integrity

- **R5-SW1 — `romable` still in the property list. [ACCEPTED, D304.]** Removed from the target-properties list and snippet; it appears only as generated source text.
- **R5-SW2 — One origin cannot describe three. [ACCEPTED, D305.]** A real gap: prog8 has no linker and the packer cannot relocate assembled code, yet only `pc_start = $8040` was given. **Three origin classes** are now defined — bank 0 at `$8040`, other switchable banks at `$8000`, the fixed bank at `$C000` — with a **fixed-bank-first build order** and a generated `romsub` interface file for cross-bank calls.
- **R5-SW3 — The banked-code rule is not self-enforcing. [ACCEPTED, D306.]** Correct: an ordinary routine in a switchable unit makes the compiler allocate parameter cells and temporaries invisibly. The rule is now **checked by an assembly scan** that fails on any write target or symbol inside `$0000–$3FFF`, and is labelled *selected pending validation* rather than established.
- **R5-HW1 — Two current-limit implementations. [ACCEPTED, D307.]** The BOM assigned `TPS2553` switches, the power section specified polyfuses, and neither gave settings. Settled on **one `TPS2553` per output with stated `R_ILIM` values and fault behaviour; polyfuses removed** — with re-derivation from the min/max equations required, since that arithmetic was already got wrong once.
- **R5-SPEC1 — "Generated" claimed before the generators exist. [ACCEPTED, D308.]** §16.3 says none of the artefacts exist, yet the reset list and pin prose described themselves as generated — giving false confidence that cross-checks were automated. Both moved to future tense with the current hand-maintained status stated.


### L. Seventh adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round6.md`, 2026-07-27)

*Provenance:* external hostile re-review of spec **v0.6**. **25 findings — 4 blockers, 11 high, 6 medium, 4 editorial.** No owner decision was needed: every finding corrected arithmetic, removed a contradiction, or narrowed an overclaim. Result: spec **v0.7**.

Two of the four blockers were **defects in round five's own fixes**, and one of those is instructive: the `OE#` gate added to fix a polarity error could not be built from the signals available. Correct algebra, wrong premise about what the cartridge edge carries.

#### CPU and bus timing

- **R6-CPU1 — The PHI2 low pulse violates the W65C02S. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D309.]** Verified: the 3.3 V AC table gives `t_PWH` ≥ 62 ns and `t_PWL` ≥ 63 ns, and a 139.7 ns cycle has only **14.7 ns** of slack over their sum. My 2-tick/1-tick split gave a **46.6 ns low pulse — 16.4 ns short**, and the reverse split fails the high pulse, so **no whole-master-tick division works at all**. Calling it a later schematic check did not make the normative waveform valid. PHI2 becomes a **50 %-duty clock generated from a 2× master phase**, edges at half-tick positions, 69.8 ns each with 6.8 ns margin — real but slim, and now a §16.2 PVT gate.
- **R6-CPU2 — Same-tick request and grant unordered. [ACCEPTED, D310.]** The request was "raised at tick 3k+1" while the grant is step 2 of that tick, and the event order contained no request-detection step — so two implementations could differ by a whole supercycle on every immediate grant. **CPU request detection is now step 2, before arbitration.**
- **R6-PCM1 — The phase timetable uses the wrong falling edge. [ACCEPTED, D311.]** §8.5 said the in-flight cycle completes at "the next multiple of 3" and that RDY at tick *m* always catches it. With PHI2 falling at **3k+1.5**, that is false at `m mod 3 = 2`, where the edge has already passed. All three phases are now tabulated, **phase 2 defers the halt by one CPU cycle**, and the 0.5-tick setup at phase 1 is flagged as the machine's tightest case. OAM DMA inherits the same three cases, so its `+6` overhead is a phase-0 figure.

#### Video

- **R6-VID1 — The queue fetches `C` after the budget moved to `C + 1`. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D312.]** Round five charged the extra fine-scroll column in the budget and **never added it to the normative queue**, so the line buffer could not obtain the partial right-edge tile it had paid for. The queue now enumerates `0 … C` per plane, each plane tested independently, with the viewport-to-map column mapping and the row derivation stated.
- **R6-VID2 — Every revised throughput total is wrong. [ACCEPTED, D313.]** Correct, and my numbers were stale in two ways: they used a 1,152-access line that no longer exists, and they ignored that line 223 fetches nothing. Recomputed per mode and cross-checked against the reviewer's independently: **49,691 / 49,810** blanking-only, **97,886 / 124,542** interleaved, **776 / 778** and **1,529 / 1,945** tiles. The "unallocated 1,152" row was also false — those slots fall through by the queue rule — and is deleted. Note the inversion this exposes: **lo-res now has more blanking-only bandwidth than hi-res**, because its lighter line-261 load frees more of that line.
- **R6-VID3 — Tier assignment still contradicted in three places. [ACCEPTED, D314.]** The round-five fix reached §6.5's summary but not the emulator guidance, §6.6, or §14.5 — one of which still told renderers to sample "VRAM as it stands at each pixel", which is exactly what a one-line-ahead buffer does not do. All three corrected.
- **R6-VID4 — Sprite limits contradict the 503-byte state. [ACCEPTED, D315.]** "32 of 32 px, exactly at the limit" is false when the budget is 503: **only 31 fit**. §6.6 also still carried "32 sprites, full stop — there is no separate per-line budget", and the `STATUS`.3 row still described only a 33rd-sprite drop. All made conditional on background and text state.
- **R6-VID5 — Cursor and pick latch at both line 261 and line 0. [ACCEPTED, D316.]** Three places said one, three said the other, and the difference is observable. Settled on **line 261** everywhere, because line 0's sprites are evaluated there.
- **R6-VID6 — Overflow ownership across the wrap. [ACCEPTED, D317.]** A genuine hole: line 0 of frame *N*+1 is evaluated during line 261 of frame *N*, so a single latch would be erased by the line-0 clear before software could read it. **Two accumulators** — a current-frame latch and a next-frame accumulator copied over at line 0 — with W1C touching only the former.
- **R6-VID7 — Priming describes an impossible history. [ACCEPTED, D318.]** "Primed during line 261 of the preceding frame" cannot apply at reset, where no preceding line 261 exists. Split into **three cases**, with the key clarification that **the fetcher runs whenever the raster runs, regardless of screen enable** — which limits the reset case to a single backdrop line and makes mid-frame enable lossless.
- **R6-VID8 — Same-tick font write versus read. [ACCEPTED, D319.]** Dual-port RAM can be write-first, read-first or no-change, and the tick order contained neither event. Both added, and the RAM specified **write-first**.

#### Cartridge and power

- **R6-CART1 — The NAND cannot implement its own equation. [ACCEPTED — blocker, and the round's most interesting finding, D320.]** Verified against all four input cases: the cartridge receives **active-low** `ROM_CĒ`, so `NAND(ROM_CĒ, R/W̄)` is **high during a selected read** — it disables the device, the exact failure the gate was added to prevent. Recovering the positive `selected` term needs a second inversion, i.e. two gates per device. **But none of that is necessary**: `CE#` already qualifies selection, so **`OE# = NOT(R/W̄)` is correct in all four cases** with a single inverter shared by both devices. The fix is simpler than the thing it replaces — **one `74LVC1G04GV` per cartridge** — with a truth table in physical levels.
- **R6-CART2 — Gate suffixes do not match their packages. [ACCEPTED, D321.]** `74LVC1G00GW` is TSSOP5/SOT353, not SC-70; `GT` is XSON8, not VSSOP-8. Moot once the NANDs went, but the replacement suffix was verified against Nexperia before being written down.
- **R6-PWR1 — `TPS2553` does not latch off. [ACCEPTED, D322.]** TI ships the plain part as *constant-current* and the **`-1` suffix** as latch-off; the power table specifies latch-off. Changed to `TPS2553-1DBVR` throughout. The `R_ILIM` values themselves check out (≈493 mA at 53.2 kΩ, ≈202 mA at 133 kΩ).

#### Audio and HDMI

- **R6-ASRC1 — Still not a unique algorithm. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D323.]** Fair: the numeric tables constrain the arithmetic without picking a sample sequence. Added **complete per-frame pseudocode** — emit-then-advance ordering, phase-bit selection, history orientation (`h[0]` oldest), per-phase DC normalisation to 2¹⁷, ROM layout and endianness, startup placement, and stereo scheduling.
- **R6-ASRC2 — Coefficients "published" while §16.3 says none exists. [ACCEPTED, D324.]** The round-five generated-artefact problem in a new location. Moved to future tense.
- **R6-AUD1 — The mute cannot implement its condition. [ACCEPTED, D325.]** Two errors in one paragraph: power-good carries no APU-enable information, so it cannot satisfy a condition that names both; and a **shunt** FET failing *open* leaves audio **unmuted**, the opposite of the claimed fail-silent. Now a **series** FET with the equation `rails_good AND apu_output_enable AND delay`, and an accurate failure statement.
- **R6-AUD2 — "Exactly 48 kHz" survived in §15. [ACCEPTED, D326.]** Corrected to nominal.
- **R6-HDMI1 — The royalty arithmetic is not publicly supported. [ACCEPTED, D327.]** The ≤10,000-unit band, the $0.05 rate and the "$3–4 per console" figure are not on the public registration page; they came from secondary sources and were stated as technical facts. **All withdrawn** — only the publicly confirmable $5,000/year plus $1.00 administration fee and the ATC requirement remain, with volume and royalty arithmetic moved to a dated business model.

#### Prog8 and editorial

- **R6-SW1 — The store scan proves less than claimed. [ACCEPTED, D328.]** Correct: a static scan cannot decide `STA (zp),Y` whose destination is a runtime pointer, and prog8's own documentation notes incomplete write detection for ROM data. The guarantee is narrowed to **"no direct store resolves into the code image"**, with **emulator instrumentation that faults on out-of-range writes** added to cover the rest. Static scan plus instrumented execution is the guarantee; neither alone is.
- **R6-SW2 — The fixture contradicts the rule it validates. [ACCEPTED, D329.]** If mutable data sits in each switchable bank the fixture breaks the subset rule; if it does not, the wording exercised nothing. **Variable ownership is now stated per bank**, and what the fixture proves — copy table, cross-bank ABI, store scan — is separated from what it does not.
- **R6-ED1 — Two latch-table headers. [ACCEPTED, D330.]** An obsolete three-column header left above the new four-column one, which renders as malformed markdown. Removed.
- **R6-ED2 — "Blanking lines are entirely free". [ACCEPTED, D331.]** Contradicted by the line-224 snapshot and the line-261 prefetch. Corrected to "most blanking bandwidth", with both exceptions named.
- **R6-ED3 — 27 MHz does not divide to 148.5 MHz. [ACCEPTED, D332.]** 5.5× is a PLL ratio, not a division. Reworded, and the synthesis folded into the existing qualification gate.
- **R6-ED4 — Analog BOM function text omits RCA. [ACCEPTED, D333.]** The interface column listed it, the function text did not. All three branches now named consistently.


### M. Eighth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round7.md`, 2026-07-27)

*Provenance:* external hostile re-review of spec **v0.7**. **21 findings — 4 blockers, 12 high, 4 medium, 1 editorial group.** One capability decision was referred to the owner. Result: spec **v0.8**.

Two of the four blockers are **consequences of round six's own fix**: half-tick PHI2 edges were introduced to satisfy the CPU's pulse-width minima, and the surrounding event model was left on whole ticks. Moving an edge without moving the events that reference it is the same class of miss as correcting an equation and leaving the wiring table alone.

#### CPU and bus

- **R7-CPU1 — Half-tick edges without half-tick events. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D334.]** PHI2 was moved to fall at **3k+1.5**, but CPU completion, write sampling and event step 4 all still sat at tick **3k+2** — **23.3 ns after the edge**, against WDC's `t_DHW` = `t_AH` = 10 ns hold guarantee. Every register write, write strobe and font write was therefore timed from data the CPU was entitled to have already changed. **CPU cycle completion is now a scheduled falling-edge event**, ordered explicitly between whole ticks 3k+1 and 3k+2.
- **R7-CPU2 — The immediate VRAM read is impossible. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D335. Owner decision.]** Verified independently: from grant at 3k+1 to the edge at 3k+1.5 is **23.3 ns**, while a 10 ns SRAM plus the CPU's 15 ns `t_DSR` needs **25 ns** — and the earliest whole-tick access does not produce data until 3k+2.0, which is 23.3 ns *after* the edge. There is no arrangement in which a read completes in its own cycle. Options put to the owner were an unconditional read stall, a prefetch holding register, or a symmetric two-cycle port. **Chosen: reads always stall at least one extra cycle**, writes unaffected. The prefetch alternative is recorded as rejected — it would reverse §5.1's deliberate "true reads, no prefetch buffer" rule and introduce a staleness window against blitter writes. *Cost, stated accurately rather than dramatically:* the **bus transaction** doubles, but the 6502 cannot issue port accesses faster than one per 4 cycles, so the software-visible cost is a **read-back loop going from 9 to 10 cycles per byte, about 11 %** — and **uploads, which are writes, are unaffected**. The blitter and display fetcher are on the private bus and do not change at all. An earlier summary of this decision said read throughput "roughly halves", which described the bus and not the machine; that phrasing is withdrawn.
- **R7-CPU3 — RDY prose and grant bounds on the obsolete edge. [ACCEPTED, D336.]** "One full tick (46.6 ns) before the falling edge" became **half a tick (23.3 ns)** when the edge moved; after `t_PCS` = 15 ns that leaves **8.3 ns** for logic, pad and an open-drain transition on the PCM phase-1 path — the tightest in the machine, now stated with a fallback (defer phase-1 steals a cycle) rather than left as a waveform note. Grant bounds also tightened from 12/11 to **11/10 ticks**, since detection now precedes arbitration.
- **R7-DMA1 — Event numbers and unconditional OAM costs. [ACCEPTED, D337.]** §8.5 still cited steps 6 and 7 after the order shifted to 7 and 8. More materially, §8.5 said OAM DMA inherits all three PHI2 phases while §6.6 promised an unconditional `8N + 6`; every such figure is now marked **phase-0**, with `8N + 7` for the other two.

#### Video

- **R7-VID1 — Correct totals beside obsolete ones. [ACCEPTED, D338.]** The main table was right; the frame-wrap bullet still said "up to 1,152, leaving 213" and the benchmark paragraph still carried 49,694 / 96,734 / 120,254 and 776 / 1,511 / 1,878. All replaced with the mode-specific figures.
- **R7-VID2 — §6.6 still assigned exact fetching to Tier 2. [ACCEPTED, D339.]** The third location, missed twice. Rewritten to read from the line buffer.
- **R7-VID3 — Cursor position latching at both lines. [ACCEPTED, D340.]** Two remaining line-0 statements corrected to line 261.
- **R7-VID4 — Summaries drifting from the detailed queue. [ACCEPTED, D341.]** "Display never needs its full capacity" is false in the hi-res worst case, which needs all 1,155 slots; "every tick available on blanking lines" omitted line 261; and the queue's dependence on `VIDEO_CTRL` needed narrowing — **bit 0 does not participate**, since the fetcher runs whenever the raster runs.

#### Audio

- **R7-ASRC1 — The coefficient rule defines no unique filter. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D342.]** "Kaiser-windowed sinc, β = 8, cut-off 21.9 kHz" leaves the tap coordinate, window centre, endpoint convention, cut-off normalisation, rounding and residue tie-break all open — and the pseudocode compounded it by describing a window containing a sample "that has not arrived yet". The **literal generator formula** is now published, with the window centred at **31.5**, a stated **32-sample group delay**, and a causal history the algorithm actually has.
- **R7-ASRC2 — Rounding after the shift. [ACCEPTED, D343.]** `round_half_away(acc >> 17)` rounds nothing: the shift has already discarded the 17 fractional bits, and on a negative accumulator a shift floors where `trunc` does not. Replaced by an arithmetic `div_round_half_away` with explicit quotient/remainder.
- **R7-ASRC3 — The trim range overflows the increment. [ACCEPTED, D344.]** Verified: nominal `$FFAE8982` sits only **+1,244.6 ppm** below `2³²−1`, so the specified +2,000 ppm wraps to a near-zero increment — a catastrophic rate change from a trim. Reduced to **±1,000 ppm**, ten times the ±100 ppm the oscillators can actually differ by, keeping the increment 32-bit and `phase_carry` at 0 or 1.
- **R7-ASRC4 — Startup, underflow, overflow. [ACCEPTED, D345.]** The FIFO was primed but the *history* was not, so the first 63 frames were a real fade that the text called inaudible. Startup now **preloads the history window** before the first output, at the cost of a stated 64-frame latency. Underflow and overflow gained literal transitions, and overflow moved to the **producer** side — discarding "the oldest frame" was a consumer-pointer operation a Gray-pointer FIFO does not grant the producer.
- **R7-AUD1 — The mute equation has no pin. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D346.]** The equation referenced an "existing mute pin" that was not in the 125-signal budget, an on-die `rails_good` input that did not exist, and an external network that was not in the BOM. Resolved by putting the timer and gate **on-die**, adding a real **`AUDIO_MUTĒ` output** (oito to **126 signals**), and using **`RES̄` as `rails_good`**, which the supervisor already drives.
- **R7-AUD2 — One NMOS is not a mute. [ACCEPTED, D347.]** Correct: after the coupling capacitors the signal swings both sides of ground and a body diode conducts on one polarity. Replaced with a **`TS5A23159` bilateral switch**, placed **before the branch split** — a deliberate shared stereo mute, two poles rather than six, switching to bias rather than open so the transition is quiet.
- **R7-AUD3 — Phantom `MCLK` and "unpopulated I²S". [ACCEPTED, D348.]** The clock table still named an `MCLK` net the part has no pin for, and §8.1 still called the I²S port unpopulated while HDMI audio depends on it.

#### Parts, licensing, toolchain

- **R7-PWR1 — The OPN is transposed. [ACCEPTED, D349.]** TI orders the suffix *device, package/carrier, then fault behaviour*, so the latch-off part is **`TPS2553DBVR-1`**, not `TPS2553-1DBVR`. The transposed form resolves to the constant-current device this project has now specified incorrectly twice.
- **R7-PWR2 — Polyfuse remnants and imprecise recovery. [ACCEPTED, D350.]** Two sections still described the removed polyfuses, and "auto-retry on EN toggle at power cycle" conflated two independent reset paths.
- **R7-CART1 — Board inventory contradicts the inverter design. [ACCEPTED, D351.]** BOM item 5a fitted an inverter "whatever the board type" while the flashcart row has only a CPLD; §11.4 still said "NOR + a single NAND" and "NOR only" in adjacent sentences.
- **R7-HDMI1 — The withdrawn estimate survived in §15. [ACCEPTED, D352.]** §7.1 withdrew the $3–4 figure; §15 still stated it and cited §7.1 for it.
- **R7-SW1 — Instrumented execution is not a guarantee. [ACCEPTED, D353.]** Correct, and the §16.2 criterion was worse than imprecise — "no write target outside $0000–$3FFF" would reject every game that touches a register. Split into a **static proof**, **dynamic evidence** and a **source rule**, with MMIO explicitly permitted.
- **R7-SW2 — The validation table demands the rejected fixture. [ACCEPTED, D354.]** §16.2 still asked for mutable data in three banks, which is exactly what §11's enforced subset forbids. Aligned, and the two-pass global allocator is now marked **rejected** rather than merely superseded.


### N. Ninth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round8.md`, 2026-07-27)

*Provenance:* external hostile re-review of spec **v0.8**. **19 findings — 5 blockers, 8 high, 5 medium, 1 editorial group.** One capability decision was referred to the owner, and it *restored* a capability rather than removing one. Result: spec **v0.9**.

**This round's headline is that the reviewer computed something the project had only asserted.** The ASRC coefficient formula published in v0.8 was evaluated across all 64 quantised phases and found to miss its own stopband requirement. That was reproduced here exactly before being accepted.

#### CPU and VRAM

- **R8-CPU1 — The address-timing model uses the wrong edge and the wrong voltage column. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D355.]** `t_ADS` is referenced to the PHI2 **falling** edge, not the rising one, and is **40 ns at 3.3 V** rather than the 5 V column's 30 ns. v0.7 put the address 0.64 ticks *after the rise*; it is in fact valid at **tick 3k+0.86 measured from the fall that starts the cycle**, long before the rise. The whole bus model is rebuilt falling-edge to falling-edge.

 **The consequence is large and welcome.** The decode-to-RDY budget goes from **24.8 ns to 84.7 ns**, and the same-cycle VRAM read that v0.7 declared impossible **closes with +21.6 ns of margin**. Since the owner had chosen the unconditional two-cycle stall *on the strength of that impossibility claim*, the decision was put back to them with the corrected arithmetic; they chose to **restore the single-cycle read**. Read-back loops return to 9 cycles per byte and the 11 % penalty disappears. *Recorded prominently: a capability was removed for two revisions because a datasheet parameter was read from the wrong reference edge.*
- **R8-CPU2 / R8-CPU3 / R8-CPU4 — Write grant, posted-write semantics, read eligibility. [ACCEPTED, D356.]** With the sampling edge now defined, a write is captured there and committed later — a genuine posted write, which v0.8 implied without specifying. Now stated: **one buffer entry**, the CPU-visible pointer increments **at capture** (so "exactly once per successful access" counts captures), `VRAM_ADDR*` writes affect only later accesses, **a read waits behind a pending write** so the port is strictly ordered, and the pending write takes ordinary CPU-port priority. Read requests become pending at the edge retiring the *preceding* cycle, which is what lets the data be fetched during the read's own cycle.

#### DMA

- **R8-DMA1 — Phase-1 PCM behaviour was left to a future waveform. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D357.]** Correct in principle and in consequence: the choice moves CPU progress relative to the raster, so it is Tier-1 observable, and two emulators following the text could place the same MMIO write on different scanlines. **Phase 1 is frozen as deferring, like phase 2.** If the bench later shows it could have caught the edge, that is a machine-version change.
- **R8-DMA2 — OAM DMA cannot have three start phases. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D358.]** A sharp observation. PCM refills arise on free-running mix ticks, so `m mod 3` genuinely cycles; **OAM DMA is triggered by a CPU write, and every CPU write retires on the same falling edge**, so it has exactly one phase. The `8N + 7` row was describing an event that cannot occur, and is deleted — a transfer always costs `8N + 6` on a blanking line.
- **R8-DMA3 — CPU-port versus OAM-DMA priority. [ACCEPTED, D359.]** A posted CPU write pending when a DMA starts now **commits first**, and only one VRAM transfer is charged to any tick, so the step-3 grant and the step-6 DMA byte are the same slot rather than two.

#### ASRC

- **R8-ASRC1 — The literal formula fails its own stopband. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D360.]** Reproduced exactly: **−72.8 dB at the worst quantised phase (32)** against a stated ≥80 dB. Two causes, both instructive. The target came from a **Kaiser order estimate**, which predicts a single-rate filter's stopband and is not a measurement of the polyphase set. And the check that would have caught it was never run — **phase 0 alone measures −96.3 dB**, because it is symmetric with a zero at Nyquist, so verifying the obvious phase would have shown a comfortable pass while phase 32 sat 23 dB worse.

 The stopband **edge** was also wrong: "≥80 dB from 23.97 kHz" names the input Nyquist, a single point, and the meaningful edge is `f_in − 20 kHz` = **27,940 Hz**, the first image of the protected band. Restated.

 **The filter is not redesigned here.** Several attempts at a replacement produced materially different stopband figures depending on how the measurement was formulated, which means the measurement methodology is not yet trustworthy — and publishing a new number obtained that way would repeat the original error with more confidence. The coefficient design is therefore promoted from a test artefact to a **blocking deliverable**, with the *requirement* and the *measurement method* normative and the table's figures labelled as targets.
- **R8-ASRC2 — Two mutually exclusive startup descriptions. [ACCEPTED, D361.]** The servo table said 16 FIFO frames and a zero history; the later bullet said a 64-frame history preload. Replaced by one counted state machine: **80 frames received, first 64 to history, 16 retained**, output and servo enabled together.
- **R8-ASRC3 — Underflow tested after the emit it replaces. [ACCEPTED, D362.]** The emit-first pseudocode would have needed to un-emit a frame. The test is now **prospective**: compute whether the phase addition would carry, and if it would with an empty FIFO, repeat the previous frame and change nothing.
- **R8-ASRC4 — "IEEE double" is not a bitstream oracle. [ACCEPTED, D363.]** Correct: IEEE-754 fixes formats, not `sin`, `sqrt` or a Bessel `I0` "summed to convergence". Generation moves to a stated 50-digit context, and **the committed generator and its ROM hash are normative**; the formula explains the design.
- **R8-ASRC5 — Group delay presented as exact. [ACCEPTED, D364.]** An even 64-tap kernel centres at 31.5 + p/64, so "32 samples" is a rounded convention, and it is **inside** the startup window rather than added to it.

#### Analog and editorial

- **R8-AUD1 — The mute has a part and a pin but no contract. [ACCEPTED, D365.]** The signal was drawn `AUDIO_MUTĒ` while its equation produced 1 when muted; renamed **`AUDIO_MUTE`**, active high. Added a truth table from `RES̄`, `AUDIO_CTRL`.1 and the timer through to COM↔NC/NO connectivity, both switch control inputs tied, a **100 kΩ pull-up guaranteeing the muted path whenever oito is not driving**, a reset-table entry, and timer restart on brownout.
- **R8-AUD2 — Switch placement ambiguous. [ACCEPTED, D366.]** The signal order is now explicit: driver and filter at mid-rail → SPDT → branch split → one coupling capacitor per branch → connector. The switch sits **before** the capacitors, where the signal is still within its single-supply range.
- **R8-VID1 / R8-VID2 / R8-VID3 — Latch and font wording. [ACCEPTED, D367.]** The last line-0 cursor statement removed; the latch table's fourth row labelled an explanatory sub-row of *per-frame* rather than a fourth class; and font visibility restated **by ordered event time** rather than "same cycle is write-first", which was broader than the half-tick ordering supports.
- **R8-PWR1 — "Fuse/PTC" against "no polyfuses". [ACCEPTED, D368.]** The blanket statement excluded the very part the input section wanted. Narrowed to **output rails**, with an exact input PTC named and added to the ledger.
- **R8-EDIT1 — Stale replacement text. [ACCEPTED, D369.]** A 12-tick waveform request after the bound became 11/10, a sentence naming `TPS2553DBVR-1` where it meant the non-`-1` variant, and a "mute FETs" deliverable after the switch was chosen.


### O. Tenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round9.md`, 2026-07-28)

*Provenance:* external hostile re-review of spec **v0.9**. **16 findings — 6 blockers, 8 high, 2 medium.** Result: spec **v0.10**.

**The most important finding is aimed at the verification tooling, not the specification.** R9-TOOL1 observes that the verifier built one round earlier reported success while seven contradictions sat in the documents. That is worse than having no checker, because it converts an unexamined document into an apparently examined one — and the README said so in as many words.

- **R9-TOOL1 — The verifier gives false assurance. [ACCEPTED, D370.]** Confirmed by running it: it passed while the ledger still said `t_ADS` = 30 ns, the README still said 0.8 against a 0.9 spec, the model still printed the deleted `8N + 7` case and the rejected filter's attenuation, §16.2 still demanded an obsolete 24.8 ns budget, a line-0 cursor statement survived, and an invented part sat in the ledger. **The cause is structural**: every model check asked whether a desired value was *present*, never whether a superseded one was *absent*; and the parts check tested that a URL field was non-empty, not that the URL established anything. Fixed by adding **banned numeric values**, **cross-document version equality**, a **rejection of family landing pages** as evidence for an orderable suffix, and a distinction between *unverified and admitted* and *unverified and unnoticed*. The model no longer carries the rejected filter or the deleted DMA case at all. **The README's claim that the tool prevents a drifting spec from being published is replaced with a statement of what it does not catch.**

 *The lesson generalises past this project: a verification tool inherits the discipline of whoever writes its assertions, and mine encoded optimism. It found the marketing page's stale package name, which was real, and that success made it look trustworthy on everything it was silently not checking.*

#### CPU and VRAM

- **R9-CPU1 — Two incompatible PHI2 schedules. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D371.]** v0.9 corrected the edge convention in the port subsection and left the global scheduler sampling at 3k+1.5, so the event order, font visibility, register writes, IRQ ordering and the PCM phase table all still referenced the withdrawn model. Unified on **integer falling edges**, with the CPU sample-and-retire ordered explicitly as **step 0 of every third tick** — a textual insert in one subsection was not enough.
- **R9-CPU2 — The single-cycle read cannot identify its own cycle. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D372.]** The decisive observation. v0.9's +21.6 ns margin measured only the **data return**; the **request** path has just **6.6 ns** between address validity at 3k+0.86 and the tick-1 slot, for input pad, `$4044` decode, synchroniser and arbitration. `t_ADS` constrains the CPU's outputs, not oito's decoded selects, and the waveform table had listed both at the same instant. **The read returns to two cycles** — capture at tick 3k+2 (53.1 ns of path) and 68.1 ns of return margin. *This reverses the round-8 restoration, which reversed the round-7 decision: I have now moved this behaviour twice on incomplete timing analysis, and the settled answer is the deterministic one.*
- **R9-CPU3 — Read-back promised from write-only registers. [ACCEPTED, D373.]** The posted-write table said `VRAM_ADDR*` "reads back the post-increment value", contradicting §5.1's write-only classification and the reason the runtime keeps a ZP shadow. Reworded to increment the *internal* pointer.
- **R9-CPU4 — §16.2 still gates on withdrawn paths. [ACCEPTED, D374.]** It demanded silicon validate the obsolete **24.8 ns** budget and the **8.3 ns phase-1 PCM path** that §8.5 deliberately rejects — a live freeze gate that could have rejected correct RTL, or reinstated phase-dependent behaviour after tape-out.

#### DMA and video

- **R9-DMA1 — OAM DMA has one phase in §6.6 and three in §8.5. [ACCEPTED, D375.]** The waveform deliverable also still requested all three, and the model still printed `1030/1031`. All three removed.
- **R9-VID1 — The last line-0 cursor statement. [ACCEPTED, D376.]** Fourth round of chasing this; the survivor was in the font-RAM paragraph.

#### ASRC

- **R9-ASRC1 — The rejected design is still normative. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D377.]** Sharp: v0.9 said tap count, window, cut-off and width were design *outputs*, while the same section still mandated 64 phases × 64 taps, an 18-bit 4,096-entry ROM, 64-sample histories, top-six-bit phase selection and 64-tap pseudocode. An RTL author could not tell whether to build that machine or wait. Filter-dependent dimensions are now parameterised as **`P` and `T`**, the 64×64 set is labelled a **rejected baseline**, and startup is expressed as `T + 16`.
- **R9-ASRC2 — Two stopband edges, and an unreproducible method. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D378.]** The table said `f_in − 20 kHz` and the next paragraph said the input Nyquist. Worse, "every quantised phase evaluated on the assembled prototype" conflates two different responses — a phase branch runs at `f_in` and has no 27.94 kHz to measure, while the interleaved prototype has *one* response, not `P`. **That is exactly why my three attempts at measuring the rejected design produced three different numbers.** Replaced with a five-part executable acceptance definition.
- **R9-ASRC3 / R9-ASRC4 — Duplicate startup rules and a scalar routine for a stereo problem. [ACCEPTED, D379.]** The servo table and the later state machine both remained normative; and the pseudocode returned one sample while advancing the shared phase, so calling it per channel advanced twice. Now one `stereo_frame()` with a **prospective** underflow test.
- **R9-ASRC5 — The group-delay sign is wrong. [ACCEPTED, D380.]** With `hist[0]` oldest and the peak at `i = (T−1)/2 + p/P`, the delay is `(T−1)/2 − p/P` — decreasing with phase, which agrees with the prose that increasing phase interpolates *forward*. v0.9 wrote `+ p/P`.

#### Analog and power

- **R9-AUD1 — The mute truth table is inverted. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D381.]** TI's `TS5A23159` connects **NC↔COM when `IN` is low**; the spec asserted the opposite. With the published wiring, reset and brownout would have selected the **signal** path and the console would have come up **unmuted**, while the nominal playing state selected bias and was silent. Renamed **`AUDIO_EN`**, active high for play, with the table taken from the datasheet.
- **R9-AUD2 — A pull-up to a rail that may be unpowered. [ACCEPTED, D382.]** 3.3 V derives from 5 V, so the switch can be powered while the pull-up's rail is at 0 V — invalid exactly when it is needed, and a possible back-power path. Replaced with a **100 kΩ pull-down to ground**, which is valid in every rail combination.
- **R9-AUD3 — A reset-table entry that does not exist. [ACCEPTED, D383.]** §8.4 claimed the pin was added to §5.1's table, which covers registers only. Moved to a signal reset table in §6.3.
- **R9-PWR1 — The input PTC does not exist. [ACCEPTED — blocker, D384.]** `0ZCJ0150FF2E` is not in Bel's series; the nearest real member is `0ZCJ0150FF2C` at **8 V max**, unusable on a 12 V input. **I invented it, and then entered it in the parts ledger — the artefact built specifically to prevent invented parts — with a family landing page as its source and a "checked" date that recorded no reading.** No replacement is named: the criteria are stated and the part is explicitly unselected, alongside the reverse-polarity topology and jack TVS. The ledger now separates parts whose *exact suffix* has been read from those whose source is a family page; **21 of them are in the latter group**, and they are enumerated in §16.2 rather than left to look verified.

### P. Deferral decision — the HDMI audio resampler (2026-07-28)

- **D385 — The ASRC coefficient design is deferred, not dropped.** Owner decision, taken after the round-nine disposition established that the published filter fails its own stopband and that I could not measure a replacement consistently enough to trust one. The decision is sound: the work is bounded, well-understood in general, and blocks a narrow slice of the project.

 **What made the deferral safe rather than merely convenient** was writing down four things in §16.2.1 that a deferred item usually lacks:

 1. **Scope.** It blocks **oito RTL freeze and sound over HDMI**. It does *not* block the emulator (the golden vector is taken upstream of the resampler), the SDK, the board schematic (the resampler is on-die; the board carries three fixed I²S wires), the analog outputs, or the HDMI picture.
 2. **Why it blocks the ASIC.** Tap count, phase count and coefficient width are silicon — ROM size, multiplier width, history registers — so **oito's die area carries an unbounded item** and no area or cost figure is final until the filter exists.
 3. **A fallback.** If it is not solved before tape-out: leave the audio path unpopulated, HDMI carries video only, analog audio unaffected. One line in a configuration image, no silicon. *This is the v0.8 position, i.e. exactly the limitation that motivated the HDMI conversion — a fallback, not a target.*
 4. **A definition of done**, including the specific instruction to measure **every phase** rather than phase 0, which is the mistake that let the first design look correct.

 **Mechanically enforced.** `tools-model.py` prints a standing `DEFERRED, OWED` notice on every run, and `tools-verify.py` **fails the build** if §16.2.1 is removed or if the specification ever claims the filter is designed while the model's `asrc_designed` flag is false. Both guards were tested by deleting the record and by inserting a false completion claim. *The point is not ceremony: an unenforced deferral is indistinguishable from an oversight three months later.*

### Q. Eleventh adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round10.md`, 2026-07-28)

Eleven findings, four of them blockers. The pattern this round is narrower and more uncomfortable than usual: **almost every finding is a repair that was made in one place and not carried to its dependants.** v0.11 fixed the PHI2 origin, withdrew the one-cycle read, deferred the ASRC and corrected the mute polarity — and each of those fixes left a contradiction standing somewhere downstream.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D386** | **R10-CPU1 [blocker] — two incompatible read-request rules.** §6.5's new table captures the request at tick 3k+2; a paragraph below still said the access becomes pending at "the sampling edge that retires its CPU cycle — for a read, the edge of the *preceding* cycle." That is the **withdrawn one-cycle mechanism**, left in place when its timing table was replaced. The global step-0 rule compounded it by saying the cycle "retires" at every falling edge, which cannot be true while `RDY` is low. Four incompatible implementations were open. | **Accepted.** §6.5 now publishes **one read state machine** as an eight-row transition list: the request latch sets **once** at step 2 of tick 3k+2 and re-detection is **suppressed** while it is set; `RDY` goes low by 3k+2.68; the edge at 3k+3 **does not retire** the cycle, which is **held** with address and direction unchanged; the latch **clears at grant**; `RDY` is released `t_PCS` before the first edge at or after data validity; retirement happens at the **first falling edge with `RDY` high**. §6.5's step-0 text now reads *"retires only if `RDY` was high at this edge"* and states that **sample, retire and hold are three events, not one**. Writes keep their own, different rule: captured at the retiring edge, one cycle. |
| **D387** | **R10-DMA1 [blocker] — the PCM phase table still used the deleted half-tick edges.** §8.5 said "PHI2 falls at integer ticks 3k" and then, in the next table, gave the next edges as *m*+1.5, *m*+0.5 and *m*−0.5. From those it derived an **8.3 ns** phase-1 budget, a "phase 2 has already missed the edge" case, and a **normative freeze making phases 1 and 2 defer** — plus a §16.2 qualification gate and a line in `tools-model.py`. | **Accepted, and this is a real arithmetic correction rather than a rewording.** With integer edges the next falling edge after step 8 of tick *m* is always `3 × (⌊m/3⌋ + 1)`, giving **3 / 2 / 1 ticks** for `m mod 3` = 0 / 1 / 2 and **124.7 / 78.1 / 31.6 ns** after `t_PCS`. So **no phase misses its edge**, every phase halts the CPU immediately after the cycle in progress, the halt rule is one expression instead of three cases, and the **shortest path is phase 2 at 31.6 ns — not phase 1 at 8.3 ns**. The steal begins **2–4 ticks** after the mix tick, not 1–4. The 8.3 ns figure, the deferral freeze and the phase-1 gate are withdrawn **together**, since every one of them was a conclusion drawn from that number. `tools-model.py` now derives all three rows from one expression and the verifier compares each. *The reviewer's arithmetic was correct and mine had been wrong for three revisions; what let it survive is that no tool computed it.* |
| **D388** | **R10-ASRC1 [blocker] — the deferred filter was simultaneously absent, failed-but-normative, and fixed-width.** `P` and `T` were declared design outputs while the pseudocode indexed `ROM[ph*64 .. ph*64+63]` and `hist[63]`, the arithmetic stayed at `16 × 18 → 34` with a 40-bit accumulator, phase selection used `log₂P` without requiring `P` to be a power of two, and a conformance bullet said an implementer "can build to this text" two paragraphs after another said no filter is specified. | **Accepted.** The rejected set moves wholesale into **§8.4.1, headed NON-NORMATIVE**, with an explicit "it must not be implemented" and the note that every literal in it — 64 phases, 64 taps, 18 bits, `2**17`, the 31.5 centre — belongs to that set alone. §8.4 keeps only parameterised statements: **`P` MUST be a power of two** (a stated constraint on the design output, since phase selection is a bit-field truncation), `ROM[ph*T .. ph*T+T−1]`, `hist[T−1]`, products `16 × W → 16+W`, accumulator **≥ `15 + W + ⌈log₂T⌉`** bits, and a **33-bit temporary** for `phase + increment` with the carry from bit 32. The general rules that survive rejection are separated into **§8.4.2, NORMATIVE**. The conformance bullet now says plainly that **an implementer cannot build the resampler from this document** — not merely that they cannot check it. |
| **D389** | **R10-ASRC2 [blocker] — the freeze gate reinstated the measurement method §8.4 calls invalid.** §8.4 explains that a per-phase branch runs at `f_in`, so 27,940 Hz is above *its* Nyquist, and that the assembled prototype has one response rather than `P` of them — then a paragraph above, plus §16.2, plus §16.2.1's definition of done, all still demanded "every one of the 64 quantised phases, evaluated on the assembled polyphase prototype." | **Accepted, and worth being precise about what was wrong.** The instinct was right — checking phase 0 alone is exactly what hid the −72.8 dB — but the wording asked for a **stopband figure per phase on the assembled prototype**, a question neither object answers. The **five-part procedure is now the only acceptance contract**, restated as a table naming the object under test for each part: the assembled prototype gets **one** reconstruction response, each branch gets passband and delay error over **only representable frequencies**, the resampler gets end-to-end images/aliases/spurs/THD+N at both trim corners, and the grid gets an inter-point bound. All three copies of the old criterion are replaced, and the correction is **not** a softening: part 2 tests the whole set and part 3 tests every branch. |
| **D390** | **R10-ASRC3 [high] — startup required more storage than the FIFO has.** The sequence was: leave the FIFO empty, accept until `T + 16` frames have been *received*, then move the first `T` to history. A 32-frame FIFO cannot hold 80, so it worked only for `T ≤ 16` — and the rejected baseline had `T = 64`. | **Accepted.** The prefill becomes a **cross-domain state machine that drains as it fills**: while muted the consumer pops each arriving frame straight into the history shift register until `n = T`, **then stops popping** until FIFO occupancy reaches 16, then enables output and the servo together. Peak simultaneous storage is `T` history registers plus 16 FIFO frames, and **the history registers already exist** — so it is realisable for any `T`. Pointer movement, history order during prefill, and the behaviour if the input pauses (wait, stay muted, do not fault) are stated, because a partial window is worse than silence. |
| **D391** | **R10-CART1 [high] — one paragraph, three incompatible board populations.** §11.4 still read "non-saving carts … carry NOR + a single NAND. A saving cart is NOR + FRAM; a plain cart is NOR only" — after the same document proves the NAND unbuildable and the BOM requires one inverter per cartridge. | **Accepted.** Every discrete-memory cartridge carries the shared `74LVC1G04GV`: **plain retail is NOR + inverter, retail-with-save is NOR + FRAM + inverter**, non-saving carts leave the save contacts NC. The verifier now fails on `NAND` in any live cartridge description. |
| **D392** | **R10-AUD1 [medium] — `AUDIO_EN` cited a table that did not exist.** Its pad paragraph pointed at "the signal reset table of §6.3"; §6.3 held a signal *count* and a promised 176-pin deliverable. | **Accepted, by writing the table rather than the cross-reference.** §6.3 gains a **signal reset-state table** covering `AUDIO_EN`, `IRQ̄`, `RDY`, `BE`, the cartridge selects, `SELECT` and the PS/2 gate outputs, each through *rail invalid / reset / after reset*, with the board guarantee that holds it. The shared rule is stated: **the passive state is the inactive one**, so a pad that is high-Z for want of a rail behaves like one deliberately deasserted. *"It is in the pin table" is not a record while the pin table is a deliverable.* |
| **D393** | **R10-DOC1 [high] — the page advertised as normative was three versions stale.** `re8-console-spec.html` identified itself as **v0.8** with the half-tick CPU model, the old PCM behaviour and the fixed failed ASRC, while the Markdown was v0.11. `build-publish.py` did not regenerate it — it copied it, and if it were missing merely asked the operator to run the generator. The verifier did not include it in `DOCS` and checked versions only against README. **A clean successful publish shipped the v0.8 contract.** | **Accepted.** Markdown is the sole editable normative source: `build-publish.py` now **generates the HTML first, verifies second, publishes third**, so what is checked is what ships. The generated page joins `DOCS`, and a **HTML check** fails the build if its version differs from the Markdown's. Regenerating it immediately surfaced ten superseded values that had been sitting in the published contract. |
| **D394** | **R10-TOOL1 [high] — the verifier reported success while encoding or overlooking six live contradictions**, and its own docstring claimed it checked "every document in the repo" while omitting the normative HTML. Its supersession exemption matched phrases like "not the" and "earlier revision" **anywhere in a line**, and a Markdown paragraph is one line — so a single "…is deleted" clause could excuse a live requirement in the same paragraph. | **Accepted, and this is the finding that mattered most.** A new **STRUCT** check tests the *shape* of a claim rather than the presence of a string: one PHI2 origin (half-tick edges banned outside history), one read-capture rule, RDY-held retirement distinguished from sampling, fixed-64 ASRC operations confined to the marked rejected block, no NAND in a live cartridge population, the withdrawn per-phase acceptance rule blocked from returning, the impossible `T + 16` startup blocked from returning, the front-page list equal to §16.2, and the §16.2 parts queues generated from the ledger. The exemption regex is **scoped to sentences and table cells**, not lines, and no longer matches bare phrases like "not the". **Every one of these guards was tested by reintroducing the defect it targets**; two of them did not fire on the first attempt and were fixed. The docstring now describes what it actually checks. |
| **D395** | **R10-PART1 [medium] — the "21 family pages" gate misclassified exact part pages.** `THS7374IPW` and `OPA3691ID` already pointed at TI **part-details** pages naming the exact suffix, package, status and carrier; `TPS2553DBVR-1` and `FM18W08-SG` likewise. One boolean, `verified_part_specific`, was carrying exact-suffix identity, electrical verification and lifecycle at once, so the count and the reason were both wrong and the list could not be used as a review queue. | **Accepted.** Four orthogonal fields replace it: **`opn_complete`** (is `part` an orderable number at all?), **`suffix_evidence`** (`exact-page` / `datasheet` / `family-page`), **`electrical_evidence`**, **`lifecycle_evidence`**. §16.2 now carries **three** generated rows instead of one hand-typed list — **6 incomplete orderable numbers** (a worse state the old flag hid: no suffix has been *chosen*), **17 family-page sources**, and **6 datasheets whose ordering table has not been matched to the suffix**. `lifecycle_evidence` is `none` for **every** part, stated uniformly rather than inferred from URL shape. The verifier fails if any queue drifts from the ledger, and checks membership **inside §16.2** — a whole-document search could never catch a drop, since all these parts are named elsewhere. |
| **D396** | **R10-STATUS1 [medium] — the front-page "explicitly outstanding" table omitted known blockers**, including the ASRC filter that §16.2.1 calls a tape-out blocker, and its status date said 2026-07-27 on a v0.11 document. | **Accepted.** The list is now **complete and generated from §16.2**, with the deferred ASRC blocker appended and labelled, and the verifier fails on drift. *A deferred blocker vanishing from the top-level summary is precisely the failure §16.2.1 was created to prevent — so it happening in the same revision that created §16.2.1 is the strongest argument for the mechanical check.* The status date is corrected. |

**What round ten says about the process.** Nine of the eleven findings are one repair not carried to its dependants — the same class as rounds eight and nine, and the class the model/verifier triad was built to catch. It caught none of them, for a reason worth recording: **the tooling checked the values I had thought to enumerate, and every one of these defects was a second rule sitting next to a correct one.** Presence and absence are both substring tests. The structural checks added here are the first that ask whether the document contains *two* answers to the same question, and the parts and status queues are the first figures generated from data rather than typed and then guarded.

### R. Twelfth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round11.md`, 2026-07-28)

Ten findings, five of them blockers. The characteristic defect this round is different from round ten's and worth naming: **repairs that stopped one transition short.** The read state machine, the ASRC deferral and the prog8 build model were each rewritten correctly and each left a dangling edge — a latch cleared too early, a formula still called normative, a rejected pass still enumerated. Round ten's findings were *two rules side by side*; round eleven's are *one rule that does not reach the end*.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D397** | **R11-DMA1 [blocker] — a low-`RDY` cycle is held, not completed, and repeated MMIO side effects were undefined.** §6.5 correctly said a held cycle re-presents itself, while §8.3 and §6.6 still said the in-flight cycle "finishes" and "completes". Since every register write and write strobe is timed from step 0, and a PCM steal can halt *any* CPU access, one access could pop two `KBD_SCAN` bytes, clear `MOUSE_STATUS` or an APU `$A5` flag the CPU never saw, post-increment `AUDIO_DATA` twice, or emit two external write strobes. | **Accepted, and answered with one rule rather than a list of cases.** §6.5 defines **`cpu_transfer_accept ≡ PHI2 falling edge AND `RDY` pin high at that edge`**, and *everything* with a side effect hangs off it: register writes, `RAM_W̄Ē`/`BOOT_W̄Ē`/`CART_W̄Ē` strobes, font-RAM writes, FIFO pops, clear-on-read, W1C, pointer auto-increment, and retirement itself. It is the **pin**, not oito's intent — `RDY` is open-drain and the debug probe pulls it too, so qualifying on an internal request signal would accept a transaction the probe was holding. **Nothing needs to be predicted**: the document already timed every strobe from step 0, so acceptance is that same event with the qualification retirement had just acquired. Reads re-drive the same value across held edges and commit only at acceptance. §16.2 gains a five-shape trace gate — ordinary read, side-effecting read, ordinary write, MMIO/command write, and `BE` takeover — plus the abort path for `RES̄`, probe ownership and debug entry. |
| **D398** | **R11-CPU1 [blocker] — the read latch cleared at grant, re-arming the still-held access.** The state machine suppressed re-detection *while the latch is set* and then cleared the latch at grant — but the CPU keeps presenting `$4044` until retirement three ticks later, so step 2 of the next tick would see an unsuppressed read and enqueue it again: **two VRAM reads and two pointer increments for one instruction.** | **Accepted.** The transaction now carries **two** pieces of state: a **`pending`** latch that arbitration consumes, and a **`serviced`** flag set at grant and cleared only at acceptance. Detection is suppressed while **either** is set, so the suppressing term outlives the grant. *The lesson is small and general: "the latch sets once" is not a mechanism, it is a hope, unless something enforces it for the whole life of the transaction.* |
| **D399** | **R11-CPU2 [blocker] — "grant on the same tick" and "earliest grant at 3k+3" could not both be true.** The global event order puts detection at step 2 and arbitration at step 3 *specifically* so a request raised on a tick can be granted on that tick; the read table nevertheless gave the first arbitrated slot as 3k+3, one tick later. The 11/10-tick request-to-grant bound depends on same-tick eligibility. | **Accepted in favour of the event order**, which is the older and more load-bearing rule. The first arbitrated slot is **3k+2**, the capture tick itself. **This does not restore a one-cycle read** — retiring at 3k+3 would need data by 3k+2.68 and it arrives at 3k+3.21 — so the read stays two cycles and the binding constraint stays the request path; what changes is the return margin, **114.7 ns** rather than 68.1. `tools-model.py` now derives the slot from the capture tick instead of carrying it as a literal. Two stale remnants went with it: event-order step 4's "CPU completion is not a whole-tick event", and the unqualified "retires the cycle" in the bus-cycle paragraph. |
| **D400** | **R11-ASRC1 [blocker] — the live table still named the rejected formula as the current oracle.** §8.4's coefficient-generation row said "the literal formula below … what is normative today is the formula, not a file", and the formula below is in §8.4.1, headed **NON-NORMATIVE** and "must not be implemented". An implementer was told both at once. | **Accepted.** The row now states **requirements on the generator that does not exist yet** — arbitrary-precision context, quantise once, explicit window/cut-off/normalisation, deterministic residue tie-break, coefficient range check against `W`, committed ROM and SHA-256 — and names no formula as an oracle. *The parameterisation repair in v0.12 fixed the dimensions and left the generation row pointing at the thing it had just quarantined.* |
| **D401** | **R11-ASRC2 [high] — the published accumulator bound was one bit short at its own stated corner.** `15 + W + ⌈log₂T⌉` signed bits does not hold: the largest *positive* product is `(−2¹⁵)(−2^(W−1)) = 2^(W+14)`, so `T` of them sum to exactly `2^(W+14+log₂T)`, while a signed `N`-bit accumulator reaches only `2^(N−1)−1`. The bound is hit **exactly**, and the formula is short **for every power-of-two `T`** — which is the case a `P`-and-`T` design lands on. | **Accepted; recomputed and confirmed.** The bound is **`⌈log₂(T × 2^(W+14) + 1)⌉ + 1`**, equivalently **`W + 16 + ⌊log₂T⌋`**. For the rejected baseline that is **40 bits — the exact minimum**, so v0.12's parenthetical that 40 bits had "one to spare" was also wrong and is corrected. Checked against the true minimum for four (W, T) pairs, including a non-power-of-two where the old formula happened to be right, which is why it survived. |
| **D402** | **R11-ASRC3 [high] — the document invalidated the −72.8 dB measurement and kept using it as proof of failure.** §8.4 calls per-phase stopband measurement on the assembled prototype a category error; §8.4.1, §16.2 and §16.2.1 still said the baseline "failed its own ≥80 dB stopband (worst phase −72.8 dB)". | **Accepted, and this is the sharpest finding of the round.** A number obtained from a withdrawn measurement cannot be evidence under the procedure that replaced it. The baseline is now rejected because it has **never been validated under the five-part procedure** — full stop. The −72.8 dB is labelled a historical result under a superseded formulation and the thing that triggered the methodology review, alongside the fact that **three formulations gave −72.8, −25.7 and −45.3 dB with no coefficient changing** — which is *why* "not validated" is the honest verdict and "failed" is not. Applied in §8.4.1, §16.2, §16.2.1 and the model's comments. The verifier now fails on any "failed its own ≥80 dB" claim, **deliberately without an exemption**, since no wording makes that assertion correct while the measurement stands withdrawn. |
| **D403** | **R11-SW1 [blocker] — the prog8 allocator was both rejected and active, and the initialised-data transformation was at a stage that cannot perform it.** One paragraph called the two-pass global allocator rejected and then said "the build therefore runs in two passes", enumerating them normatively. §16.2 carried two incompatible gates — a fixture requiring *all* mutable state in the fixed bank, and an allocation gate requiring three banks *with mutable initialised data*. Separately, a **post-link packer cannot give an initialised array a RAM home**: `prog8c` and 64tass have already emitted every reference against the ROM label, and no post-link step can rewrite absolute references it cannot identify. | **Accepted on both counts.** The build is **one compilation per bank; there is no allocation pass**, and the rejected mechanism is kept only as the recorded reason. The contradictory §16.2 allocation gate is replaced by an **initialised-data transformation** gate. The transformation moves to the only stage that can rewrite addresses — a **pre-compile source transformation** emitting address-bound declarations at RAM homes, a separate ROM blob and a copy table, with `prog8c` run once on the transformed source. *Upstream documentation confirms initialised arrays stay in program memory under `%option romable`; it never offered the RAM-copy transformation this document was claiming from it.* |
| **D404** | **R11-HW1 [high] — direct 3.3 V I²S into the CH7035B is not electrically established.** The brief datasheet gives pin 56 a **0–2.5 V** signal level and publishes no I²S `V_IH`/`V_IL` or absolute maxima for pins 56–58; `VDDIO = 3.3 V` covers the parallel-video bank. The spec called the three wires "fixed". | **Accepted as an open gate, not papered over.** The **protocol** on those wires is fixed; the **voltage domain is not**, and closing it may require a three-channel level translator or a compatible oito pad domain — either of which changes the BOM and the pin/power budget. Added to §16.2, and §16.2.1's "board schematic capture unblocked" row is scoped to mean *unblocked by the deferral*, not that the board is otherwise closed. *Ambiguity in a brief datasheet is not evidence of safety, and this project has already shipped one invented part by treating absence of contradiction as confirmation.* |
| **D405** | **R11-HW2 [medium] — the new reset table said an open-drain `BE` pad drives high.** It cannot; and the wired-AND with the debug probe depends on it never doing so. | **Accepted.** `BE` is **released (high-Z) and reads high through its pull-up**, pulled low only during oito or probe bus-mastering. *A table added in v0.12 to record safe passive states contained a row that described an impossible drive — worth noting, because the table's whole premise is that the passive state is the safe one.* |
| **D406** | **R11-TOOL1 [medium] — the model's closing line claimed project-wide status it had no basis for**: "Blocks oito RTL freeze and HDMI audio.
