Everything else is unblocked." §16.2 carries dozens of independent gates on schematic freeze and tape-out. | **Accepted.** The notice now says the ASRC **specifically** blocks those two activities and **counts the remaining §16.2 gates from the specification itself**, so the number cannot drift and the sentence cannot quietly become false. |

**One defect was found in the verifier by its own break test.** The sentence splitter added in round ten did not fire on `…same paragraph.* **The build…`, because the character before the space is `*` rather than the full stop — so an exempt clause still swallowed the following live requirement, which is precisely the shape `segments()` exists to catch. Fixed to allow emphasis markers between the punctuation and the space. **This was found only because each new guard is tested by reintroducing the defect it targets**; two guards passed a silently ineffective test before that, and the pattern is now worth stating as a rule: *a guard that has never been observed to fail is not evidence of anything.*

### S. Thirteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round12.md`, 2026-07-28)

Eight findings, four blockers. Round twelve's shape is different again: **round eleven's `cpu_transfer_accept` rule was correct and the document then asked for events that rule makes impossible.** A definition was added without auditing what it invalidated.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D407** | **R12-BUS1 [blocker] — the takeover sequence waited for an acceptance that `RDY` low makes impossible.** §6.5 defines acceptance as a falling edge with `RDY` **high**; §6.6, §8.3 and §8.5 then told bus masters to assert `RDY` low and "let the in-flight cycle reach its acceptance edge". With `RDY` low that edge is false by definition. §6.5 also said resumption begins "a fresh transaction", though WDC resumes the same held internal state. | **Accepted.** Three edges are now named separately — **sample edge** (every fall), **hold edge** (`RDY` low: held, re-presented, no side effect), **acceptance edge** (`RDY` high: `cpu_transfer_accept`) — and every bus-master sequence says *hold edge*. A **preserve/abort table** covers PCM, OAM DMA, probe ownership, debug entry and reset: only **`RES̄` aborts**. *An earlier revision had probe ownership and debug entry clearing `pending`/`serviced`, which would have forced the same CPU access to be serviced twice — the exact duplicate `serviced` was introduced to prevent.* "A fresh transaction" is withdrawn: it is the same transaction finally reaching acceptance. |
| **D408** | **R12-BUS2 [blocker] — acceptance-edge gating cannot implement an asynchronous write strobe.** `RAM_W̄Ē`/`BOOT_W̄Ē`/`CART_W̄Ē` are qualified by **PHI2 high**, so they are active from 3k+1.5, while `RDY` need only be valid by 3k+2.68. **54.8 ns of a 69.8 ns write pulse has already reached the memory** before the falling edge decides anything. Repeating the held cycle repeats the pulse — a further nonvolatile FRAM cycle, or a duplicated NOR unlock/program or smart-cartridge command that changes device **command state**. | **Accepted; the round-eleven repair was a gating rule where only a scheduling rule can work.** Verified by computation before deciding: the runt-pulse alternative (start the strobe at oito's arbitration tick 3k+2) leaves **46.6 ns**, below what a 70 ns FRAM needs, so it does not close either. The rule is now: **oito never asserts `RDY` to hold a cycle that emits an external write strobe.** It has `R/W̄` and its decode by ≈3k+1.3, so it defers to the next cycle. **Writes to oito's own registers are still held freely** — no external pulse, internal commit. **The deferral is bounded at 3 CPU cycles**: the longest run of back-to-back external writes a 65C02 produces is the interrupt/`BRK` push sequence (`PCH`, `PCL`, `P` to the stack). Consequences recomputed rather than assumed — PCM steal onset worst case **13 ticks** rather than 4, and **OAM DMA becomes `8N + 6 … 8N + 9`** rather than a constant, controllable by following `STA $4061` with a non-write cycle. Tax and FIFO headroom are untouched: 9 ticks against a 448-tick mix period cannot cause an underrun. |
| **D409** | **R12-BUS3 [blocker] — held side-effecting reads promised stable data with no snapshot and no matching commit.** `cpu_transfer_accept` says *when* a side effect commits, not *what it commits to*. If `KBD_SCAN` is read while empty and a byte arrives during the hold, the CPU could receive `$00` and pop the new byte; if an APU `$A5` flag sets after the first hold edge, acceptance could clear a flag the CPU never saw. Either the value is snapshotted and the commit is wrong, or the value is live and "the same value across held edges" is false. | **Accepted.** Each transaction now carries **two latches captured at the same event that sets `serviced`**: a **read-data** latch (re-driven unchanged at every hold edge) and a **commit token** — for a FIFO the identity of the head element or the token **empty**; for clear-on-read/W1C the exact **bit mask returned**. At acceptance the commit applies **exactly the token**: pop that element, or nothing if the snapshot was empty, so a byte arriving during the hold **stays queued**; clear only those bits, so a later flag **survives**. §16.2 gains held-empty-then-arrival and held-status-then-new-flag traces. |
| **D410** | **R12-SW1 [blocker] — pre-compile RAM homes can overlap prog8's own BSS.** Moving the initialised-data transformation before `prog8c` fixed the relocation impossibility but created an ownership collision: address-bound declarations are **memory-mapped** and reserve nothing, while the same compilation independently allocates BSS, static parameter cells and temporaries in `$0200–$3FFF`. Fixed-bank ownership stops two *banks* colliding; it does nothing about two allocators inside one bank's build. | **Accepted.** An **initialised-data arena** is sized before compilation and taken from the **top of `$0200–$3FFF`, growing down**, and a **generated `re8.properties`** bounds the compiler's allocatable range at `arena_base − 1`. The packer re-checks the map and fails on overlap. **The load-bearing step is the bound, and it is a dependency rather than a certainty**: if the pinned compiler does not honour a bounded range in its target definition, a **compiler patch is the only remaining mechanism**, and that is now an explicit §16.2 gate. *Detecting overlap afterwards is validation, not placement — a build that succeeds by chance is not a build.* |
| **D411** | **R12-HW1 [high] — the safety-relevant reset table omitted the boot, RAM and VRAM control pins.** Added in v0.12 as the record of signals whose unpowered behaviour matters, it covered the cartridge selects and stopped there — omitting `BOOT_CĒ`/`BOOT_ŌĒ`/**`BOOT_W̄Ē`**, `RAM_CĒ`/`RAM_ŌĒ`/`RAM_W̄Ē`, private-VRAM `/CE`/`/OE`/`/WE`, and oito's bus-master `R/W̄`, address and bank outputs. | **Accepted.** All are added with pad state and board guarantee. The `BOOT_W̄Ē` row states the point plainly: **`BOOT_CTRL.BOOT_FLASH_WE` protects the firmware only after oito is powered, reset and decoding** — before that the pull-up is the whole protection. RAM/VRAM enables get the contention-current argument, and the bus-master row requires oito high-Z whenever `BE` is high, with `R/W̄` driven high before it is driven at all. |
| **D412** | **R12-TOOL1 [medium] — the structural verifier passed the contradictions it was built to prevent.** `HELD_COMPLETES` matched only "finish"/"complete", never "reaches its acceptance edge"; the retirement guard was a **document-wide existence test**, so one qualified sentence anywhere excused an unqualified one elsewhere; nothing compared acceptance-edge strobes against the PHI2-high equations; nothing required a read snapshot. Separately, ledger facts `40.0`/`62.0`/`63.0` were searched as literal strings against a spec saying "40 ns", producing permanent false warnings. | **Accepted on every point.** New transition-level guards: low-`RDY`-asked-to-accept (with negated forms like "**not** `cpu_transfer_accept`" correctly exempt), the three edge terms required by name, external-write strobes rejected if *gated* rather than *scheduled*, the scheduling rule required to exist, and the read snapshot and commit token required. The retirement test is **per sentence** and narrowed to the shape that was wrong — *"an edge retires a cycle"* — because a blanket ban on the word produced eight false hits, and R12-TOOL1's other half is that persistent false warnings hide real ones. Ledger facts now compare **numerically with units**. Warnings fell from 33 to 30, and the three removed were all false. |
| **D413** | **R12-TOOL2 [medium] — a clean publication build had an undeclared mandatory dependency.** `tools-build-spec-html.py` imports `markdown`, `build-publish.py` runs it *before* verifying, and the repository had no `requirements.txt` or setup step — only a note that `cwebp` is optional. The "generate, verify, publish" claim was not reproducible from a clean checkout. | **Accepted.** `requirements.txt` added with `markdown` **pinned**, since the generated HTML is a published artefact and a renderer upgrade would change it silently. README gains a Setup section that states which dependency is required and which is optional. |
| **D414** | **R12-DOC1 [medium] — the spec said ten adversarial reviews while v0.13 and the README said twelve**, in two normative places. | **Accepted, and made underivable-by-hand.** Both corrected, and `tools-verify.py` now **computes the count from the review files on disk** and fails if any document states a different one. *Three places drifted because nothing derived the number — the same root cause as the parts queue and the front-page list, both of which are now generated for the same reason.* |

**What round twelve says about the process.** Round eleven's finding was "a rule that stops one transition short"; round twelve's is **"a correct new rule whose consequences were not propagated."** `cpu_transfer_accept` was right, and three sections kept asking for an event it had just made impossible — including, in R12-BUS2, one that physics had already made impossible independently. The general lesson is uncomfortable but simple: **adding a definition is an edit to every sentence that touches its subject**, and the verifier can only enforce that once someone works out what the definition forbids.

### T. Fourteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round13.md`, 2026-07-28)

Eight findings, three blockers. **All three blockers are defects in v0.14's own repairs** — the fourth round running in which that is true, which is itself the most useful thing this round says.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D415** | **R13-BUS1 [blocker] — the acceptance-gated external strobe came back.** v0.14 established that `RAM_W̄Ē`/`BOOT_W̄Ē`/`CART_W̄Ē` cannot be gated at a falling edge, then two sentences in the same section said "one qualification serves both" and "every write strobe … gated by `cpu_transfer_accept`". Neither was scoped to internal effects, so both readmitted the unimplementable rule beside §11.3's PHI2-high equations. | **Accepted.** §6.5 now carries an explicit **two-mechanism table**: internal effects (register writes, font RAM, pops, clears, accumulator consume, W1C, pointer increment, retirement) are **gated** at acceptance; the three named external strobes are **scheduled** and never gated. "One qualification serves both" is deleted; the later sentence names the three strobes as excluded; and a bus-master request is stated to **wait for the first non-external-write cycle**. The verifier guard now matches the three strobe names rather than one phrase — *the old guard required the literal words "external write strobe", which the returning sentence simply did not use*. |
| **D416** | **R13-DMA1 [blocker] — OAM DMA had two normative costs.** §6.6 correctly published `8N + 6 … 8N + 9`, while §8.5 still said a transfer "always costs `8N + 6`", and the examples still read 54 cycles and 1,030. "One trigger phase" and "no external-write deferral" are different claims; the §8.5 sentence conflated them. | **Accepted.** §8.5 now says the trigger has one *phase* while the first hold edge still moves 0–3 cycles, and every live example is a range: **1,030–1,033** for a full transfer, **54–57** for six entries, each exact only under the SDK sequence that follows `STA $4061` with a non-write cycle. `tools-model.py` derives **both endpoints from one function** (`oam_full_min/max`, `oam_six_min/max`) and the verifier checks all four; publishing only the floor is precisely how the constant survived. |
| **D417** | **R13-READ1 [blocker] — clearing the returned mask loses a reassertion of an already-set bit.** v0.14's commit token said "clear only the bits in the mask" and concluded a flag set during the hold survives. **That holds only if the bit was 0 at snapshot time.** If APU `$A5`.0 is already 1 and voice 0 underruns again during the hold, clearing the snapshotted mask erases the second underrun — a one-bit sticky latch carries no generation identity, so nothing distinguishes "the old 1" from "another event set this 1". | **Accepted, and the conformance trace was as wrong as the rule.** Each held clear-on-read transaction now accumulates **`A` — every bit *set by an event* after the snapshot, recorded even when the live bit was already 1** — and acceptance applies `live ← (live & ~S) | A`. §16.2's trace is rewritten to **start with the tested bit already set and re-set that same bit**, with an explicit note that a trace setting a *different* bit passes without exercising the defect. *The v0.14 trace would have passed against the broken rule.* |
| **D418** | **R13-READ2 [high] — the mask token could not express `MOUSE_WHEEL`, and W1C was in the wrong category.** The wheel is a signed accumulator saturating at ±127, not a bit field. Clearing the live value loses later motion; subtracting the snapshot is **non-invertible** under saturation and opposite-signed deltas. W1C is a *write* whose mask comes from CPU write data, not a read whose mask is "returned". | **Accepted.** Three commit types are now defined — **FIFO** (head identity or `empty`), **sticky clear-on-read bits** (snapshot `S` plus post-snapshot set mask `A`), and **numeric consume-on-read accumulator** (snapshot `V` plus a separately accumulated post-snapshot delta `D`, applied as `live ← D`, with saturation stated to apply at each accumulation step). W1C moves to the write path: sample the CPU's write byte for the held transaction, apply once at acceptance. A held-wheel trace with **both signs and a saturating run** is added. |
| **D419** | **R13-SW1 [medium] — `re8.properties` was both a versioned artefact and a per-build generated file.** One file identity cannot be both the compatibility mechanism (paired with the pinned `prog8c` hash) and the ownership mechanism (RAM range ending at `arena_base − 1`). The arena also had no failure bounds. | **Accepted.** Two files with different names: **`re8-base.properties`** (versioned, checked in, paired with the compiler hash) and **`re8-build.properties`** (generated per build, a deterministic copy with **one key overridden**, its SHA-256 recorded in the build map). Arena bounds are explicit — `0 ≤ initialised_size ≤ $3E00`, `arena_base = $4000 − round_up(size, 2)` — with **failure before `prog8c` runs** on overflow and **failure during compilation**, as ordinary RAM exhaustion against the reduced range, if the compiler's own cells do not fit. |
| **D420** | **R13-TOOL1 [high] — the repaired verifier passed all three returned blockers.** Its external-strobe guard required a literal phrase the offending sentence avoided; the model carried only OAM floors, so no comparison against `+9` was possible; nothing rejected "always costs `8N + 6`"; the snapshot guard checked only that two phrases existed. It also passed a **stale SVG**: `diagrams/compositor-arbitration.svg` still read "OAM DMA (≈683 cycles)", superseded since v0.3 — and that file is **inlined into the normative HTML**, so the figure's caption ships as part of the contract. | **Accepted on every point.** Strobe guard matches the three names plus "every write strobe", with the correct exclusions exempt; OAM min *and* max come from the model and all four are checked; snapshot guards require a post-snapshot set mask, a `MOUSE_WHEEL` rule and the non-invertibility statement, and reject W1C filed with clear-on-read. A new **DIAGRAM** check scans SVG **text nodes** for superseded facts, proved by re-inserting the 683-cycle caption. The diagram itself now carries no cost figure — *a conceptual arbitration diagram should not have been quoting a cycle count in the first place*. |
| **D421** | **R13-DOC1 [medium] — filename counting was off by one and had no truthful state for an unresolved report.** `len(round*.md) + 1` ignored that the v0.1 row records **two** reviews resolved before v0.1, and the moment a new report file appeared the checker demanded a count the documents could not honestly claim, since the same sentence says every finding is dispositioned. | **Accepted, and this was a fair hit on a fix I had just made.** Two values are now tracked: **performed** (`2` pre-v0.1 + report files) and **dispositioned** (design-history review registers + the first review, whose register is §2.A–D). The documents state the *dispositioned* count; a mismatch is a **warning naming the outstanding report**, not a failure. *A checker that has no valid state during normal work will be worked around rather than fixed.* |
| **D422** | **R13-DOC2 [medium] — the README carried a second, undated review total.** "Seven adversarial review rounds found roughly 250 defects" sat immediately below the current status and read as a running total. | **Accepted.** It is now explicitly a **dated snapshot of the motivation** — through round seven, v0.7 — with the reader pointed at the generated status above and the per-round registers for current figures. |

**On the shape of these rounds.** Findings are converging: 32, 25, 21, 19, 16, 11, 10, 8, 8. **Blockers are not**: 7, 4, 4, 5, 6, 4, 5, 4, 3. And for four consecutive rounds, nearly every blocker has been a defect in the *previous round's repair* rather than a pre-existing one. That is the signal worth recording. It does not mean the work is going badly — each individual repair has been correct — but it does mean the document has reached a size and interdependence where **a change is not local**, and the tooling can only catch the consequences someone has already thought to encode. The honest conclusion is that this converges on *findings* but not obviously on *blockers*, and that continuing to iterate on prose is now yielding less than an executable model of the bus contract would.

### U. Fifteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round14.md`, 2026-07-28)

Eight findings, three blockers. Two of the three blockers are **scope errors rather than logic errors**: a rule that was correct but bound only one of the two parties it needed to, and a requirement stated without any mechanism that could produce it. That is a different failure from the last four rounds and worth naming.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D423** | **R14-BUS1 [blocker] — the no-stall-on-external-write rule bound only oito, and the probe is the second `RDY` puller.** `cpu_transfer_accept` deliberately observes the *pin* because `RDY` is open-drain and wired-AND — and then the rule that protects the pulse was scoped to one puller. §13 gives the probe five halt paths (host `halt/run`, `freeze (RDY)` before register capture, live-memory access, dev bootstrap, autonomous crash detection), and **the stack-overflow heuristic triggers off a push write**, so it could land inside the published three-write interrupt push sequence and repeat a stack write, an FRAM write cycle or a NOR command. | **Accepted.** The rule is restated as binding **every `RDY` puller**, with the two enumerated in a table and the probe's obligation stated: sample `R/W̄` and the address window, **defer the halt to the first non-external-write cycle**, then assert `RDY` in time for that cycle's falling edge. The **SYNC-qualified hardware breakpoint keeps its existing timing** as a separately safe case — it holds the cycle after an opcode fetch, which is an operand or dummy read. *The preserve/abort table is not a defence here: preserving the transaction is correct, and it is precisely why the held external write would be re-presented.* |
| **D424** | **R14-BUS2 [high] — the cartridge strobe equations required "a valid CPU cycle" while their own truth table enables non-CPU masters.** The table has a PCM steal row and a probe bus-master row; during both, `BE` is low and there is **no valid CPU cycle at all**. Read literally, the qualifier suppressed `ROM_CĒ`, `SAVE_CĒ` and `CART_W̄Ē` during exactly the rows that enable them — making PCM sample fetches and probe programming of a dev cartridge impossible. | **Accepted.** Replaced by a defined **`bus_cycle_valid`** naming all three owners — CPU (`BE` high, cycle not held), oito (`BE` low, PCM/OAM), probe (`BE` low, §13) — applied to `RAM_W̄Ē` and `BOOT_W̄Ē` as well. Write protection is stated to gate the probe identically: `save`, `wp` and `we` are evaluated whoever owns the bus. |
| **D425** | **R14-DMA1 [blocker] — the active-display OAM ceiling omitted the deferral added to the blanking range.** Blanking became `8N + 6 … 8N + 9` in v0.15, but the active-display row still read "up to `8N × 4 + 6`". The startup deferral happens *before* the transfer, so it applies identically. | **Accepted.** Active display is now **`8N × 4 + 6` … `8N × 4 + 9`** — **4,102–4,105** for a full transfer, **198–201** for six entries. The model's `oam()` gains a `mult` argument so blanking and active-display endpoints come from **one function**, and the verifier checks all eight values. *The deferral adds once rather than scaling with `N`, which is why "≈4,100" stayed reasonable while the normative formula was three cycles short.* |
| **D426** | **R14-SW1 [blocker] — the three prog8 origin classes had no origin mechanism.** §11.0 requires `$8040`, `$8000` and `$C000`; §14.1 gives every class the *same* generated target whose only override is the RAM ceiling, over a base target with `pc_start=8040`. prog8 has no origin flag. **As written, two of three classes compile at the wrong absolute addresses** — which the packer cannot repair and from which the fixed-bank interface and vector map cannot be built. | **Accepted, and this was a requirement masquerading as a design.** The mechanism is now normative: the SDK injects **exactly one module-level `%address` directive** per generated top-level bank source, alongside the `%option romable` it already injects; `%address` **takes precedence over `pc_start`**; the selected origin and each generated source's SHA-256 go in the build map; and the three-bank fixture **asserts the first emitted address of every class**. *"Its own origin and its own invocation" named the requirement and no way to satisfy it.* |
| **D427** | **R14-REG1 [high] — W1C was still in a read-side list, and its coincidence rule existed for only one of four families.** §6.5's acceptance list read "read side effects (FIFO pops, clear-on-read, W1C)" while text below correctly called W1C a write. Of `IRQ_STATUS`, `STATUS`.3, `INPUT_STATUS` and `KBD_STATUS`.2, only the first stated that a coincident set beats a clear — and `STATUS`.3 adds a frame-boundary auto-clear, making it a three-way question. | **Accepted.** W1C is removed from the read-side list, and **one global precedence order** is published for all four families: **automatic clear → CPU W1C mask → hardware set**. Set applies last, so a coincident set always wins and no event is lost; the worst case is one redundant service, against a silent unreproducible bug. `STATUS`.3's three-way case falls out of the same order rather than needing a special rule. |
| **D428** | **R14-READ1 [medium] — "during the hold" did not define the whole post-snapshot interval, and `S` was the wrong mask.** The snapshot is captured when `serviced` sets, acceptance is a later edge, so **a gap always exists even with no hold** — an event landing there would have been consumed as part of a snapshot taken before it. Separately, `S` as "the returned bit mask" would clear `MOUSE_STATUS` bit 0 `present` and the protocol bits on every read. | **Accepted.** The interval is defined as **from after the snapshot's event step to the acceptance edge's step 0**, ordered by the §6.5 event list, regardless of whether any hold edge occurs. **`S = read_data & clear_on_read_mask`**, with the mask published per register — `MOUSE_STATUS` bits 1–3 only. |
| **D429** | **R14-TOOL1 [high] — the verifier's new guards were phrase-shaped and missed their own dependants.** The W1C guard rejected the exact string `clear-on-read or W1C`, so the live `clear-on-read, W1C` passed — a punctuation change was enough. The external-strobe guard checked that *oito* had the scheduling sentence, not that every puller obeyed it. Nothing modelled the active-display OAM ceiling, compared truth-table owners against the "valid CPU cycle" qualifier, or checked that each prog8 origin had a mechanism. | **Accepted, and the guards are now data-driven.** `RDY_PULLERS`, `BUS_OWNERS`, `W1C_REGISTERS` and `PROG8_ORIGINS` are **inventories the checks quantify over**, so adding a puller or a W1C family extends the check rather than requiring a new pattern. W1C is matched semantically — any `read side effects (…)` list containing W1C fails, punctuation irrelevant. **Two defects were found in the guards by their own mutation tests**: a regex that missed "non-external-write" because it required a space, and a puller check that failed because `segments()` splits table cells and so separated a puller from its obligation — correct for scoping an exemption, wrong for reading a row as one statement. That check now matches at line granularity, with the reason recorded. |
| **D430** | **R14-DOC1 [medium] — the checker computed both review totals and validated only one.** v0.15's README said "Fourteen adversarial reviews performed, fourteen dispositioned" while a fifteenth report sat unresolved; the checker warned about the outstanding report but never rejected the false half of a sentence it had the data to reject. | **Accepted.** Both **labelled** totals are now parsed and compared against their derived counts independently, so `performed` and `dispositioned` each fail on their own. A mismatch *between* the two totals stays a warning, because that is the normal mid-round state. |

**What round fourteen says about the process.** The blocker count is 3, down from 4/5/4/3 — but two of these three were *scope* failures rather than logic failures: a correct rule bound to one of two parties, and a requirement with no mechanism. Those are cheaper to find than the timing defects of rounds 10–13 and cheaper to fix, which is mildly encouraging. The more useful observation is in D429: **the two guard defects were found by mutation-testing the guards, not by reading them.** The habit of proving each check fails on the exact text it targets has now caught more defects in the checker than in the specification, which is the right ratio for a checker.

### V. The executable bus model (2026-07-28)

- **D431 — the CPU bus contract moves from prose to a state machine, and the specification's timing tables become its output.** Taken after round fourteen, on evidence rather than taste: of the **19 blockers in rounds 10–14, 13 were in the bus contract**, and every one was one of three shapes — two transitions given for the same state, a rule whose consequences were not propagated, or a rule quantified over one agent instead of all. Prose has no mechanism against any of them, and the verifier could only catch each *after* a reviewer had found it and someone had encoded a pattern for it.

 **What was built.** `tools-bus-model.py` holds the contract as a single `step()` function over an explicit state — tick, cycle kind, `live`/`pending`/`serviced`/`snapped`, the set of agents pulling `RDY`, the bus owner, and per-transaction counters. It enumerates the **complete reachable state space by BFS** — about 1,900 states across nine cycle kinds and both external halt requesters — so a property that holds, holds for every input sequence rather than for the ones a test happened to try.

 | Property | The defect it makes impossible |
 |---|---|
 | `ext_strobe_once` | a held external write pulsed twice into FRAM, NOR or flash |
 | `one_grant_per_txn` | two VRAM reads and two pointer increments for one instruction |
 | `one_commit_per_txn` | a held read popping two FIFO bytes |
 | `no_effect_at_hold_edge` | committing on every falling edge rather than the accepting one |
 | `txn_survives_takeover` | a takeover deleting the access the CPU is in the middle of |
 | `no_event_lost` | a second event on an already-set sticky bit erased by a clear the CPU never saw |

 **Three design choices carry the argument.** *Agents are a list*, so `ext_strobe_once` quantifying over `PULLERS` is what makes R14-BUS1 — "the rule binds only oito" — unrepeatable: adding a puller *is* extending the proof. *The external strobe is modelled as physics, not policy* — it fires on every presentation of the cycle, because that is what an asynchronous PHI2-high pulse does — so the scheduling rule is what makes the property hold rather than something the model assumes. And *the tables in §6.5 and §8.5 are generated from the model*, so a sentence elsewhere can no longer contradict a table, because the table is not a sentence.

 **All seven historical blockers are reintroduced as mutations and must break their named property.** R10-CPU1, R11-CPU1, R11-DMA1, R12-BUS1, R12-BUS2, R13-READ1 and R14-BUS1 each become a `Rules` flag; the build fails if any of them stops being caught. This is the README's own rule — *a property nobody has watched fail is not evidence of anything* — applied to the thing that is now the argument for replacing prose.

 **Two defects were found in the model while building it**, both by that mutation suite rather than by reading: `one_service_per_txn` was written as a predicate over a *trace*, which spans several transactions and so could not express "once per transaction" at all; and `no_event_lost` conflated **delivered to the CPU** with **consumed from the producer**, which is precisely the distinction R13-READ1 is about — so the mutation that should have failed it passed. Both are fixed, and the second is the more interesting: the property had been *stated* correctly in the specification and *modelled* incorrectly.

 **Scope, stated so it is not overclaimed.** This covers the CPU transaction, `RDY` ownership, external strobes and read-commit semantics. It does **not** cover the ASRC filter (a DSP design problem), the parts ledger (an evidence problem) or the prog8 pipeline (an upstream-capability problem) — the other 6 blockers of rounds 10–14. It is also a model of the *contract*, not of the silicon: §16.2's measurement gates are unchanged, and the traces it now generates to `artefacts/bus-traces.md` are the conformance fixtures, not bench results.

### W. Sixteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round15.md`, 2026-07-28)

Seven findings, two blockers. **Both blockers landed inside the bus contract — the domain the executable model was built for — and the model did not catch either.** That is the most important thing this round says, and the disposition below is mostly about why.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D432** | **R15-BUS1 [blocker] — `bus_cycle_valid` made ownership depend on `RDY`.** The CPU clause read "`BE` high **and the CPU cycle is not held**", which is wrong twice. A held external **read** would lose `ROM_CĒ`/`SAVE_CĒ`, contradicting §6.5's promise that a held cycle re-presents unchanged and re-drives its data — and since `RDY` may be released as late as `t_PCS` = 15 ns before acceptance, a select that only became valid at release would give a **70 ns cartridge or FRAM 15 ns to answer**. A breakpoint routinely holds the operand cycle after an opcode fetch, and that operand is often in cartridge ROM. For a held **write**, suppressing the strobe because the cycle is marked held is **exactly the late gating §6.5 proves impossible**. | **Accepted.** Validity now depends on **ownership and `debug_select_suppress` only**, never on `RDY`. A held external read keeps its select for **every** presentation, so device access time is measured from the first presentation and `RDY` release constrains only when the CPU samples. External-write uniqueness follows from the scheduling rule **and nothing else** — stated in those words, because the alternative is a model that looks correct for a reason hardware cannot reproduce. |
| **D433** | **R15-BUS2 [blocker] — the motherboard decode stayed CPU-only.** `RAM_CĒ = !(!A15 && !A14 && cpu_memory_cycle)` used a term defined nowhere and specific to the CPU, so with `BE` low it deasserted — precisely when **OAM DMA reads its source block from system RAM** and when **the probe's live-memory access reads or writes it**. Neither feature could reach its own documented storage. | **Accepted.** `cpu_memory_cycle` is replaced by `bus_cycle_valid`, and §11.3 now publishes **complete CE/OE/WE equations plus an owner table** for system RAM *and* boot flash, covering CPU read/write, OAM source read, probe read, probe write, debug suppression and reset. **Probe writes to boot flash are explicitly refused** — the §11.1 updater owns that path, so a live-memory write cannot brick the machine. *This was the same scope error as R14-BUS1/BUS2, one layer down: §11.3 acquired three-owner validity in v0.16 while the motherboard memories kept the old qualifier.* |
| **D434** | **R15-BUS3 [high] — "exactly two `RDY` pullers" contradicted §2's three.** §2 says three parties sit on the open-drain line — oito, the probe, and **the CPU itself during `WAI`** — while §6.5 said two, and `tools-bus-model.py` encoded two. | **Accepted, and the fix is a distinction rather than a number.** **Electrical pullers (3)** and **external halt requesters (2)** are now separate sets. `WAI` is exempt **for a stated reason**: it is the CPU's own instruction, so the cycle it stops *is* a `WAI` cycle and can never be an external write in progress — the rule guards against halting a cycle you did not choose. The model carries `ELECTRICAL_PULLERS` and `HALT_REQUESTERS` as separate lists and prints both, and the §6.5 table is generated from the three-entry one. |
| **D435** | **R15-VAL1 [high] — the external-write validation gate still tested only oito.** The normative rule bound the probe in v0.16; §16.2 did not follow. The round-fourteen blocker was specifically probe *firmware* behaviour, and neither the SYNC-qualified breakpoint gate nor the `R/W̄`-ownership gate proves an arbitrary probe halt deferred. | **Accepted.** The gate now names **every external halt requester** and requires **probe-originated traces at every position in the three-write interrupt push sequence**, across host halt, register-capture freeze, live access, dev bootstrap and crash detection — measured, with the note that a state-machine trace does not qualify probe firmware. |
| **D436** | **R15-READ1 [medium] — the commit table's column heading still said "Accumulated during the hold"**, contradicting the prose directly above it, which had been corrected to snapshot-to-acceptance. A heading is normative, and an emulator author reading the table alone would implement the old rule. | **Accepted.** The column is **"Accumulated after snapshot, before acceptance"**. |
| **D437** | **R15-DOC1 [medium] — the generated front-page table was malformed.** It declares one column; two rows had been copied wholesale from §16.2 with their criterion and reference cells attached. The source Markdown was structurally invalid whatever a renderer happened to recover. | **Accepted.** Every row is generated from the §16.2 **Item** cell only, and the verifier now fails if the front-page rows do not all have the same cell count. *A generation-boundary error, and the reason it survived is that the previous check compared row *contents* and never row *shape*.* |
| **D438** | **R15-DOC2 [medium] — the bus traces were cited as current proof while §16.3 said no artefact exists.** Two statements about the same file. | **Accepted, by separating two artefact classes.** `re8-bus-waveforms-<ver>/` is **measured silicon**; the new `re8-bus-traces-<ver>.md` is **generated model output** — evidence about the contract, not about the hardware. §16.3 gains per-artefact status instead of one aggregate "none exist yet", and the working path `artefacts/bus-traces.md` is labelled **build output, not a release artefact**: unversioned, unhashed, and no longer cited as normative proof. |

**What this round says about the executable model, stated plainly.** The bet made after round fourteen was that moving the bus contract into a state machine would close the class that had produced 13 of 19 blockers. Round fifteen found two more blockers in that same domain, and **the model was silent on both**. The reason is not that the approach failed but that **its scope was narrower than the domain it claimed**: the model represented the CPU *transaction* — edges, holds, acceptance, snapshots — and did not represent the *decode* at all. Chip selects, per-owner validity and the memory-control equations were outside its state, so R15-BUS1 and R15-BUS2 were as invisible to it as they had been to prose.

The model has therefore been extended rather than defended: it now carries `select`, `select_dropped_while_live` and a `decode_owners` term, with two new properties — **`read_select_held`** (a held external read keeps its select) and **`decode_owner_complete`** (every owner reaches the memory it is documented to use) — and both round-fifteen blockers are added to the regression suite as mutations that must break them.

**One new check is of a different kind and worth naming.** R15-BUS1's deepest point was not that the rule was wrong but that it would have been *right for the wrong reason*: marking a held cycle invalid suppresses a second strobe in an abstract model while hardware has already emitted the first. So the suite now has a **mechanism test** — remove the scheduling rule *and* add the validity gating, and `ext_strobe_once` must **still fail**. If it passed, the property would be satisfied by something silicon cannot do. Checking that a property fails for the right reason is a stronger claim than checking that it fails.

**Honest scorecard.** The model caught nothing this round. It would have caught both, had it been built one layer wider — and the layer it was missing is exactly the one the round-fourteen findings (R14-BUS2, the cartridge owner table) had already pointed at. The lesson is not about notation: **a model's scope is a claim, and it needs the same scepticism as any other claim in this document.**

### X. Seventeenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round16.md`, 2026-07-28)

Ten findings, five blockers. **Four of the five are in the decode and ownership text added in v0.17** — new normative content with five holes in it. The fifth, and the most consequential, is a gap that had been open since the debug probe was first specified.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D439** | **R16-BUS1 [blocker] — `we` had two incompatible definitions in one subsection.** First `SAVE_CTRL.4` (the cartridge command arm), then `PHI2 high AND !R/W̄`. Either reading is destructive: the later one bypasses `CART_WE_ENABLE` so ordinary writes reach NOR command state, and the earlier one makes **system-RAM writes depend on a cartridge gate**. | **Accepted.** Split into **`write_phase`** and **`cart_we_enable`**, with every symbol in the subsection defined exactly once in one block. `write_phase` appears in every physical WE predicate; `cart_we_enable` only in the ROM-space command arm. |
| **D440** | **R16-BUS2 [blocker] — the cartridge equations had the wrong polarity for the pins they named.** `ROM_CĒ = (A && !save) || F` evaluates to **1** for a selected access, while an active-low pin must be **0** — and outside a selected access it evaluates to 0, which would *assert* the pin. The motherboard equations added in v0.17 correctly used `!(...)`, making the discrepancy explicit rather than typographical. | **Accepted.** **Positive predicates first** (`rom_select`, `save_select`, `cart_write`, `ram_*`, `boot_*`), then every pin as its inversion, in one block with one convention. `bus_cycle_valid` and `write_phase` are now **inside** each predicate rather than in introductory prose, and the truth table is re-expanded as **electrical 0/1 levels** so "asserted" cannot conceal another polarity error. |
| **D441** | **R16-BUS3 [blocker] — the equation did not implement the table's probe boot-write prohibition.** The table said boot flash is read-only for the probe; the equation was owner-agnostic, so a probe driving a boot-window address with `R/W̄` low satisfied every term whenever `BOOT_FLASH_WE` happened to be set. **The brick-safety property was prose only.** | **Accepted.** **`boot_program_owner ≡ owner == cpu && BOOT_CTRL.BOOT_FLASH_WE`** is now a term in `boot_write`, so the refusal is in logic. It resets false, and only the RAM-resident updater of §11.1 sets it. |
| **D442** | **R16-BUS4 [blocker] — probe-owned register and port accesses had no acceptance event.** §13 has the probe driving the VRAM and palette ports and arming `CART_WE_ENABLE` for dev bootstrap, while holding `RDY` **low** throughout — and every internal side effect fired at `cpu_transfer_accept`, which requires `RDY` high. So a probe write to `VRAM_DATA` or `SAVE_CTRL` never committed, no pointer advanced, and the documented bootstrap path could not run. Releasing `RDY` is not a repair: it would clock the tri-stated CPU forward while another master owns its bus. | **Accepted, and this one was put to the owner as a capability question** rather than decided unilaterally, since the alternative was withdrawing a documented debug capability. The decision was to **generalise**: `owner_transfer_accept` is now parameterised, with cases for cpu, oito and probe, and the probe case is specified in full — when it fires, that `RDY` stays low, read-data timing, VRAM-port stalls *of the probe rather than the CPU*, identical snapshots and commit tokens, pointer increments, unchanged write protection, unmapped reads, and reset behaviour. Every internal side effect is now defined against the general form. *This was not a v0.17 regression: it had been latent since the probe was specified, and only became visible once acceptance was made explicit enough to check.* |
| **D443** | **R16-BUS5 [blocker] — wired-AND `BE` is a request line, not a one-hot grant.** A low level says *someone* asked the CPU to release the bus; it does not say who may drive. Nothing prevented a probe live-memory request overlapping a PCM steal or OAM DMA, and §6.3's reset table made it worse by driving oito's bus-master outputs "while `BE` is low (oito **or probe** bus-mastering)". Two masters could enable their drivers into each other, and `bus_cycle_valid` would have validated the contended cycle. | **Accepted.** Ownership becomes an explicit **one-hot `owner ∈ {none, cpu, oito, probe}`** register held in oito, with a **seven-step probe acquisition sequence** — request, oito stops issuing takeovers, CPU reaches a hold edge, **oito acknowledges**, only then probe transceivers enable, probe disables drivers, `BE` then `RDY` released. `DBḠ`'s live-bus-master frame is distinguished from the opcode-jam frame. §6.3's row is corrected to **`owner = oito`**, not `BE` low. |
| **D444** | **R16-BUS6 [high] — `bus_cycle_valid` identified a CPU owner during reset.** The CPU clause was simply "`BE` high", and `BE` has a pull-up and is released during reset, so the clause was **true while `RES̄` was low** — the decode could assert selects from a floating address while the reset table required every select inactive. | **Accepted.** `owner = none` during reset, and a stated three-level precedence: invalid rail or `RES̄` → all enables inactive; `debug_select_suppress` → all ordinary selects inactive; otherwise exactly one owner. |
| **D445** | **R16-BUS7 [high] — `boot_win` did not contain `BOOT_SRC`.** Defined only as an address range, the external flash would be selected **beside the internal stub at reset** and **beside the cartridge after handoff** — data-bus contention in both. | **Accepted.** `boot_win ≡ (BOOT_SRC == 1) && A15 && A14 && A13`, with all three `BOOT_SRC` states in the decode table and `BOOT_BANK` stated to be an **address source, not a select condition**. |
| **D446** | **R16-DBG1 [medium] — `debug_select_suppress` was called "an explicit §13 signal" that §13 never defined**, and the entry wording said the probe "raises `DBḠ`" for an active-low signal, where *asserts the request* and *drives the level high* are opposite. | **Accepted.** `debug_select_suppress` gains a definition table: reset value, assertion on the **acknowledged** debug-entry transition **before** the probe enables D0–D7, deassertion **after** it disables them — the suppression window strictly contains the drive window — scope including oito's internal register decode, and falling-edge timing. "Raises" becomes **"asserts low"**. |
| **D447** | **R16-VAL1 [high] — the held-read repair had no measured waveforms.** v0.17 added correct prose and a model property; §16.2 required none of the four physical cases. The generic gate proves transaction count, not that each `CĒ`/`ŌĒ` stayed selected across every presentation or that a 70 ns device had its access time from the first presentation. | **Accepted.** A per-memory gate for **system SRAM, external boot flash, cartridge NOR and save FRAM**, each showing first presentation, repeated hold presentation, `RDY` released at the minimum 15 ns, the single accepted sample, and a takeover-and-return case with **no select glitch**. |
| **D448** | **R16-VAL2 [medium] — snapshot validation had no unstalled case.** Every published case was a held transaction, so an emulator could pass all of them while running the accumulator only when a hold flag is set — losing an event in the ordinary one-tick decode-to-acceptance interval, which is the exact scope error the interval definition was written to remove. | **Accepted.** Two **unstalled** cases added, with `RDY` never low, and the requirement that **the expected result is identical whether or not `RDY` was ever low**. |

Plus the three wording corrections the review listed separately: the stray punctuation in "Save writes and command writes are separate paths", "the device sees a selected read" for a CPU write (it is not a read — `R/W̄` is low; the device is selected with **both `OE#` and `WE#` inactive**), and dropping the word "complete" from the equations until reset, source selection, permitted owner, polarity and PHI2 qualification were all literal terms — which they now are.

**The model, and what it did and did not do.** It caught nothing again — but for a different reason than last round, and the distinction matters. R16-BUS1, BUS2, BUS3, BUS6 and BUS7 are **naming, polarity and term-completeness defects in equations**: they are about whether a symbol means one thing and whether a pin's polarity matches its predicate. A state machine over behaviour does not see them, and it is not obvious that any executable artefact short of synthesising the decode would.

R16-BUS4 and R16-BUS5 are different: they **are** behavioural, and the model should have found them. It did not, because it modelled `owner` as a *label carried in the state* rather than as a *contended resource*, and modelled acceptance as one condition rather than as a family. Both are now represented: `drivers` is a set with a **`one_hot_ownership`** invariant, and probe accesses have a **`probe_can_commit`** invariant. The state space grew from 1,879 to **12,514** states, which is the honest cost of representing ownership properly, and regressions now early-exit so the build step stays around ten seconds.

**Eleven historical blockers are now checked mutations.** The pattern across three rounds is consistent and worth stating plainly: **each round finds the model's next missing dimension.** Round 15 found that it had no decode; round 16 found that it had no contention and no owner-parameterised commit. That is progress of a kind — the gaps are getting more specific — but it is not the same as the model having closed the class, and this register should not be read as claiming otherwise.

### Y. Eighteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round17.md`, 2026-07-28)

Ten findings, seven blockers. **The most serious was not a technical defect at all: two decisions in this very register, D447 and D448, and the v0.18 version-history row, all claimed §16.2 gates that were not in the document.** That comes first, because a false record is worse than a missing gate.

#### D449 — the lost gates, and how a disposition came to be untrue

**R17-VAL1 and R17-VAL2.** D447 stated that §16.2 required per-memory held-read waveforms; D448 stated that two unstalled snapshot cases had been added. Neither row existed. The version-history row repeated both claims.

**The mechanism, traced rather than guessed.** The front-page outstanding list contains a **one-cell copy of every §16.2 row title**. So `text.index("| **Snapshot-and-commit traces** |")` finds the *front-page* copy, not the §16.2 row — both round-sixteen edits landed there, mangling a one-column table, and the front-page regeneration that ran seconds later overwrote them. Two scripts each reported success; neither had done what it said.

*My first hypothesis was a stale `cp` restore from `/tmp`. That was wrong, and worth recording as wrong: the actual cause is more specific and more useful, and I only found it by grepping for where the text had gone rather than by reasoning about the workflow.*

**Three fixes, because one would not have been enough.**

1. **The gates are restored**, edited **inside a slice of §16.2** rather than by document-wide index.
2. **`REQUIRED_GATES`** is a named inventory in `tools-verify.py`. A claim to have added a gate now means adding its title there, so the claim and the document fail or pass together. **Prose asserting that a gate exists is not evidence; that list is.**
3. **`tools-mutate.py`** runs every mutation in a **throwaway copy** of the repository. The live tree is never modified, so no restore can revert real work. It immediately found that **two of my guards were weaker than I believed and one did not exist at all** — `owner_transfer_accept` was checked by any mention rather than by its definition block, and nothing checked `cart_A` in `cart_write`.

#### The bus and decode blockers

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D450** | **R17-BUS1 [blocker] — the probe acquisition sequence never asserted `BE`.** It claimed the CPU "releases its pins" at an `RDY` hold edge. `RDY` does the opposite: it holds the CPU with address, direction and `R/W̄` **re-presented**. Only `BE` tri-states them, so step 5 would have enabled the probe against a still-driving CPU. | **Accepted.** `BE` assertion is now step 4, followed by a **≥1 full PHI2 cycle** bus-release interval before `owner = probe`. "Releases its pins" is reserved for the `BE` interval. §13's claim that the probe uses `BE` "for breakpoints and jamming" is corrected — **jamming requires `BE` high**; `BE` is for live ownership and bootstrap only. |
| **D451** | **R17-BUS2 [blocker] — the `DBḠ` "live-owner frame" and "reply phase" do not exist.** `DBḠ` is **probe-only open-drain**; oito cannot drive it. A reply phase on a one-directional wire is a name, not a protocol. | **Accepted, and it cost a pin and a connector.** **`DBACK`** is a new oito→probe output: acquisition-complete in one phase, per-transfer ready in the other — both oito→probe, which is the direction ready/stall semantics need. oito goes to **127 signal pins** (the model derives it). The probe header was **fully allocated at 40 positions**, so this was put to the owner as a routing decision; the choice was to **widen it to 2×21 = 42**, keeping both grounds for the 300 mA budget and leaving one spare. The request encoding is `DBḠ` low ≥1 µs then **two** short pulses — distinguishable from the single pulse that means *step*. **Timeouts are 10 ms in both directions**, because a protocol without one deadlocks the machine. |
| **D452** | **R17-BUS3 [blocker] — probe `cycle-complete` had no transport and contradicted its own direction.** The equation described probe→oito; the table beside it described oito→probe ready. Neither had a wire. It also called PHI2 "probe-driven" while §13 says the probe never drives PHI2. | **Accepted.** A five-step per-transfer handshake on `DBḠ`/`DBACK`, with **`DBACK`'s rise as `owner_transfer_accept(probe)`**, firing exactly once. Cycles are **probe-requested and oito-generated**. A probe external write does not begin until it can finish, so its PHI2-high pulse happens once — the same guarantee the CPU gets, by the same reasoning. |
| **D453** | **R17-BUS4 [blocker] — `cart_write` had no `cart_A` term.** Any write to `$C000–$FFFF` had `rom_select` true through `cart_F` *and* `cart_write` true if either gate was armed, so **the cartridge saw `FLASH_CE#` and `FLASH_WE#` both low in the bank the same table calls read-only** — and `CART_W̄Ē` pulsed on system-RAM, register and boot writes too. | **Accepted.** `cart_A` added, with the invariant **`cart_F -> !cart_write`** stated and checked. |
| **D454** | **R17-BUS5 [blocker] — the selects had no access-phase term**, so consecutive same-window accesses could merge into one continuous low `CĒ` — no new falling edge and no precharge, which the `FM18W08` requires per operation. And "just add PHI2 high" would give a **69.8 ns window against a 70 ns part**, which does not close. | **Accepted, and it exposed the thinnest budget in the document.** `access_phase` is **per *transaction*, not per presentation**, which reconciles held reads (same access, correctly no new edge) with per-access pulsing (distinct accesses separated by the second one's ≈1.3-tick decode delay = **60.5 ns of precharge, automatically**). The access window is stated: 219 ns for a two-cycle port read, but **79 ns against a 70 ns part for an ordinary one-cycle CPU read of cartridge NOR — 9 ns before decode spread and PVT.** That is now a measured gate with two named fallbacks, a faster NOR grade or a slower CPU, rather than an assumption. |
| **D455** | **R17-BUS6 [blocker] — the "expanded exact" probe rows selected RAM and boot flash together.** Single `Probe read`/`Probe write` rows with no address term, in a table calling itself the expansion of address-qualified equations. | **Accepted.** Five address-qualified probe rows, and an explicit invariant: for every owner, address and `BOOT_SRC` combination, **at most one of `RAM_ŌĒ`, `BOOT_ŌĒ`, `ROM_CĒ`, `SAVE_CĒ` may be low.** |
| **D456** | **R17-BUS7 [blocker] — CPU-only commit rules still contradicted `owner_transfer_accept`.** The generated edge table said a low-`RDY` edge fires "nothing" — but probe acceptance *is* a low-`RDY` falling edge. The register-write rule, the snapshot text and the W1C payload all still said `cpu_transfer_accept`. A conforming emulator could commit a probe W1C write or never commit it. | **Accepted.** The edge matrix is **regenerated by owner**, stating the two simultaneous facts separately: at a low-`RDY` edge **the CPU does not retire**, *and* **the current owner may accept**. W1C takes its mask from a **current-owner write-data latch**. Every commit rule now names `owner_transfer_accept(current_owner)`. |
| **D457** | **R17-VAL3 [high] — the new protocols had no validation coverage.** The pre-existing "Probe R/W̄ ownership" row predates all of it. | **Accepted.** A gate requiring simultaneous probe/PCM and probe/OAM requests, an assertion that **CPU, oito and probe drivers are never enabled together**, the acquisition frame and `DBACK` timing, timeout and release, the per-transfer handshake, stalled VRAM reads, once-only writes, and **reset at every state**. |

Plus the wording corrections: "probe-driven PHI2 cycle" → probe-requested and oito-generated; the CPU does not "release its pins" at a hold edge; the duplicate "Picture over HDMI — unblocked" row in §16.2.1 deleted; and the §13 claim that acknowledgement "is explicit enough without a pin" narrowed to *stepping*, which is the only case where it was true.

**One more tooling defect, found while fixing these.** `tools-bus-inject.py` imported the bus model with `importlib`, which caches bytecode in `__pycache__` — so it injected a **stale model** and reported "0 regenerated" after `render_effect_matrix` had been rewritten. `tools-verify.py` was fixed for exactly this in round nine; **the lesson did not travel to the next script that needed it.** Now exec'd from source, like its sibling.

**Assessment.** Seven blockers is the highest since round twelve, and five of them were in text written in the two preceding rounds. The bus model again caught none — the decode and protocol defects are naming, polarity, transport and address-qualification problems, and a behavioural state machine does not see those. What did the work this round was the **structural verifier** (once the guards existed) and the **mutation harness**, which found three inadequate guards in its first run. The pattern worth naming: *the further this document goes, the more of the value comes from checks on the checks.*

### Z. Nineteenth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round18.md`, 2026-07-28)

Nine findings, five blockers. **Four of the five are in the probe protocol invented across rounds 16 and 17**, and one of those is the most serious kind: a **safety claim the hardware cannot honour**.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D458** | **R18-BUS4 [blocker] — the timeout and reset "fail-safe" were impossible.** After acquisition the probe's `74LVC245` output enables are controlled by its **MCU**, and oito had no signal that disables them. Yet the spec said that on a 10 ms timeout oito releases `BE` — **enabling the CPU against a still-driving external master** — and that `RES̄` forces "every driver off", which oito cannot do either. The normal release path is safe only *because the probe cooperates*; a timeout exists precisely for when it does not. | **Accepted, and answered in hardware rather than in prose.** **`DBACK` becomes the physical grant: every probe address, data and `R/W̄` transceiver has its `OE` tied to it.** `DBACK` low ⇒ all probe drivers off, in hardware, whatever the MCU is doing. That makes each recovery path real: at timeout oito **drops `DBACK` first**, so the drivers are electrically off before `BE` is released; `RES̄` pulls `DBACK` low **asynchronously**; a **100 kΩ pull-down** means a dead oito cannot be misread as a grant. Every timer is now **cancelled by a named signal transition** rather than by inferring firmware state, because "the probe did not act" is not implementable. *This is the second time this project has published a mechanism that could not exist — the invented `DBḠ` reply phase was the first. The tell is the same both times: a capability described without naming the wire that carries it.* |
| **D459** | **R18-BUS1 [blocker] — probe acceptance had three incompatible definitions**: an old falling-edge/`cycle-complete` equation, a `DBACK` rising edge in the generated matrix, and a falling-edge sampling rule. Worse, the matrix accepted **any** `DBACK` rise while `owner = probe` and the acquisition acknowledgement *is* such a rise — so it could fire a `reg_commit`, FIFO pop or pointer increment before any transfer was requested. The matrix also listed an **`ext_commit`** effect defined nowhere, contradicting the rule that external strobes are scheduled and never gated. | **Accepted.** **One definition: the falling edge of the PHI2 cycle oito *generates* for a request**, at step 0 of that tick, qualified by **`probe_transfer_pending`** — which the acquisition rise cannot satisfy. `cycle-complete` is deleted everywhere. `ext_commit` is removed from the model: the external strobe has **no** acceptance-gated effect, which is what the neighbouring rule already said. |
| **D460** | **R18-BUS2 [blocker] — `DBḠ` was not uniquely decodable.** ACQUIRE was "low ≥1 µs then two short high pulses", but **the first of those pulses is already a complete STEP command** — so oito had to step the CPU during an ownership request or change its specified step behaviour, with no inter-pulse gap, deadline or rollback. In OWNED, "released high ≥1 µs" meant relinquish while the probe was also said to release `DBḠ` after every transfer, so **an idle host was indistinguishable from a deliberate release**. | **Accepted.** A complete state machine keyed on **mode plus duration**, with `t_LONG`, `t_SHORT`, a **`t_DEC` = 2 µs decision window** and `t_GAP`. STEP becomes irrevocable only at the *end* of the window, which is what makes it prefix-free with ACQUIRE — at a stated cost of **2 µs of single-step latency**, disclosed rather than hidden. In OWNED, REQUEST and RELINQUISH are separated by **duration**, and a 900 ns–1 µs pulse is explicitly **malformed** and ignored. `debug_select_suppress` is stated **deasserted throughout OWNED** — otherwise the ENTER DEBUG that precedes acquisition would make `bus_cycle_valid` false and every live access decode to nothing. |
| **D461** | **R18-BUS3 [blocker] — the handshake could acknowledge stale data and remove data before sampling.** `DBACK` was already high after acquisition, so an immediately-ready transfer had no new acknowledging edge; and `access_phase` was released **at** `owner_transfer_accept`, so `CĒ`/`ŌĒ` deasserted on the very edge announcing validity — the memory could stop driving before the probe sampled. The text also said oito drives D0–D7 for a probe read *and* that "oito does not drive the bus while `owner = probe`". | **Accepted, and simplified rather than patched.** **PHI2 is the ready signal**, because oito generates it: in OWNED, oito emits cycles on request and simply **withholds the completing edge until data is valid**. A stalled VRAM read waits there, so **the probe stalls and the CPU does not**, and no second ready wire is needed. Data and select are held **`t_PHD` ≥ 20 ns past the accepting edge**. "oito does not drive the bus" is narrowed to the **master-side** address, bank and `R/W̄`; its slave-side D0–D7 drive on reads is required. |
| **D462** | **R18-BUS5 [blocker] — the boot overlay still enabled cartridge ROM underneath it.** The round-sixteen repair qualified the *boot* select by `BOOT_SRC` and never masked the overlapping *cartridge* select, so `cart_F` still covered all of `$C000–$FFFF`. A read at `$E000–$FFFF` asserted **boot-flash output and cartridge `ROM_CĒ` together** with `BOOT_SRC = 1`, and enabled the cartridge **beneath the on-die stub** with `BOOT_SRC = 0` — falsifying the exclusivity invariant stated in the same table. | **Accepted.** The fixed window **splits**: `cart_F_low` (`$C000–$DFFF`, always visible) and `cart_F_high` (`$E000–$FFFF`, visible **only** when `BOOT_SRC = 2`). All three `BOOT_SRC` cases are expanded, and the invariant now names the **internal stub** as a data source — it is one, and the previous version covered only external pins. |
| **D463** | **R18-PIN1 [high] — `DBACK` had no reset, power-off or probe-side electrical contract**, and it invalidated §13's hot-plug argument: that argument rests on the *bus transceivers* defaulting high-Z, and `DBACK` is a direct console output into the MCU, not behind them. | **Accepted.** Added to §6.3's reset table and §13's electrical table with invalid-rail, reset, normal and probe-power-off states; **≈33 Ω series resistor into a clamp-tolerant input, 100 kΩ pull-down**. The hot-plug statement is re-scoped: permitted on the bus lines, **qualified on `DBACK`**, which is named as the counterexample the previous generalisation created. |
| **D464** | **R18-DOC1 [medium] — the added pin and header positions did not propagate.** BOM item 11, §6.4, §13, a §16.2 gate, `diagrams/system-block.svg` and the marketing page all still said 40-pin / 126 signals / 36 header signals, and the package arithmetic disagreed with itself (151 used vs ~150 totalled vs 25 spare). | **Accepted, and moved into the model.** `pin_total_est`, `pin_spare`, `header_positions` and `header_signals` are **derived** and checked, so the six locations cannot drift again. *The SVG mattered most: it is inlined into the normative HTML, so a stale caption ships as contract.* |
| **D465** | **R18-REG1 [medium] — the timeout wrote an undefined "`STATUS`-adjacent debug error state"**: no register, bit, reset value, clear rule or interrupt behaviour. | **Accepted by removing it rather than allocating one.** The fault lives in **`DBG_FAULT`, a JTAG debug-space bit**, and is **not CPU-visible**: `STATUS`'s reserved bits stay reserved, nothing in `$4009–$400F` is allocated, and the emulator has nothing to model. *A debug-adapter fault is not a machine state, so it does not get machine state.* |
| **D466** | **R18-VAL1 [high] — the probe gate listed scenarios but not the load-bearing properties.** It could pass while the protocol still stepped the CPU on an ownership prefix, accepted at acquisition, sampled after `CĒ` release, or released the CPU into a stuck driver. | **Accepted.** Eight explicit assertions added, including prefix-free decoding with **malformed and truncated frames**, an **acquisition-with-no-transfer** trace, data surviving `t_PHD`, and — the one that matters most — **`DBACK` gating measured with the probe MCU deliberately frozen after driver enable.** |

Plus the wording corrections: the cartridge table's "probe-driven" → probe-requested/oito-generated; the remaining "CPU's write data" → the current-owner write-data latch; the 219 ns figure attributed to a **held external read** rather than an oito port read, which does not select cartridge NOR; and pin 42 relabelled a **connector-position reserve**, since a grounded position is not a signal spare.

**Assessment, and it is not comfortable.** Five blockers, four of them in two rounds' worth of my own new protocol text, and the pattern is consistent: **each round I specified one more layer of a debug bus and each round the review found that the layer below it did not exist.** Round 16 invented an acknowledgement with no wire; round 17 gave it a wire but no framing, no data hold, and a fail-safe the hardware could not perform; round 18 fixes those and the honest expectation is that round 19 finds something in the framing.

The general lesson is narrower than "go slower". It is that **a protocol is not specified until every claim in it names the signal that carries it** — and that the failure mode has a reliable tell, which is a capability sentence with no wire in it. The `DBACK`-gates-`OE` fix is the first thing in this area that is safe *by construction* rather than by cooperation, which is the standard the rest of it should have been held to from the start.

### AA. Twentieth adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round19.md`, 2026-07-28)

Eleven findings, seven blockers. **The first one is the worst thing I have done in this project: I fixed a safety hole by silently breaking two documented capabilities, and described the result as "safe by construction".**

#### D467 — R19-BUS1 [blocker]: the blanket `OE` gate

v0.20 said *"every probe address, data and `R/W̄` transceiver has its `OE` tied to `DBACK`"*, and I wrote it up as the first thing in this area that was safe by construction rather than by cooperation. Three things were wrong with it:

1. **A `74LVC245` `/OE` disconnects *both directions*.** So `DBACK` low — which is every moment the probe does not own the bus — removed the **receive** path that §13 requires for passive sniffing: address/data/`R/W̄` at full bus rate, `BANK_SELECT` and ghost-port shadowing, address-plus-SYNC breakpoint matching. All of it, in normal run.
2. **It disabled the data bus during instruction jamming**, which deliberately runs with **`BE` high and `owner = cpu`**, and therefore with `DBACK` low. Jamming was one of the two capabilities the round-16 generalisation existed to preserve.
3. **`/OE` is active-low.** A literal wire from an active-high `DBACK` would have *enabled* the transceivers exactly when the grant was absent — the inverse of the intended behaviour, in a sentence I called a hardware guarantee.

**Disposition.** `/OE` is tied **enabled** on every device and **`DIR` is the gated term**, with `DBACK` + `BE` distinguishing the modes: `DBACK` low ⇒ drive nothing; `DBACK` high + `BE` high ⇒ **jam, D0–D7 only**; `DBACK` high + `BE` low ⇒ **owned**, address/bank/`R/W̄` and data on writes. A six-row per-transceiver table replaces the sentence, with `DIR` defaulting to **receive**, explicit `probe_drive_*` equations that each contain `DBACK`, a rule that direction changes only while not driving, and jam turn-off ordered **before** suppression release. **Receiver availability and outbound-drive authorisation are now separate things**, which is what the sentence conflated.

*The verifier guard I added last round demanded the `OE` gate — so it encoded the defective design **as the requirement** and would have failed the correct one. It now checks that every `probe_drive_*` term contains `DBACK` and that no `OE` is gated at all. A guard can be a bug report about the specification or about itself, and this one was the second kind.*

#### The rest

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D468** | **R19-BUS2 [blocker] — §13 still published the withdrawn immediate-step protocol.** Every pulse under 1 µs stepped on its falling edge, back-to-back at ~500 kHz, no dead band — so **ACQUIRE's first pulse stepped the CPU**, the exact round-18 defect, with the two sections disagreeing on action time by 2 µs. `DBACK` was also still called "acknowledge/ready" in §§6.3, 12 and 13 after §11.3 made PHI2 the ready signal. | **Accepted.** §11.3's state machine is the **sole** definition; §13 keeps only what is specific to it (oito gates PHI2, one cycle per committed STEP, the ≈7-cycle threshold rationale, and why stepping needs no acknowledge line while ownership does). `DBACK` is renamed the **outbound-drive grant** everywhere. |
| **D469** | **R19-BUS3 [blocker] — ACQUIRE committed before it could be decoded.** "Two short pulses", irrevocable at the **second rising edge** — at which the second pulse's width is unknowable, so the probe could still hold it into the dead band or into `t_LONG`. The malformed-frame rule also called a post-`t_DEC` pulse malformed, which would have required retroactively undoing a committed STEP. | **Accepted.** ACQUIRE commits at the **second falling edge**, once the width is known. A pulse beginning after `t_DEC` **starts a new command**. Intervals are half-open — `t_SHORT` = [100 ns, 900 ns), dead band [900 ns, 1 µs), `t_LONG` = [1 µs, ∞) — so behaviour is defined *at* the endpoints, which it was not. Committed actions are scheduled at the next PHI2 boundary. |
| **D470** | **R19-BUS4 [blocker] — the literal `access_phase` still released at the acceptance edge**, recreating the read-data race the handshake prose had just fixed: every `CĒ`/`ŌĒ` predicate reads that definition, and the probe samples *on* that edge. | **Accepted.** Release is **owner-specific**: `probe_access_release = accepting edge + t_PHD`, guaranteed **at the header connector** so ribbon delay is inside the budget. The back-to-back precharge is **restated rather than inherited** — 40.5 ns for probe transfers against the CPU's 60.5, because the release moves later. |
| **D471** | **R19-BUS5 [blocker] — the register/font-write rule still named a per-transfer `DBACK` rise**, an edge that no longer exists: `DBACK` rises once at acquisition and stays high. | **Accepted.** Replaced with the canonical condition. |
| **D472** | **R19-BUS6 [blocker] — timeouts returned the bus without setting `owner`.** They did `DBACK` low → `BE` released → `RDY` released, leaving `owner = probe` while the CPU physically drove — and `bus_cycle_valid`, the decode and `owner_transfer_accept` all read that register. Separately, the acquisition timer was on the **probe** side with nothing cancelling acquisition inside oito, so a slow oito could grant a bus nobody was waiting for. | **Accepted.** A **six-step recovery** used by every timeout and by `RES̄`: `DBACK` low → `owner = none` → discard pending state → `owner = cpu` → release `BE` → release `RDY`, ordered so drivers are off before the decode can select and the decode is neutral before the CPU returns. **All three timers move to oito**, which makes a late grant impossible by construction and keeps `DBG_FAULT` consistent — only oito-side events can set an oito register. |
| **D473** | **R19-DEC1 [blocker] — the internal boot stub was named in the exclusivity invariant with no predicate.** `internal_stub_selected` had no address qualification, no `bus_cycle_valid`, no `access_phase`, no read/write term — so the invariant was not evaluable, and **jamming could not be shown to have every ordinary source off** before the probe drives D0–D7. | **Accepted.** `stub_select`/`stub_read` get the same positive-predicate discipline as every external memory, and the invariant is restated over **six evaluable predicates** so it can be machine-checked. A write into the overlay with `BOOT_SRC = 0` is stated to be accepted and discarded — mask ROM, nothing latches, no strobe. |
| **D474** | **R19-BUS7 [high] — pulse bounds, timer cancellation and the idle-ownership requirement disagreed.** The transfer procedure accepted "high for < 1 µs", including dead-band pulses and omitting the 100 ns floor; timers cancelled on "the next request edge", which a stuck-high pulse would exploit; and §16.2 demanded "arbitrarily long idle ownership" while §11.3 bounded it at 10 ms. | **Accepted.** The transfer procedure uses **`t_SHORT` by name**; timers cancel only on a **validated request** — the falling edge of a `t_SHORT` pulse; and ownership is **bounded**, with §16.2 rewritten to prove *no premature timeout, expiry at the bound, and safe recovery* instead of the opposite. |
| **D475** | **R19-VAL1 [high] — the validation gate proved the wrong property.** It required the blanket `OE` gate, and so would have certified the defect of D467. | **Accepted.** Rewritten to validate **console-facing drive enables per signal and per mode** across all six transceiver modes, with frozen-MCU tests in **every** mode rather than only after driver enable, plus `/OE`-and-`DIR` polarity, direction-change contention, `owner`-before-`BE`, and `t_PHD` at the connector. |
| **D476** | **R19-REG1 [medium] — `DBG_FAULT` was a name, not a field.** No instruction, bit, width, reset value, set causes, coincidence rule, or persistence — and its "console side records it" was inconsistent with a probe-side timer. | **Accepted.** `DBG_STATUS` at TAP IR `0x1C`, 8 bits, reset `$00`, `DBG_FAULT` = bit 0, sticky, set by **oito-side timeouts only**, cleared on read through JTAG, **set beats clear-on-read**, and **persists after recovery** — which is the only time it is useful. |
| **D477** | **R19-DOC1 [medium] — §13 said "all 40 electrical positions" on a 42-position header**, and its hot-plug paragraph contradicted itself, citing a §2.1 sentence about a **controller-port short circuit** as though it were connector policy. | **Accepted.** 40 → 42, and **insertion policy separated from signal power-off tolerance**: console-off-only is the supported and validated sequence; power-off tolerance is required regardless because a probe can lose power while attached. "Permitted but untested" is deleted — it is not a support status. |

**Assessment.** Four consecutive rounds of probe-protocol blockers, and this is the round where the pattern stopped being "the layer below did not exist" and became **"the fix broke something else"**. That is a worse failure than the previous three: an incomplete specification is visibly incomplete, whereas D467 read like a finished safety argument. The tell was available and I did not use it — **a hardware gate that makes a capability impossible is not a fix, and the way to notice is to enumerate the modes the signal appears in before asserting a rule over all of them.** The six-row transceiver table is what that enumeration looks like; it should have preceded the one-sentence rule rather than replacing it a round later.

### AB. Twenty-first adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round20.md`, 2026-07-28)

Seven findings, four blockers, all four in the transceiver design written **one round earlier**. Alongside them, the owner asked for a diagram audit and a judgement on whether the bus contract needed its own chapter. Both are answered here.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D478** | **R20-BUS4 [blocker] — I invented probe-driven `BANK0–BANK5` pins that do not exist**, while §6.3 said oito drives them "only while `owner = oito`" and even that "the CPU drives them". The CPU has no `BANK` pins and they are not on the 42-position header. Read literally, **ordinary CPU cartridge reads left the cartridge's high address bits undriven.** | **Accepted.** `BANK0–5` are **cartridge mapper outputs, not host-bus-master lines**, and §6.3 splits them into their own row: driven by oito for **every** cartridge access whoever owns the host bus, sourced per §11.0 — masked `BANK_SELECT` in the switchable window, the fixed bank above it, oito's own address during a steal, and the same mapping during a probe access. `BANK` is removed from every probe-drive term. *The tell I missed: I added a column to a table without asking whether the signals in it are on the connector.* |
| **D479** | **R20-BUS1 [blocker] — `DBACK` had no jam-mode lifecycle.** `jam_drive = DBACK && BE` requires `DBACK` high during a jam, but §6.3 said it is low unless `owner = probe` and only acquisition ever raised it — so **`jam_drive` was false throughout DEBUG and instruction jamming was impossible.** Adding a rise was not enough: the first-transfer timer started on any `DBACK` rise, so a jam grant would have armed an ownership timer. | **Accepted, in the new §6.9.** `DBACK` is defined in **every** state — NORMAL, DEBUG, jam, turnaround, ACQUIRE, OWNED, RELINQUISH, timeout, EXIT, reset — with the jam grant rising **after** `debug_select_suppress` and falling **before** it. **Ownership timers are qualified on `owner = probe`**, not on any rise. Every mode change passes through **`DBACK` = 0 for ≥ `t_TA`**, so jam→OWNED is revoke → change → re-grant rather than a bare `BE` edge reinterpreting a live grant. |
| **D480** | **R20-BUS2 [blocker] — the always-enabled bidirectional transceiver had no realisable turnaround.** With `/OE` tied low there is **no high-impedance state**, so `DIR` *was* the drive, and my rule "`DIR` may change only in the tick after `probe_drive_*` has gone low" had no signal that could create that tick.
