A legal read→write reverses the D transceiver **while the read target still drives for `t_PHD`**. | **Accepted, and the hardware changes.** The probe uses **separate receive and drive buffers** per group: a `74LVC245` strapped console→probe with `/OE` tied low — always available, never driving toward the console — plus a `74LVC244`-class buffer whose `/OE` **is** the drive term. That yields a genuine high-Z state, so **break-before-make becomes a mechanism**: `probe_write` is latched only at a turnaround, and `t_TA ≥ 1 PHI2 cycle = 139.7 ns` is published for read→write, write→read, jam→owned, owned→release and reset. *Two buffers per group is more parts on an accessory, which is the cheap place for them, and it was the only one of the three options that produces a real not-driving interval.* |
| **D481** | **R20-BUS3 [blocker] — one recovery sequence could not describe both timeout and reset.** My six steps end at `owner = cpu` with `BE` and `RDY` released — a return-to-execution — while reset requires `owner = none` for the **whole** time `RES̄` is asserted. It also discarded "any outstanding `access_phase`" while the takeover contract says the CPU's transaction is *preserved*. | **Accepted.** §6.9.3 publishes **two** transitions. Timeout/relinquish: revoke drive, discard **probe-owned** state only, **re-present** the preserved CPU phase, `owner = cpu`, then `BE`, then `RDY`. Reset abort: revoke drive, `owner = none` **and hold it**, clear **both** CPU and probe transaction state, selects inactive, `owner = cpu` only at a separate reset-release event. **`cpu_access_phase` and `probe_access_phase` are now named separately**, and re-presentation is stated to produce **exactly one** falling `CĒ` edge — the ambiguity spanned three behaviours with different FRAM/NOR consequences. |
| **D482** | **R20-TIM1 [high] — the precharge derivation contradicted its own timeline.** "Wait ≥1.3 ticks **after release**, giving 60.5 − 20 = 40.5 ns" is incoherent: waiting 1.3 ticks after release gives 60.5 ns, and the subtraction only works if the next select is fixed relative to **acceptance**. Neither reading was connected to the request protocol. | **Accepted, and the number was wrong in both direction and value.** Derived from the request timeline: a probe transfer is **not pipelined** — its next request cannot begin until the previous one is sampled — so the minimum `CĒ`-high interval is `t_SHORT` + 1.3 ticks = **160.5 ns**, *larger* than the CPU's 60.5 ns rather than smaller. One value is now used everywhere, validated against one connector-level waveform for all four orders. |
| **D483** | **R20-PIN1 [high] — always-enabled buffers do not by themselves give power-off safety.** "`DIR` defaults to receive so no line is driven" establishes the direction **while powered** only. | **Accepted.** Three named requirements replace the argument: **`Ioff`/partial-power-down** as an explicit datasheet property of the exact device; MCU-side injection limiting when the MCU rail is absent but the buffer is powered; and — because `/OE` tied low **prevents** local isolation — a **power-good gate on the receive `/OE`** if the chosen part cannot guarantee `Ioff`, accepting that sniffing stops during a brownout. **Unused channels must be terminated**: the `R/W̄` group uses one of eight. The exact `74LVC245` suffix is one of the ledger's 17 family-page entries, so an existing gap is now load-bearing. |
| **D484** | **R20-VAL1 [high] — the rewritten gate was internally impossible**, requiring "receivers available for sniffing in **every** mode" while the normative table correctly says D0–D7 receive is unavailable during an OWNED write and nothing is available unpowered. | **Accepted.** The gate now requires **the receive paths appropriate to each mode**, and adds the jam-grant edges, timer non-interference, the jam→ACQUIRE turnaround, reset holding `owner = none`, preserved-phase re-presentation as one `CĒ` edge, oito-driven `BANK` throughout, and the 160.5 ns interval. |

#### D485 — a bus arbitration chapter, and a diagram audit

**On the chapter: yes, and it is now §6.9.** The ownership contract had accumulated across **five** sections — §6.5, §6.6, §8.3/§8.5, §11.3, §13 — and this is where more blockers have been found than anywhere else in the document. §6.9 is written as the **single home** for it, not a summary beside the others: it holds the two-bus distinction, the `DBACK` lifecycle, the transceiver architecture and the two recovery transitions, and the other sections **reference** it rather than restating anything. *That constraint matters: a chapter that duplicated the contract would have created exactly the two-rules-for-one-question defect this project keeps finding.*

**On the diagrams: three of six were stale, and one materially so.**

| Diagram | Finding |
|---|---|
| `compositor-arbitration.svg` | **VRAM priority order was wrong** — it showed blitter second and CPU/DMA third, inverting the actual display > CPU/DMA > blitter. The per-frame budget was also v0.1-era: sprite patterns "≈29–57 K" against a real 113–115 K, and "Display total ≈175–200 K (50–56 %)" against **232–259 K (65–72 %)**. Both corrected from the model's figures |
| `system-block.svg` | **"116 signal pins"** — eleven short of the derived 127, and `DBACK` was missing from the header line. `RDY`/`BE` were described as one thing; they are now separated into the three-party wired-AND and the request-not-grant |
| `audio-chain.svg` | claimed a **"polyphase FIR · 64 × 32 taps"** ASRC — dimensions that are a **rejected baseline** and now design outputs. Replaced with `P × T — NOT YET DESIGNED` and a pointer to the §16.2.1 deferral |
| `memory-map.svg`, `raster-timing.svg`, `boot-sequence.svg` | checked against the model and the current text; **current** |

**A new generated diagram, `bus-ownership.svg`**, is emitted by `tools-bus-model.py` — so §6.9's figure carries the derived `t_TA` and cannot drift from the model. And **four stale patterns from this audit are now in the `DIAGRAM` guard**: a signal-pin count in the 110–126 range, ASRC tap counts, "2 · Blitter", and the old budget wording. *An SVG is inlined into the normative HTML, so a figure's caption ships as contract — the guard existed since round 13 and had only the patterns known then.*

**Assessment.** Five consecutive rounds of probe-protocol blockers. The distinctive failure this time is D478: I added a column to a hardware table without checking whether its signals exist on the connector, and simultaneously left a clause that would have broken ordinary cartridge banking. That is the same shape as the invented `DBḠ` reply phase and the invented PTC part — **asserting a signal without checking it is real** — and the third occurrence of it. The lesson recorded last round was *enumerate the modes before asserting a rule over them*; the one this round adds is narrower and should have been obvious: **before writing that an agent drives a signal, confirm the signal reaches that agent.**

### AC. Twenty-second adversarial review (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round21.md`, 2026-07-30)

Ten findings, four blockers. The review found that v0.22's separate-buffer repair still joined an always-enabled receiver output to an MCU push-pull output, inverted the meaning of active-low `/OE`, drove the jam data bus during CPU stack writes, and specified a direction turnaround that no protocol could perform. It also showed that the new arbitration chapter had not yet defined the third owner, `oito`.

All dispositions preserve the existing feature set. They select and specify hardware and timing needed to implement capabilities already claimed; none adds, changes or removes a system capability.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D486** | **R21-BUS1 [blocker] — receive outputs and MCU drive outputs shared one net.** An always-enabled `74LVC245` output and an MCU GPIO configured as output are two push-pull sources connected together. Propagation delay guarantees disagreement during a transition, and an error guarantees sustained contention. | **Accepted; the topology changes.** Receive and drive are electrically separate. Four exact `74LVC245APW,118` devices feed 25 dedicated input GPIO. Eight *different* GPIO feed a `74LVC244APW,118` for D0–D7. Three cascaded `74LVC595APW,118` devices store A0–A15 and `R/W̄` from three serial-control GPIO. The other 12 header signals are direct. The accounting is exact: **37 + 8 + 3 = 48 GPIO**. No net joins two push-pull outputs. The QFN pin/PIO-window map remains a measured/implementation gate rather than being inferred from the count. |
| **D487** | **R21-BUS2 [blocker] — the active-high drive terms were described as active-low `/OE` levels.** Read literally, an authorised high disabled the driver and a revoked low enabled it. No inverter, fail-safe pull-up or propagation budget existed. | **Accepted in hardware equations.** `nDBACK = NOT(DBACK)`, `/OE_ARW = nDBACK OR BE`, and `/OE_DATA = nDBACK OR (BE XOR R/W̄)`, implemented by exact `74LVC1G04GV`, `74LVC1G86GV,125` and `74LVC2G32DP,125`. Every `/OE` has a passive pull-up; gate/buffer delay occurs before connector-level `t_TA` starts; `DBACK` stays low across `BE` changes. The MCU supplies values but cannot authorise drive. |
| **D488** | **R21-BUS3 [blocker] — jam drive contended with the CPU's register-capture stack writes.** `jam_drive = DBACK && BE` was true for the whole jam, including `PHA`/`PHX`/`PHY`/`PHP`, when the CPU drives D0–D7 and the probe must receive. Entry and exit were not state transitions. | **Accepted.** Physical `R/W̄` is a hardware input to `/OE_DATA`: in jam, CPU read/fetch (`R/W̄ = 1`) enables injection and CPU write (`R/W̄ = 0`) disables it while the always-on receiver captures. §6.9.3 now defines entry, one-cycle STEP, ACQUIRE-from-jam and EXIT; `debug_select_suppress` strictly contains the drive window. The validation gate requires all four capture pushes and four restore instructions with setup/hold and no overlap. |
| **D489** | **R21-BUS4 [blocker] — intra-OWNED turnaround depended on lowering `DBACK`, but nothing could request or announce it.** Re-granting would also restart the first-transfer timer. | **Accepted by removing the false dependency.** `DBACK` stays high for the entire OWNED interval. Read→write waits for the selected target to become connector-high-Z plus `t_TA`, then latches `R/W̄ = 0` with data already stable. Write→read latches `R/W̄ = 1`, waits for the D driver to become connector-high-Z plus `t_TA`, then requests the read. The first-transfer timer starts only on the explicit `ACQUIRE_WAIT → OWNED_GRANT` transition; a jam rise and local turnaround match no timer start. Same-direction accesses retain the separately derived 160.5 ns `CĒ`-high precharge. |
| **D490** | **R21-BUS5 [high] — stale normative copies in §§6.3, 11 and 13 contradicted §6.9.** They retained the old bidirectional population, old MCU-controlled enables, old recovery order and “complete or suspend.” | **Accepted by making §6.9 the sole ownership/electrical contract.** §11 keeps only `DBḠ` command encoding, timers, transfer carrier and decode; it references §6.9 for handoff. §13 keeps capability-specific behaviour and the header contract. §6.3's `DBACK` row now distinguishes always-on receive from outbound active-low enables. Every active internal operation **completes; none suspends**. |
| **D491** | **R21-TIM1 [high] — release paths ordered signals but never waited `t_TA`; reset could enable the CPU while probe outputs were still turning off.** | **Accepted with a named release-wait state.** Probe→CPU and oito→CPU make old outputs high-Z, set `owner = none`, hold every select inactive, and wait connector-level `t_TA` before `owner = cpu`, `BE`, then `RDY`. Reset lowers `DBACK` asynchronously and keeps `BE` low until the same wait completes; `owner` remains `none` for the whole reset assertion. The preserved CPU access is re-presented only on non-reset recovery. |
| **D492** | **R21-BUS6 [high] — `owner = oito` had no complete transition, priority or acceptance event.** OAM's host read and later VRAM write were conflated, and simultaneous OAM/PCM/probe requests were unordered. | **Accepted in §6.9.1.** Total order: reset; finish active internal work; committed debug/probe request; OAM; PCM; CPU. CPU↔oito and CPU↔probe transitions list request, safe hold, `BE`, `owner = none`, high-Z wait, grant, release and resume. `owner_transfer_accept(oito)` is the PHI2 falling edge that latches a PCM cartridge byte or OAM system-RAM byte; OAM's later VRAM write is explicitly a private-bus grant. The generated effect matrix now carries that definition. |
| **D493** | **R21-BOM1 [high] — the BOM, current budget and parts ledger described the superseded circuit.** | **Accepted.** BOM item 11f records four receive transceivers, one data driver, three storage drivers and three enable gates. Exact Nexperia order/packing codes and official exact-product sources are in the ledger with `Ioff` and production evidence. The controller's `74LVC244A` is also closed to `74LVC244APW,118`. The accessory allocation is **160 mA MCU/USB + 60 mA logic + 30 mA misc + 50 mA margin = 300 mA**, with measured maximum still a gate. The parts queues become 30 active / 4 removed, 4 incomplete OPNs, 15 family-page sources and 6 datasheet-ordering checks. |
| **D494** | **R21-VAL1 [high] — validation encoded the invalid architecture and omitted MCU-side contention, polarity, jam writes, local turnaround, release wait and oito ownership.** | **Accepted and rewritten.** The probe gate now requires the 48-pin/PIO map, both sides of every buffer, the literal `/OE` equations, frozen-MCU revocation, every jam/owned/turnaround/recovery/reset/power state, all master transitions, arbitration collisions, oito acceptance edges, power sequencing and ≤300 mA measurement. `tools-verify.py` is updated for outbound-only gating and exact Nexperia product pages; its probe-bank check is constrained to actual drive assertions rather than unrelated pin-table prose. Round-21 mutations cover shared-output topology, polarity, jam-write exclusion, release wait and oito acceptance/priority, and a dedicated mutation proves the constrained probe-bank guard still fires. |
| **D495** | **R21-DOC1 [low] — duplicated `access_phase` sentence; metadata and diagrams would otherwise remain one revision behind.** | **Accepted.** The duplicate is removed. `bus-ownership.svg` now shows the two active-low equations, jam read/write split, `ACQUIRE_WAIT`, total priority and concrete oito acceptance events; `system-block.svg` names the exact controller part and isolated probe receive/drive logic. Spec/README advance to **v0.23 and twenty-two reviews**, with the ledger and warning counts updated. |

**Why the output-latch solution was selected.** A larger MCU would move the problem without resolving fail-safe authorisation; disabling the receivers while driving would sacrifice passive observation of the probe's own waveform and add another unsafe enable; a CPLD would add a toolchain and configuration dependency. Three commodity storage registers consume only three GPIO, preserve all 37 simultaneous inputs, have independent storage clocks so shifting cannot disturb the bus, and expose active-low output enables that the console grant can revoke in gates.

**Diagram audit.** Only `bus-ownership.svg` and `system-block.svg` contain the changed contract. The other SVGs describe video, audio, memory, raster and boot behaviour and require no update. The ownership SVG remains generated by `tools-bus-model.py`; its checked-in source and published inline copy were regenerated in lockstep by the required build.

### AD. Validation-register audit and staging (2026-07-30)

This was a documentation and closure-planning audit, not another adversarial hardware review. It changes no console capability, interface, timing promise or selected architecture. It asks a narrower question: whether every item in §16.2 still represented a distinct validation task and whether each could occur at the stage the heading claimed.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D496** | **The “hardware validation” register mixed five kinds of obligation.** Its 42 rows included physical measurements, unfinished engineering, SDK fixtures, procurement evidence and HDMI legal/compliance work, all described as gating schematic freeze or tapeout. Several measurements explicitly required fabricated oito silicon, while HDMI ATC requires a representative finished product. | **Accepted; §16.2 becomes a staged closure register.** The 42 raw rows are consolidated into 18 gates assigned to SDK freeze, schematic freeze, RTL/tapeout sign-off, prototype/post-silicon characterization or production release. A cross-stage row names separate evidence at each stage. Post-silicon measurements no longer pretend to be tapeout prerequisites; pre-tapeout closure is simulation/formal/STA evidence. |
| **D497** | **Several rows were duplicate views of one task.** CH7035B input/audio/I²S/HPD items repeated its qualification; probe R/W̄ and RP2350 mapping repeated the full probe gate; FRAM timing repeated the held-read/write campaigns; CPU timing and prog8 fixtures were fragmented; four parts rows overlapped. | **Accepted by consolidation without deletion.** Every former criterion survives as a named subcriterion inside the relevant qualification plan. In particular, consolidation preserves the normative external-write obligation—not merely its capture list: **both oito and the probe must defer an `RDY` halt past a cycle emitting an external write strobe**. The front-page list now carries only the 18 independent gates. The ASRC filter and §16.2.1 deferral are one obligation, removing the 43rd front-page entry that duplicated a §16.2 row. |
| **D498** | **The CPU-port row required validation of the rejected 6.6 ns one-cycle request path.** §6.5 already rejects that path and selects the 53.1 ns two-cycle capture. A path that no implementation uses cannot be a sign-off target. | **Accepted.** Pre-tapeout timing closes the selected 53.1 ns address-to-capture, 84.7 ns address-to-RDY and 31.6 ns PCM/RDY paths. The 6.6 ns figure remains only as the documented reason the one-cycle access is rejected. |
| **D499** | **Several criteria named the wrong evidence.** A “redrawn waveform” is documentation, an undesigned ASRC has no candidate to validate, current-limit arithmetic is not an inrush test, and “within the following cycle” did not define the breakpoint interval. | **Accepted.** The bus row requires measured falling-edge waveforms; ASRC explicitly separates design completion from five-part candidate acceptance; `TPS2553-1` separates datasheet/tolerance calculation from prototype inrush and limit testing; and breakpoint timing now names the qualifying sample, following hold edge, phase and PVT. |
| **D500** | **Parts and scope status were overstated.** The lifecycle row implied all 30 active parts had no evidence, while the ledger records exact-page lifecycle evidence for five Nexperia interface parts and none for 25. The scope excluded every passive value while §16.2 required exact R-2R values to substantiate the analog amplitude. | **Accepted.** The lifecycle gate closes only the 25 open ledger records and states what the completed five mean; the orderability gate keeps the generated 4/15/6 queues in one row. The scope now excludes ordinary passive detail but retains values required to define or substantiate electrical-interface, safety or timing claims, with final selections in schematic/BOM evidence. |
| **D501** | **The front-page equality guard allowed extras.** It checked that every §16.2 row appeared but did not reject an additional row, which is how the ASRC deferral appeared twice while the text claimed the list was generated exactly. | **Accepted in the verifier contract.** The front-page sequence must equal the §16.2 Item sequence exactly: missing, extra, duplicated or reordered entries fail. The required-gate and parts-queue guards now locate the renamed §16.2 section, and mutation coverage includes the extra-row case. |

**Diagram audit.** No SVG depicts validation-stage taxonomy, release gates or the front-page register. The system, ownership, memory, raster, video/audio and boot diagrams describe capabilities and signal behaviour, none of which changed; no SVG update is required.

### AE. Marketing-page reconciliation with specification v0.23.1 (2026-07-30)

This audit reconciles `re8-spec.html` with the current normative specification after the adversarial-review campaign. It changes no console capability, interface, timing promise or selected architecture.

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **D502** | **The page still carried machine facts superseded after its v0.1 refresh.** It limited sprites to 32×32, described a plain retail cartridge as one chip, promised integer-only digital scaling, called the 3.5 mm line output a headphone jack, and claimed sub-frame response for every input device. | **Corrected.** Sprite sizes now reach 64×64 and the per-line pattern budget is disclosed; the retail population is NOR plus the required inverter; scaling matches §7.1's nearest-neighbour rule with integer factors preferred rather than required; the 3.5 mm connector is explicitly line level; and only the gamepad path carries the ≈2.5 ms acquisition figure, while PS/2 devices follow their report cadence. |
| **D503** | **The page presented provisional or unfinished work as shipping fact.** Unqualified claims covered HDMI picture/audio, the absent ASRC coefficient design, completed parts orderability/lifecycle evidence, a released SDK/debugger and firmware, and a manufactured, manufacturable platform. | **Corrected without changing the design target.** Digital A/V now states the HDMI licensing, compliance, scaler-qualification and ASRC gates; BOM copy distinguishes the factory-fresh rule from incomplete evidence; prog8 examples are labelled as the intended API and the missing toolchain fixtures are named; firmware and debugger copy describe plans rather than completed artefacts; and the CTA describes an unmanufactured, manufacturability-driven design. |
| **D504** | **The accessory and rear-panel copy contradicted its own evidence.** “Designed with 8BitDo” implied a real partnership despite the adjacent disclaimer, and the rear render was captioned as showing every output although it predates the current analog-audio connector set. | **Corrected in text only.** The accessories are explicitly imagined editions, and the caption says the current analog-audio layout is not represented. Per the owner's instruction, no render or image asset was changed. |

**Diagram and render audit.** These edits correct marketing wording only and do not change the normative design, so no SVG requires an update. Render and image assets were deliberately left untouched; the rear-panel caption now records their known scope.

### E. Minor/documentary

- **G28 — Marketing vs spec drift. [CLOSED 2026-07-26 — page and render regenerated.]** The marketing page (`re8-spec.html`) was rewritten against specification v0.1: CPU restated as 7.159 MHz, the 16-voice APU replaces "4-channel PSG", two BG planes, the "last standalone classic-bus CPU" and "zero flicker / zero latency" claims removed, cross-machine comparisons dropped, deep technical detail (memory map, pin budget, DB9 pinout, fab economics) moved to the technical specification document, and the page now links that specification. The **oito render was remodelled from LQFP-64 to LQFP-144** (20×20 mm body, 36 gull-wing leads per side at 0.5 mm pitch) in `RE-8 Console.blend` and `renders/re8-asic.png` regenerated. `publish/` is now built from source by `build-publish.py`, whose verification step fails on any recurrence of the stale claims — so this class of drift cannot silently return.
- **G53 — Editorial & internal-consistency defects. [RESOLVED 2026-07-25 — applied throughout.]** All eleven corrected in one pass; two needed a call, both recorded: **entry is `JMP ($FFFC)`** (the header's entry-point field is demoted to advisory tooling metadata, like the region byte), and **boot validates only the header checksum** (the ROM CRC32 is for tooling/`.sav` keying/self-test — never a boot-time 1MB scan). The original list follows for the record. (1) **§13 capability 1 contains stray Cyrillic** — "zero ROM/**софт** overhead" (should read "zero ROM/software overhead"). (2) **§15 restriction numbering repeats** — the list runs 1…14 then restarts at "12. VGA output carries no audio" and "13. Cartridge max 1MB"; renumber to 15/16. (3) **G28 punch-list cites "D7"** for the pin budget — the correct decision is **D19** (D7 is HDMI licensing). (4) **§2 BOM states the W65C02S as "4.0–8.0MHz @3.3V"** — the WDC datasheet's AC table gives **8 MHz at 3.3V** (4 MHz belongs to 2.5V); state simply "8MHz @3.3V" so the ~11% margin claim is unambiguous. (5) **PAL references are vestigial** post-D24 — §3 still lists the 17.734MHz PAL subcarrier and BOM #7 says "NTSC/PAL composite"; mark them as unused-capability-of-the-part, not console features. (6) **§6.1 contradicts §6.5/§6.6** — it claims sprites have "**zero flicker**" (§6.6/G37 drops the 33rd sprite per line) and that the blitter has a "**dedicated bus**" (§6.5: it shares the one VRAM bus at priority 2); it also carries un-labelled marketing comparisons ("vs Amiga 500 ~10,500, Lynx ~16,000") that G28 requires be framed as marketing. (7) **The §6.6 priority ladder is numbered "-1, 0, 1…"**, which renders incorrectly as a Markdown ordered list — convert to a table or relabel levels 1–9. (8) **Two different things are called "cursor"** — the text-layer cell cursor (`TEXT_CURSOR_X/Y`) and the mouse pointer (`CURSOR_*`, §9.2); rename one (e.g. `TEXT_CARET_*`) to avoid register-level confusion. (9) **Stale front-matter/closing wording** — the header still says "**Status: DRAFT — contains known gaps**" and §17's title says gaps are "**intentionally NOT fixed here**" while §18 closes with "the next stage is to resolve §17 items one by one"; all three predate the resolution campaign and feature freeze and should be restated (e.g. "gaps resolved through 2026-07; G40–G53 open from final review"). (10) **§11.1 vs §11.2 entry-point redundancy** — the handoff does `JMP ($FFFC)` while the header defines an **entry-point field ($18)**; state which is normative (and if both, what happens when they disagree). (11) **Full-ROM CRC32 at boot** (header $1C) would cost seconds on a 1MB cart at 7 MHz — clarify that the boot ROM validates only the header checksum, with the ROM CRC used by tooling/emulator.
- **G29 — Transcript-level errata. [RESOLVED 2026-07 — corrected in this spec.]** Each source-transcript slip is superseded by the canonical text: "12-bit parallel RGB-565" → **12-bit 4:4:4** (RGB565 is 16-bit; the palette is a 12-bit master, §6.6) — *footnote (G49): the transcript's "RGB-565" was most likely naming the **CH7035B's input format**, not the palette; that correction stands, and the actual oito→scaler mapping is now defined as RGB-888 with bit replication in §7.1*; "DVI governed by VESA" → dropped (DVI-D is royalty-free TMDS, §7.1); "zero-latency 1080p" → **the CH7035B frame-buffers and adds latency** (§7.1/§6.7); the "5–7MHz" vs "5–13MHz" pixel-clock ranges → **pinned at 5.365 / 6.702 MHz** (§6.7); "$4FFFF" → **$4FFF** ghost port (§5.1/§13). No open item — the transcripts are historical; this spec is authoritative.

| **Fifth adversarial review** (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round4.md`, 2026-07-27) | external hostile re-review of spec v0.4; **29 items (R4-TIM1…R4-SPEC3), 9 blockers** — frame-relative counter breaking APU cadence, fetch-slot assignment, DMA timing models, PCM FIFO refill arithmetic, inverted cartridge `OE#`, a non-existent CH7035B MCLK pin, an unimplementable ASRC, and premature HDMI licensing claims — sourced **§2.J** |
| **Fourth adversarial review** (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round3.md`, 2026-07-27) | external hostile re-review of spec v0.2; **49 items (R3-HW1…R3-SPEC4)**, a majority of them defects in the previous round's fixes — arbitration schedule self-consistency, sprite bandwidth, APU sample domains and resampling, `SAVE_CTRL` gating, firmware/recovery formats, a broken `far_call`, mis-counted IRQ-wrapper figures, halved analog amplitude, a LAST TIME BUY audio transmitter, and two impossible video fallbacks — sourced **§2.H** |
| **Third adversarial review** (`re8-sol-adversarial-review-round2.md`, 2026-07-27) | external hostile re-review of spec v0.1; **80 items (R2-HW1…R2-SPEC7)** covering SRAM/flash lifecycle, DIT4192 electricals, boot paging and PS/2 pin counts, the cartridge write gate, APU and blitter determinism, mid-frame latch points, PS/2 liveness and identification, cartridge integrity circularity, shared-pipeline video fallbacks, probe bus mastering and step framing, prog8 ROM-ability and multi-bank linking, and status wording — sourced **§2.G** |
| **Final technical review** (2026-07-25, internal) | post-feature-freeze audit of this document itself: register/memory-map cross-checks, pin-budget and bandwidth arithmetic, datasheet verification (W65C02S 3.3V rating, AD725 availability, CH7035B input formats), prog8/64tass toolchain verification, and internal-consistency sweep — sourced **G40–G53** |

Originally this document recorded source conflicts in §17 rather than adjudicating them. That campaign is complete: **G1–G39 were resolved one by one through 2026-07**, the feature set was frozen (§16, D33–D35), and the **final technical review's G40–G53 have since been closed** (D36–D44). Three adversarial reviews followed: **SOL-A1…SOL-H5** (§2.F), **R2-HW1…R2-SPEC7** (§2.G) producing spec **v0.2**, **R3-HW1…R3-SPEC4** (§2.H) producing **v0.3**, **R4-TIM1…R4-SPEC3** (§2.J) producing **v0.5**, **R5-VID1…R5-SPEC1** (§2.K) producing **v0.6**, **R6-CPU1…R6-ED4** (§2.L) producing **v0.7**, **R7-CPU1…R7-HDMI1** (§2.M) producing **v0.8**, **R8-CPU1…R8-EDIT1** (§2.N) producing **v0.9**, and **R9-CPU1…R9-TOOL1** (§2.O) producing **v0.10**. What remains open is open *by design*: **G28** (marketing page/render refresh — now also covering the LQFP-176 change) and **G30** (fab economics), plus the validation items in spec §16.2, which cannot be closed on paper at all. **The next stage is emulator planning**, for which §5.1, §6.5–6.8, §8 and §9 are the normative inputs.
